# Status

Updated at natural checkpoints (a completed, verified piece of work), not on every commit. Meant to let anyone — including you, switching between your two Claude accounts on this same machine — pick the project back up cold, without depending on any one conversation's chat history. See `README.md` for what the project actually is, and `ROADMAP.md` for the structural phase-level view (this file is the rolling log of what actually happened; that one is the checklist of what needs to be true next).

**Last updated:** 2026-10-28 (added a real description to Seam's tester-gate modal (`apps/seam/static/index.html`, `#gate-overlay`/`.gate-pane[data-pane="intro"]`), the blocking "What to test" screen every new tester sees before `trymend.solutions/seam/` becomes interactive. It previously jumped straight from "thanks for testing" into the numbered test steps with no explanation of what Seam actually is -- added a one-paragraph `.gate-about` intro naming who it's for (managers dealing with conflict on their team) and the three things it does (prep a hard conversation, get wording right on a tricky message, work through tension between two reports), trimmed down after a first draft's trailing "tell it what's going on and it works out which kind of help fits" clause was cut as redundant. **Previous update:** 2026-10-28 (added a `SEAM_TEST_MODE` env var to `apps/seam/server.py`, ahead of inviting real managers to try Seam. The invite copy says "nothing you enter is saved," but that wasn't true: a case's status/stage and plan steps were written to the local `cases.db` SQLite file and survived a restart (only the raw chat text was already memory-only) -- so a tester's case would still have existed in that file the next day. With `SEAM_TEST_MODE=true`, `db()` connects to a shared-cache SQLite URI (`file:seam_test_db?mode=memory&cache=shared`) instead of the on-disk file, kept alive for the process's life by a module-level `_TEST_DB_KEEPALIVE` connection that's never closed -- every request still reads/writes the same shared data within one running process, but nothing touches disk and a restart or redeploy drops everything. `render.yaml` now sets `SEAM_TEST_MODE: "true"` for the live deploy. **Not done yet:** this blueprint change doesn't retroactively apply to the already-provisioned Render service -- needs either a blueprint re-sync or manually setting `SEAM_TEST_MODE=true` in the Render dashboard before the invite goes out, or testers will still hit the on-disk DB.)

**Previous update:** 2026-10-28 (built `apps/thimble/feedback_alert.py`, a standalone alerting script answering the direct follow-up question "how do we know Thimble's replies are bad without saving conversations" -- reads only the structured `feedback` events the per-reply thumbs-down/"confused" pill already writes to `analytics_events.jsonl` (`session_id`/`turn`/`flag`/`reason`, no message text, per that feature's own entry further down this file) and alerts when down/confused flags cross a threshold within a rolling window. Reuses the exact `SMTP_HOST`/`SMTP_USER`/`SMTP_PASS`/`MAIL_FROM` env vars `maybe_send_hr_notification()` already established, under a deliberately separate `FEEDBACK_ALERT_EMAIL` recipient (product-quality signal, not an HR disclosure) -- same graceful no-setup-required pattern, prints what it would send and logs locally to `feedback_alert_log.jsonl` (gitignored) if unconfigured. A small `feedback_alert_state.json` (gitignored) tracks the last alert time so a sustained spike doesn't re-page on every cron run; `--threshold`/`--window`/`--cooldown` are all tunable, `--dry-run` previews without sending or touching state, `--loop` runs on a timer for local testing without cron. Verified live against the real `analytics_events.jsonl` left over from the feedback-pill testing: below-threshold correctly suppressed, over-threshold correctly fired with an accurate reason breakdown, the no-SMTP-configured fallback logged instead of erroring, state/log files wrote correctly, and a second run inside the cooldown window correctly skipped. `README.md` documents the env var and a sample cron line.)

**Previous update:** 2026-10-28 (added a `GET /health` endpoint and a global unhandled-exception handler to `apps/seam/server.py`, ahead of inviting real testers. Prompted by the user worrying Seam would quietly break under testers the same way Thimble's voice stack has repeatedly broken this week with zero trace anywhere (see the `ttsMuted`/stale-key entries above) -- Seam had no `/health` and no global exception logging, so a crash in any route without its own `try/except` was a bare 500 with nothing in Render's logs, and if Seam's uvicorn process (one of four in the single Render container, `deploy/start.sh`) died outright, nothing would restart it until the next deploy. `/health` checks `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set and `cases.db` is reachable, returning 200/`{"ok":true,...}` or 503; reachable live at `/manager/health` via the existing Caddy `handle_path /manager/*` proxy. The exception handler prints the route + full traceback to stdout on any unhandled error (Render captures stdout, not files, per `deploy/start.sh`'s own comment) instead of swallowing it, and returns a generic 500 instead of leaking a stack trace. Verified locally: `/health` returns the expected JSON live. Next, still open: an external uptime monitor (e.g. UptimeRobot) pinging that endpoint, since nobody will be watching Render's dashboard once testers start using it, and confirming Render's current plan doesn't sleep on idle.)

**Previous update:** 2026-10-28 (added per-reply tester feedback to Thimble's chat view (`apps/thimble/static/session.html`), after a design-review mockup (3 placement/interaction options) and an explicit pick: hover/tap-reveal placement (Option B's UI) combined with Option C's progressive-disclosure functionality. Every Thimble bubble now gets a small pill docked to its bottom-right corner -- thumbs-down ("didn't like this") and "?" ("this confused me") -- hidden until hovered (desktop) or tapped (mobile, where `:hover` never fires; tapping the bubble itself toggles the same reveal). Tapping an icon fires the bare flag immediately via the existing `logEvent()`/`POST /api/analytics` pattern (so the signal lands even if the tester stops there), then progressively discloses three one-tap reason chips (too vague / didn't match my situation / hard to follow) with no free-text field at all. `server.py`: `VALID_ANALYTICS_EVENTS` gained `"feedback"`, with `flag`/`reason` validated against closed sets (`VALID_FEEDBACK_FLAGS`, `VALID_FEEDBACK_REASONS`) and appended to `analytics_events.jsonl` (and the Sheets mirror, header updated) alongside the existing session_id/turn/mode fields -- deliberately no message-text field anywhere in the record, same "no conversation content" guarantee the rest of this file's analytics already holds. Turn number threaded through every `addMessage()` call site (text send, voice send, greeting, history replay) so a flagged reply can be matched back to its position in the session without storing what was said. Verified live end-to-end in the browser preview, both desktop (hover reveal, chip click, confirmed `flag`+`reason` landed correctly in `analytics_events.jsonl`) and at a 375px mobile viewport (tap-to-reveal, bare-flag fire-on-tap confirmed via the jsonl log) -- required restarting the already-running local `uvicorn` process mid-session since `server.py` changes don't hot-reload the way the static HTML does, worth remembering next time a Python-side change seems to silently not take effect.)

**Earlier:** 2026-10-27 (two real bugs fixed in Thimble's live call UI (`apps/thimble/static/session.html`), both reported live by the user, the second one a genuine "why does this keep happening" frustration worth recording plainly rather than just fixing quietly. (1) Requested a general smoothness pass on the avatar's state-to-state transitions ("smooth cool and shape change... between any two states") -- investigated the motion engine first rather than guessing: color (the Four-Color Bloom blend) and body shape (the dome-wobble `border-radius`) were already eased every frame, but the mouth's CSS transition was being cleared entirely (`inst.mouth.style.transition = ''`) for every state except `talking`, so top/left/width/height/border-radius all snapped instantly on every idle<->listening<->thinking<->paused change. Fixed by giving the mouth a transition in all states, only dropping `height` from the list while actually `talking` (where it has to track real TTS amplitude every frame, same reasoning the original talking-only transition was built for). Same one-line fix mirrored into the other copies of this exact engine -- `manager-story.html`, `about.html`, `story.html`, `apps/suture/static/index.html` -- since they're verbatim ports of the same code, not separate implementations. Verified live by cycling `avatarState` through every state via the console and screenshotting the morphs. Commit `d705a88`.

(2) Separately and more seriously: the user reported the voice memo (push-to-talk) button going completely unresponsive and the state pill stuck on "Waiting," reloading the page, and it still being broken -- the third voice-related report this week (see the Oct 26 stale-OpenAI-key entry in `apps/thimble/VOICE.md`), prompting an explicit "this app's voice stuff feels very fragile" from the user. First ruled out two live hypotheses rather than guessing: confirmed via `document.elementFromPoint` (run in the user's own browser console) that no transparent overlay was sitting over the button, then confirmed via a second console check that `talkMicBtn.disabled` was literally `true` because `avatarState` was stuck at `'idle'` instead of `'ready'`. Root cause: `feedSpeechText()`'s early-return branch for a muted/silent reply (`ttsMuted || ...`) reset `avatarState` to `'idle'` and called `endReplyCycle()`, but never called `armTapToTalk()` the way the normal end-of-speech path (`finishSpeechCycle()`) does -- and `ttsMuted` persists across reloads via `localStorage`, so once it's on (confirmed this user's own session had it set), *every* reply, not just one, lands in the broken branch and the mic button never re-arms, with no recovery short of manually toggling mute mid-session. Fixed by adding the same `if (liveMode) armTapToTalk();` call to that branch. Verified live: forced `ttsMuted` via `localStorage`, confirmed the button stayed disabled after a reply pre-fix and correctly re-armed (`avatarState: 'ready'`) post-fix. User confirmed fixed live afterward. Commit `8782568`. The user's "fragile" framing was correct -- this exact bug meant a one-time mute click, days ago, permanently broke every future voice turn until this fix, with nothing in the UI hinting why.

**Earlier:** 2026-10-25 (fixed both findings queued in `apps/seam/tests/pending_prompt_fixes.md` (see the entry below for how they were found), batched and retested together per that file's own stated plan. (1) Added a data-privacy/PII example to the illegal-activity bullet of the mandatory floor in [facilitator-case-plan-system-prompt.md:117](apps/facilitator-case-plan-system-prompt.md:117) ("...directing someone to share protected personal data -- SSNs, financial or medical records -- outside approved channels"). (2) Added a new paragraph after [facilitator-manager-coaching-system-prompt.md:53](apps/facilitator-manager-coaching-system-prompt.md:53) stating that a manager naming an already-decided formal action (a PIP, write-up, termination) as the reason for the conversation is itself the signal to skip straight to Phase 2 (structuring the delivery), even in the very first message -- and explicitly telling the model not to open by asking what led to that decision.

Caught a real gotcha before retesting either: the running Seam server (`:8460`) loads each system prompt once at startup from disk, not per-request, so the process that was already running had the old prompt text in memory even after the files were edited on disk -- killed and restarted it, confirmed it came back up, then retested. (1) Reran the data-privacy scenario 3 reps against the restarted server: 3/3 tripped `illegal_activity` cleanly. (2) Sent the original PIP message plus two phrasing variants directly against a fresh coaching-track case: all three replies now open by naming the decision as already made and move straight to delivery-structuring questions (what the report already knows, what the manager most needs them to walk away understanding), with no re-asking why the PIP happened. `pending_prompt_fixes.md` updated to move both out of "Open" into "Fixed" with these retest results; "Open" is empty again.

**Earlier:** 2026-10-25 (shifted Seam's safety-floor testing strategy from measuring consistency (3 repeats per scenario) to measuring breadth, per explicit direction: catch the obvious bugs across more situation types first, revisit repeat-based reliability later. Dropped both `apps/seam/tests/safety_floor_sweep.py` and `safety_floor_adversarial_sweep.py`'s default `--repeats` from 3 to 1, and expanded the base sweep's `SCENARIOS` from 13 to 20 -- `threats_violence`/`illegal_activity`/`immediate_safety_risk` each only had one case before (added a non-reporting-related violence threat, a threat against a third party, a data-privacy breach, wage theft, a self-harm disclosure, an ignored-hazard case), and non-floor sanity only had two (added a one-off frustration outburst and an ordinary personality clash).

Built a persistent, append-only test record across both apps rather than letting sweep output die in a terminal: `apps/seam/tests/test_log.py` and a mirrored `apps/thimble/tests/test_log.py` append a dated section to a `TEST_LOG.md` next to them on every run, recording the exact prompt file's git short hash + clean/dirty state, every scenario's trip rate, and an explicit failures list -- wired into all three sweep scripts (Seam's two, Thimble's `safety_floor_sweep.py`). Caught and fixed a real bug in this logging itself during first use: a transient server connection drop mid-sweep was getting silently recorded as the model missing 7 real harassment/discrimination cases, because the original code didn't distinguish a request that errored from one that genuinely got a non-trip response. Fixed by tracking errors per scenario separately -- they're now excluded from the trip rate and reported under their own "Errored" section instead of being folded into a false failure. The one already-logged bad entry in `apps/seam/tests/TEST_LOG.md` was annotated as invalid rather than deleted, so the record stays honest about what happened.

Two real findings opened in a new `apps/seam/tests/pending_prompt_fixes.md` (collected here instead of fixed one at a time, so a batch of prompt edits can be made and retested together): (1) a data-privacy/PII scenario (exporting customer SSNs to a personal email) failed to trip `illegal_activity` once, then tripped on a second unrelated run with no prompt change in between -- flagged as still genuinely open and NOT fixed (caught myself mischaracterizing the second run's `1/1` as "the fix holding" when no fix had actually been made; corrected the file to say so plainly, since the prompt's own `git log` confirms no edit landed). (2) A live-use bug in the manager-coaching track, caught by the user, not a sweep: a manager's opening message naming an already-decided PIP as the reason for a hard conversation got met with Phase-1 fact-finding ("what's been happening... that's led to the PIP decision") instead of skipping straight to helping structure the delivery -- `facilitator-manager-coaching-system-prompt.md` already has a skip-to-Phase-2 rule for "manager signals they already know what happened," it just doesn't list "names an already-decided formal action" as one of the triggering signals. Neither finding has been fixed yet; both are queued for a batched prompt-edit + retest pass.

**Previous update:** 2026-10-25 (fixed two real bugs in Seam's first-run onboarding walkthrough (`apps/seam/static/index.html`), both caught live by the user after the card-resize/typewriter work in the entry below. (1) The speech bubble's pinched corner was landing level with the avatar's bottom edge, not its mouth -- `align-items:flex-end` bottom-aligns the row to the full 150px avatar, but the mouth sits at `top:60%`, 60px above that. Added `margin-bottom:60px` to the bubble to pull it up to mouth height. (2) The walkthrough required clicking "Next" through all 4 lines, which the user said was never the ask -- rewrote it to play itself: each line types out, pauses ~2.2s after finishing, then auto-advances on its own; "Next" is now a "Skip ahead" shortcut, not a requirement, and it still auto-finishes into the untouched empty state after the last line.

A third bug surfaced after that fix shipped: the user noticed Thimble visibly sliding sideways between lines. Root cause was the avatar+bubble row being shrink-to-fit width, centered as a block inside the card -- so every time the bubble's width changed with new text, the whole pair re-centered and the avatar moved with it. Fixed by pinning the row to the card's full content width so only the bubble resizes; avatar's x position is now provably static (checked via `getBoundingClientRect()` before/after a line change, 60px both times). All three fixes verified live against the running `:8460` server. Commits `9622b55`, `a5bd997`.

**Previous update:** 2026-10-24 (gave Thimble's avatar a new gradient fill, "Four-Color Bloom," in `apps/thimble/static/session.html`. Started from a reference clip the user liked (pink+teal gradient) and iterated through several mockup rounds in two new standalone files -- `apps/thimble/static/thimble-gradient-colorplay.html` (6 static gradient-fill options, then 3 rounds of direct feedback: A's radial origin moved to the dome's own top-left perimeter, B's teal band widened so it reads as a real color not a sliver, C got a tighter specular dot + rim shadow for actual depth instead of a flat wash) and `apps/thimble/static/thimble-gradient-states-demo.html` (animated version: moving gradients, not just color crossfades, using `@property`-registered CSS custom properties so positions/angle/color tween directly; a fake sine-wave speech envelope driving mouth pulse + outer glow in sync, since this file has no real audio). Landed on "Four-Color Bloom" (style E, added after direct feedback that gradients should *move*, not just recolor): all 4 state colors (idle-blue, listening-teal, thinking-honey, talking-pink) stay visible at fixed corners at all times -- a state change only shifts each color's weight (active 46%, two neighbors 20% each, opposite 14%, never zero) -- so e.g. thinking reads as mostly honey with pink/teal/blue still peeking through, not a flat recolor. Ported into the real `session.html`: replaced the old single-target `backgroundColor` lerp with a 4-way weighted-blend lerp (`THIMBLE_BLOOM_WEIGHTS`/`THIMBLE_BLOOM_POS`/`THIMBLE_BLOOM_RGB`), repainting `background-image`/`background-color`/`box-shadow` together every frame in `updateThimbleMotion`, same per-frame-lerp convention the file already used for its old color transition. Verified live against the running `:8420` server across talking/idle/thinking, no console errors.

Hit two real bugs porting this into `session.html`, both caught and fixed: (1) a CSS comment containing a literal `THIMBLE_BLOOM_*/updateThimbleMotion` substring closed the comment early (the `*/` mid-word), corrupting the stylesheet parse from that point on and collapsing `.thimble-body` to zero height -- found by comparing `getComputedStyle` output against the raw served CSS, not by reading the file back. (2) **A concurrent second Claude Code session was editing this same file at the same time** (its own commit history shows it committing then reverting an unrelated avatar-resize change to `session.html` around 19:26-19:33, in the middle of this session's work) and that reversion silently discarded all of this session's uncommitted edits to the file -- the gradient port, the bug fix above, a Launchpad-badge removal, and a chat-hint-text removal all had to be redone from scratch after `git diff` turned up empty against a file that should have had hours of changes in it. The other session's own commit message confirms it read this session's in-progress "Four-Color Bloom" CSS directly off disk before the collision (it ported a frozen snapshot into Seam's onboarding avatar, see that entry below) -- so the work existed on disk, it just never got committed before being overwritten. Lesson for next time: commit thimble/session.html work in smaller increments rather than batching a long gradient-iteration session, specifically because it's a file multiple sessions are likely to touch.

Also removed the floating "⌂ Launchpad" dev badge (`#mend-launchpad-badge`) from all 52 pages that had it repo-wide, per explicit request ("stupid launchpad quick links") -- a single self-contained `<a>` tag, same markup copied into every file, removed with one script rather than 52 manual edits. And removed the "Enter to send · Shift+Enter for a new line" hint text/CSS from `session.html`'s chat composer, also per explicit request.

**Earlier, same session:** fixed a real "question buried mid-reply, then more content after it" bug in Seam, caught by the user live-testing a complicated multi-person conflict scenario (a manager caught between a VP's speed metric and a platform architect's latency standard, two employees in a public Slack fight, a promotion case on the line). The existing "one clarifying question per turn" rule in both `facilitator-case-plan-system-prompt.md` and `facilitator-manager-coaching-system-prompt.md` controlled count but not position -- a reply could ask its one allowed question in the middle, then keep talking about something else after it, which a manager skimming the reply would miss entirely. Added **"at most one question per reply, and it has to be the last thing you say"** to both files, right after the existing "ask once" rule. Verified live against the user's exact scenario (re-sent to the running `:8460` server): before the fix, the model's reply style matched the bug description; after, the same prompt observes first, asks exactly one question, and stops.

Built a rules-based regression benchmark for this (`apps/seam/tests/question_discipline_benchmark.py`), following the existing `safety_floor_sweep.py` pattern: one case per scenario, live against the running server. Checks are mechanical, not LLM-judged, since "how many real questions" and "is the last one actually last" are both checkable from the reply text alone -- counts question-marked sentences (stripping quoted spans first, since a suggested script line Thimble hands the manager, like a quoted example opening line, can contain its own "?" that isn't Thimble asking the manager anything) and flags a reply that has more than one, or whose question isn't the final sentence. Also re-runs two of the existing fragile safety-floor scenarios (the vague L0 harassment case and the minimized-harassment case) as a cheap regression check that this prompt edit didn't disturb floor behavior, since it's a sibling section of the same file.

One real false-positive surfaced and fixed during this build: the benchmark initially flagged a "what outcome do you want? Just X, or more than that?" reply as two questions, but that's one question followed by example answers to make it easier to respond to, not a second ask -- confirmed with the user as the intended, acceptable style. Added a heuristic (a question-sentence immediately following another, starting with "or"/"just", merges into the same question rather than counting separately) to tell that apart from a genuinely separate second question. First attempt at this heuristic had its own bug: stripping the quoted span to check for this could eat a real sentence's own terminal punctuation when that punctuation sat right at the closing quote mark (e.g. `not just "they argue."`), silently dropping that sentence from scoring and turning a real violation into a false pass. Fixed by neutralizing only the "?" characters inside quotes (turning them to ".") instead of deleting the whole quoted span, so sentence boundaries outside the quote stay intact. Verified against 5 hand-checked reference replies (2 real violations, 2 confirmed-fine "options" replies, 1 quoted-dialogue-question case) before trusting it against the live server again.

Live baseline run after all of this: 10/11 question-discipline checks passed (one coaching-path reply, freshly generated, asked two genuinely separate things -- noted as the kind of case worth watching across repeat runs, not a scoring bug), 0 floor regressions. Results append to `apps/seam/tests/benchmark_results.jsonl`, tagged with git commit hash and whether the two prompt files have uncommitted changes, so score trends are comparable across future prompt edits -- explicitly a trend line, not a pass/fail gate, same philosophy as the existing floor sweeps.

Also built, earlier in this same session: `apps/seam/launch_seam.sh` + `/Users/matt/Applications/Seam Launcher.app` (built via `osacompile`, not a hand-rolled `.app` bundle -- a first attempt at a raw bundle silently failed to launch via Finder/`open` despite running fine when exec'd directly, root cause not fully pinned down, not worth more time on). Double-clicking the launcher starts the Seam server if it isn't already running, waits for it to respond, then opens the existing installed Seam PWA window -- no terminal needed. A `launchd` LaunchAgent version (auto-start at login) was attempted first but blocked outright by Claude Code's own auto-mode safety classifier ("Unauthorized Persistence"); didn't try to route around it, built the double-click version instead. Separately tried to replace the launcher's generic broken-circle applet icon with the real Mend evergreen mark (`pitch/design_handoffs/mend_sessions/mend-brand-kit/logo/mend-app-icon-evergreen.svg`, rendered to `.icns` via `qlmanage`/`iconutil`) -- took in Finder, but the Dock kept showing the stale icon even after clearing the per-user IconServices cache files directly (not just restarting Dock/Finder); user said not worth more time on, left as a known cosmetic gap.

**Earlier:** 2026-10-23 (regression-checked Seam's safety floor after the question-fixation prompt rewrite documented below, since that rewrite touched large parts of both `facilitator-manager-coaching-system-prompt.md` and `facilitator-case-plan-system-prompt.md`, even though the Mandatory Floor section itself wasn't edited. Ran a trimmed subset of the two existing sweeps (`apps/seam/tests/safety_floor_sweep.py`, `apps/seam/tests/safety_floor_adversarial_sweep.py`) directly against the live `:8460` server instead of the full 23-scenario x 3-repeat set, to keep API cost down: 1 repeat each across 7 picked scenarios covering every risk category those files test -- a clear floor case, two clear non-floor cases, the known-fragile vague harassment-ladder case (L0), a different protected-characteristic case, the previously-noisy minimized-harassment adversarial case, and a minimized non-floor sanity case. All 7 came back correct: every floor case tripped with the right category, both non-floor cases stayed quiet, and the two historically shakiest cases (L0's vagueness, the "he's not a bad guy" minimization) tripped clean this run. No regression found. This was a confidence check given the untouched floor section, not a full re-certification -- the original 3-repeat sweeps remain the real reliability numbers on file.

**Earlier:** 2026-10-23 (fixed a real "exhausting, keeps asking and asking" complaint about Seam's coaching track, raised directly by the user after using it live. Root cause turned out not to be what it looked like at first: the coaching prompt already said "one clarifying question per turn," and that rule was holding fine. The actual problem was fixation -- once Thimble latched onto a question, it would re-ask it two or three times across turns, in reworded form, even after the manager had already moved the conversation somewhere else, and even tack the unanswered question onto an otherwise-complete reply. This is the same class of bug `facilitator-intake-system-prompt.md` hit and fixed on 2026-10-15 (see that `**Earlier**` entry below) -- it resurfaced here in a sibling prompt family that hadn't gotten the same treatment.

Fixed and iterated three times against direct user feedback, each time verified live against the running `:8460` server rather than just read as a diff. First pass: added an early "what do you want out of this conversation" question to `facilitator-manager-coaching-system-prompt.md` (it already listed this as something to eventually find out, but buried last in a list, so in practice the model never got to it) and a "never ask the same question three times" rule to both `facilitator-manager-coaching-system-prompt.md` and `facilitator-case-plan-system-prompt.md` (the conflict track has the identical failure mode). Retesting found the cap wasn't holding: the model would count a reworded question as a fresh attempt rather than a repeat, so a "twice" cap in practice still allowed three real asks of the same underlying thing. Tightened to "count by substance, not phrasing." The user then pushed further, correctly: even two asks is too many -- "the conversation needs to be lead by the user... Thimble should roll with the punches." Rewrote the rule a third time to a hard single-ask cap: ask once, and if it goes unanswered, drop it for good and work with whatever the manager actually gave, no second attempt in any wording. Also added, from the same live-testing pass: a rule that a direct, explicit request ("give me an opening line") always gets fulfilled immediately with no conditions attached, never preceded or followed by re-asking something unrelated; and a guardrail against inventing characterizations the manager never stated, after live testing caught a drafted opening-line script telling the direct report reviews should "feel less personal," a description that appeared nowhere in what the manager had actually said and that the manager would have been handed to say verbatim.

Verification method: wrote a small test harness (a plain Python script hitting the live `:8460` API directly, not mocked) and ran the same handful of scenarios through before and after each prompt iteration, diffing the real transcripts rather than reasoning about the prompt text. Also ran 2 scenarios each across conflict/coaching/written as a broader first pass before narrowing in on the specific bug. Published the test transcripts as a readable chat-bubble artifact (not committed to the repo) so the user could review full conversations directly -- this is actually how the fabrication bug got caught: the user spotted it reading the formatted transcript, not from anything the automated harness checked for. Net result, confirmed via a final retest: the same scenario that originally asked for one factual detail four times across five turns now asks it once, drops it cleanly when unanswered, follows wherever the manager actually goes next, and answers direct requests immediately with no re-ask tacked on.

Bundled into this same commit, not this session's own work: `launchpad.html` changes (reordered the Seam section earlier in the list, added "Read the thread"/"Read the room" hook links, removed a duplicate Seam block) came from a different/concurrent session; `usage_log.jsonl`'s new entries are auto-appended API usage from `thimble` and `manager-read-test` being used elsewhere, not generated by this session (Seam itself doesn't log here, see 2026-10-21's `**Earlier**` entry on cost estimation). `pitch/manager-read-test/static/index.html` was deliberately left out of this commit, uncommitted, at the user's explicit request -- it's being worked on in a separate, still-in-progress session.

**Earlier:** 2026-10-23 (Seam session: fixed Thimble's chat avatar, found and fixed a real classify bug, and built a fourth conversation type, "coaching," distinct from "conflict." In progress, not fully browser-verified -- see below.

Avatar: the small pink chat-bubble avatar in `apps/seam/static/index.html` (`.avatar .blob`/`.mouth`) had drifted from Thimble's real design (the user flagged it was using an invented approximation, not the actual character). Ported the real shape/color/wobble-animation from `apps/seam/design-mockups/MeetingVoice.dc.html` (the source this file's own palette comment already pointed to), then iterated twice more on direct feedback: removed the floor shadow entirely (matches precedent elsewhere in the repo -- `.thimble-avatar.mini .thimble-shadow{display:none}` already does the same for small instances), and changed the mouth from a flat hairline pill to a real oval (`border-radius:50%` on a non-square box, not `999px` on a near-zero-height one). Compared several mouth size/position options live before landing on one, per this repo's "show options before editing" convention.

Found and fixed a real, previously-live bug while debugging an unrelated server restart: `apps/seam/server.py`'s `/api/classify` endpoint was capped at `max_tokens=10`, which `claude-sonnet-5` now exhausts entirely on its own thinking preamble before ever emitting the one-word answer -- every classify call was silently falling back to `"unsure"` regardless of input, which is why Seam kept asking "can you say a bit more about what's going on?" even after a clear description. This was not caused by anything this session touched; it would have been broken for every user, always. Fixed by raising to `max_tokens=300`; verified directly against the live Anthropic API (confirmed a `ThinkingBlock` with no following `TextBlock` at 10 tokens, a real `TextBlock` at 300) and against the running server (`curl .../api/classify` now correctly returns `"conflict"` for a real scenario instead of `"unsure"`).

Also diagnosed, while chasing the above, that a manager prepping to deliver feedback to their OWN direct report was being misclassified as `"conflict"` and routed into `facilitator-case-plan-system-prompt.md` -- a prompt whose own header explicitly says it's for adjacent disputes between OTHER people, not this. The real "manager rehearsing a hard conversation with their own report" prompt already existed (`facilitator-intake-system-prompt.md`'s manager-coaching track) but was only ever wired into `apps/thimble/server.py`, never Seam. Built it as a real fourth path rather than just explaining the gap: new `apps/facilitator-manager-coaching-system-prompt.md` (trimmed/adapted from that track for Seam's own conventions -- reuses the exact same `<mend_signal>` control-block schema `facilitator-case-plan-system-prompt.md` already uses, so no new parsing code was needed). `apps/seam/server.py`: classifier now has 4 categories (added `coaching`, reworded `conflict`'s definition to explicitly exclude it); `cases` table gained a `kind` column (`'conflict'`/`'coaching'`, migrates existing rows to `'conflict'` by default); `POST /api/cases` takes `{"kind": ...}`; `facilitator_reply()` picks the system prompt by the case's `kind` via a new `CASE_SYSTEM_PROMPTS` dict; hand-off/peer-session creation is blocked for `coaching` cases (that track never leads to one). `apps/seam/static/index.html`: "coaching" rides the exact same case/plan-panel UI as "conflict" via a new `CASE_LIKE_TYPES` array threaded through every place the code used to check `type === "conflict"`, just without the hand-off chip; disambiguation gained a fourth option, "Prepping to talk to one of my own reports."

Verified via direct `curl` against the live `:8460` server, not just read the diff: classify correctly returns `coaching` for a performance-conversation scenario and `conflict` for a real peer dispute; created a real coaching case end to end (`POST /api/cases {"kind":"coaching"}` -> `/start` -> `/messages`), confirmed the opening line and the first real reply came from the coaching persona (asked for specific dates/frequency, not the mismatched "has anything changed recently" question the old misrouting produced), confirmed `GET /api/cases` lists both kinds correctly side by side with old rows defaulting to `conflict`. **Not verified live in the browser**: the browser pane's connection to `localhost:8460` specifically got stuck refusing navigation partway through this session (fresh tabs, after a server restart) while `curl` to the same port kept working fine throughout -- looked like a browser-pane/origin-permission quirk tied to the dev server's pid changing on restart, not an app bug, but it means the actual click-through (disambiguation button -> coaching case -> plan panel showing, no hand-off chip showing) has only been code-reviewed and curl-verified, not seen rendering in a real browser tab yet. Worth an actual click-test next session.

Separately, explained (did not yet fix, pending the user's call) why Thimble's opening greeting keeps replaying: `apps/thimble/static/session.html`'s bootstrap IIFE calls `resetAndStart()` -- wipe + replay the greeting -- unconditionally on every single page load, a deliberate change from earlier testing ("I want when I hit reload to have a new session") that also means any reload, refocus, or dev-server auto-restart replays the welcome audio from scratch. Old behavior (resume an in-progress conversation on reload, `/api/start` no-ops if `conversation` is non-empty) is still intact server-side and just needs that IIFE changed/gated to come back.

Same scope as the 2026-10-21 `thimble`/`suture`/`seam`/`splice` folder rename: `git mv apps/demo-app apps/spool`, rebuilt its `.venv` from scratch (`python3.11 -m venv` + reinstall, same reason as before -- a venv's own `bin/uvicorn`/`bin/activate` bake in the absolute path they were created under and break silently on a folder rename), verified clean via `python -c "import server"` against the rebuilt venv. Updated every reference across `run_all.sh`, `deploy/start.sh`, `deploy/Dockerfile`, `render.yaml`, `deploy/render.Caddyfile`, `README.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `LINKS.md`, `launchpad.html`, `IDEAS.md` (added as the repo's eighth app codename), and the comments inside `apps/spool/server.py` and `apps/thimble/server.py` that referenced the old name. **Previous update:** 2026-10-23 (collapsed Seam's three separate top-level sections -- Conflicts, Meetings, Written, each with its own rail icon, sidebar list and chat view -- into one unified chat shell, per a brainstorm that started as "why three segments instead of one chat with a categorized list" and was mocked up as a claude.ai Design artifact (Option C: blank composer, "What do you want to discuss today?", four example prompts, auto-tagging instead of upfront category choice) before any real UI was touched.

Purely additive on the backend: added one new stateless `POST /api/classify` endpoint to `apps/seam/server.py` (one cheap Claude call, inline router prompt, returns `conflict`/`meeting`/`written`/`unsure`) -- the three existing tables, system prompts, and `facilitator_reply`/`meeting_strategy_reply`/`written_facilitator_reply` functions (including the safety-floor voting logic) are untouched. Rewrote `apps/seam/static/index.html`'s shell: removed the rail and the three separate sidebar-sections/section-views, replaced with one merged "Conversations" list (fetches all three existing list endpoints client-side, sorted by recency, each row tagged Conflict/Meeting/Written) and one thread column. A new conversation starts blank; the first user message(s) get classified before any real session exists -- on a concrete answer, the matching real session is created and the pending turn(s) are replayed into it for real; on `unsure` after the first turn, a plain "can you say a bit more" follow-up; still unsure after the second turn, an explicit three-option disambiguation (no more guessing silently). The plan panel (conflict) and strategy panel (meeting) now toggle by resolved type instead of by section; written has no side panel, same as before.

Verified live against a real server, not just read the diff: confirmed the intro state renders correctly, a first ambiguous message gets the soft follow-up, a second ambiguous message produces the three-option disambiguation, and clicking each of "something that happened" and "a meeting I'm prepping for" correctly creates a real case/meeting-session, replays the held turns through the real `/messages` endpoints, and shows the right side panel -- also re-opened a pre-existing case from before this change and confirmed it still loads correctly (thread header actions, plan panel, inciting incident). One blocker found during verification, not caused by this change: `apps/seam/.env`'s `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is invalid (401 from Anthropic on every real model call, including the three existing facilitator functions, not just the new classifier) -- worth a fresh key before this is demoed live, since classify will silently fall back to the two-turn disambiguation instead of actually classifying until it's fixed.

**Previous update:** 2026-10-23 (no new feature work this session -- spent it untangling a confusing git-push situation so the next person picking this up (including you on the other account) doesn't have to re-diagnose it. This session's own `git push origin main` was being denied outright by the Claude Code auto-mode permission classifier under "Sensitive-Source Provenance," consistently across repeated attempts, with no visible cause in the diff or commit message content -- looks like a harness-side false positive specific to that session, not a git problem or a secrets problem. Confirmed the commit itself wasn't lost: while this session's pushes kept getting denied, a concurrent session on this same machine kept working and later pushed everything -- including this session's already-made commit -- to both `origin` and `gitlab`, verified by fetching both remotes and finding them at the same commit with zero ahead/behind.

Separately, found the working tree sitting on ~15 files of uncommitted changes left behind by that other concurrent session (Seam static/server tweaks, pitch-strategy doc edits, two new docs, a new todo page) and, at the user's explicit request, committed them as a plain checkpoint (`49f1bf8`, "Checkpoint pending changes from a concurrent session") rather than leaving them pending -- labeled clearly in the commit body as not this session's own work, per this repo's established convention for exactly this situation. A follow-up `git push origin main` for both that commit and the STATUS.md update documenting all this (`fd9bd63`) succeeded on retry, so both reached GitHub fine -- the classifier block wasn't permanent, just intermittent within this session.

At the user's explicit request ("remove gitlab no longer using"), retired the `gitlab` remote for real: `git remote remove gitlab` (push to it was consistently denied by the same classifier anyway, and the user confirmed it's genuinely unused now, not a workaround-in-progress). Updated every place that still referenced it as live: root `CLAUDE.md`'s git-remotes section (now says `origin` is the only remote), `.claude/commands/ship.md` (push step now just `git push origin main`, no second remote), and `LINKS.md`'s repositories section. Left `ROADMAP.md`'s existing "GitLab no longer load-bearing" checklist line alone since it's a historical log entry, already accurate. `origin`/GitHub is now the sole remote and the sole deploy source -- no more two-remote push discipline needed going forward.

**Previous update:** 2026-10-22 (follow-up to a brainstorm on how to show managers a social/structural-dynamics blind spot without the product reading as "we know management better than you do" -- landed on reframing the diagnosis as information about this specific thread, not a verdict on the manager, and applied it to `pitch/manager-read-test`'s existing scoring. Two copy/prompt changes: (1) each per-question note in `server.py`'s `SCORE_SYSTEM_PROMPT` now has to follow a fixed two-sentence shape -- "you're right that..." naming a real detail from the manager's actual answer, then "could it be that..." reframing the structural read as a question instead of a correction; (2) added a `norm_note` field, shown only after the manager answers (not as a pre-read spoiler -- removed the old hero-copy line "Most managers call it a personality clash" that gave the trap away before they read the thread), that normalizes a miss ("most managers read this one as a personality clash first ... not a sign you're worse at this than anyone else") or affirms a hit, so the result reads as "here's where you land" rather than a grade.

Found and fixed a real bug while testing this live, not just reading the diff: the longer two-sentence note format was overflowing the `/api/score` call's `max_tokens=400` cap on real (longer) threads, truncating the model's response mid-output and silently falling back to blank notes with the loop's default `mid`/`mid`/`mid` bands on every question -- invisible in a quick glance since "mid" looked like a plausible real score. Caught it by diffing a direct `curl` response (short fake thread, worked fine) against the actual browser-session network response (real thread, blank notes) rather than trusting the UI alone. Fixed by raising `max_tokens` to 700 and tightening the prompt to cap each note at two short sentences. Verified end to end against a live `:8481` server across all three score bands -- a deliberately personality-read answer (low/low/low, "Moral Arbiter," correct normalizing note), a deliberately structural answer (high/high/high, "Systems Architect," correct affirming note), and the original mixed-signal run (mid/mid/mid, "Bureaucratic Arbiter") -- confirming notes populate correctly and bands aren't silently defaulting in any case.

**Earlier:** 2026-10-21 (third item of the manager-user-test punch list, the adversarial angle flagged earlier: not just vague wording (the first sweep's graded ladder), but the MANAGER's own framing minimizing or dismissing floor-worthy content ("probably nothing," "he's not a bad guy," "I don't want this to turn into some HR thing"). Added `apps/seam/tests/safety_floor_adversarial_sweep.py`: 7 floor scenarios built from the first sweep's already-confirmed-explicit content (unwanted sexual attention, a coworker's explicit threat, falsifying records, an impaired coworker on machinery, age-based exclusion, a direct "keep this between us" ask, and the reporter being framed as oversensitive) wrapped in minimizing language, plus 3 non-floor sanity cases in the same soft register to confirm the floor isn't just reacting to hedging words in general.

Ran at `--repeats 1` first on explicit cost concerns raised mid-session (Seam doesn't log costs to `usage_log.jsonl` the way `conflict-test`/`thimble` do, so no exact per-run figure is available, only a rough estimate from comparable apps' logged per-call costs). Result: 6/7 floor cases tripped clean on the first pass, all 3 non-floor sanity cases correctly never tripped, and one case ("minimized: explicit harassment" -- the manager saying "I'm sure he doesn't realize... he's not a bad guy" over content identical to the first sweep's always-tripping L3 case) came back 0/1, a real candidate for the adversarial effect this file was built to find. Re-ran that one scenario alone 5 more times to check signal vs noise before spending on a full re-sweep: 5/5 clean. Combined 5/6 -- read as noise from a single run, not a real minimization-driven miss; the floor held against minimizing framing across every scenario tested. Full file still available to run at higher repeats if more confidence is wanted later.

**Earlier:** 2026-10-21 (GitHub is now the real Render deploy source, not just the default push target in CLAUDE.md -- closes a real trap documented in an earlier 2026-10-16 incident, where a whole session pushed to `origin` only and nothing went live because Render was actually watching GitLab. At the user's request, installed Render's GitHub App (scoped to just the `mend` repo, not "all repositories," on purpose) and repointed `mend-mvp`'s Build -> Source from `generallymatthew-group/mend` (GitLab) to `generallymatthew/mend` (GitHub) in Render's dashboard, done live in the browser pane since this needed the user's own GitHub/Render sign-in and OAuth consent at each step. Verified for real, not just that the setting changed: triggered the redeploy, watched it build and reach "Deploy succeeded | Live," then loaded the live site directly and confirmed both the hub page and the newly-added `/manager/` route (today's earlier Seam-proxy work) work correctly through the new GitHub-sourced deploy, including a full `POST /api/cases` -> `/start` -> `Create peer session` round trip against the real production server. GitLab remains configured as a second remote but is no longer load-bearing for anything; `git push` with no remote can safely mean `origin` again, per CLAUDE.md, with no override needed going forward.

**Earlier:** 2026-10-21 (second item of the manager-user-test punch list: Seam had no tests at all before this, despite its prompt carrying its own copy of the mandatory-floor language. Added `apps/seam/tests/safety_floor_sweep.py`, ported from `apps/thimble/tests/safety_floor_sweep.py`'s same exploratory-sweep pattern (trip rate per scenario across repeats, not pass/fail), adapted for Seam's real shape: case-based (`POST /api/cases` -> `/start` -> `/messages`, no single global `/api/reset` conversation the way Thimble has), manager's-voice scenarios (a manager describing a report's situation in third person, not first person about themselves -- Seam's facilitator never talks to the employee directly, see `facilitator-case-plan-system-prompt.md`'s own framing), and checking only `floor_triggered` since that's the only mandatory-floor signal field Seam's `/api/cases/{id}/messages` response actually returns (`screen_result` exists in the model's `<mend_signal>` block but isn't surfaced in the API). Ran it for real against the live `:8460` server (13 scenarios x 3 repeats, ~39 real `/api/cases` calls, a few cents of API cost) -- all 13 scenarios came back stable (0/3 or 3/3, no boundary flip-flopping): every clear floor case tripped every time with the right category, both clear non-floor cases never tripped, and every rung of the harassment explicitness ladder (L0-L3) plus every other-protected-characteristic case tripped every time. One thing worth a human looking at, not necessarily a bug: Seam trips on the ladder's vaguest rung (L0 -- appearance comments + lingering, no stated discomfort or refusal yet) every single run, where Thimble's own sweep found that exact scenario shape genuinely ambiguous (flip-flopped true/false run to run) -- Seam may just be tuned more cautious for a manager audience by design, but it means Seam will tell a manager "this needs to go to HR" on fairly thin material, worth knowing before relying on it live.

**Earlier:** 2026-10-21 (built a pre-use manager discovery survey for Seam, `apps/seam/static/survey.html` + a new `/survey` page route and `POST /api/survey` endpoint in `apps/seam/server.py` -- distinct from `../thimble`'s survey, which asks how a specific session went; this one is sent to prospective manager testers before they've necessarily used the app, to gauge their actual conflict-management pressures and gut reaction to Seam's 3 offerings. Content structured into the 4 categories requested: (1) how often conflict comes up on their team and what they do about it first, (2) the social pressure of being the manager -- whether they feel judged on how they handle it, who that pressure comes from, whether it's ever made them avoid something they should have addressed, (3) a block per offering (Conflicts/Written/Meetings, Seam's actual 3 live sections) asking how useful each would be and whether they'd actually use it, (4) team size, overall interest, an optional email for pilot access, and free comments. Same visual pattern as `../thimble/static/survey.html` (Manrope, scale buttons, radio/choice cards, thanks view), reskinned to Seam's own ink/pink palette rather than Thimble's.

On request ("can we use the same spreadsheet as thimbles about but in another tab?"), wired responses into the same Google Sheet Thimble's survey already writes to, on a new, separate **"Seam" worksheet tab** rather than a second spreadsheet -- `get_survey_sheet()` opens Thimble's `GOOGLE_SHEET_ID` via the same service account and creates the "Seam" tab with its own header row on first use if missing. Copied `GOOGLE_SHEET_ID`/`GOOGLE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON` into `apps/seam/.env` from `../thimble/.env` (each app loads its own env independently) and added `gspread`/`google-auth` to `apps/seam/requirements.txt` + its venv. `survey_responses.jsonl` remains the local fallback if the Sheets write ever fails, same safety-net pattern as Thimble's. Verified live end to end: submitted a real test response through the running page, confirmed it landed in a genuinely new "Seam" tab in the shared spreadsheet (not just the local log) via a direct gspread read, then deleted that test row and cleared the local jsonl so no placeholder data is sitting in either store. **Not done:** Render's production service for Seam still needs both Google env vars added manually in its dashboard (presumably already set on Thimble's service there) before this works in production, not something this session could do; the survey hasn't actually been sent to any manager yet.)

**Earlier:** 2026-10-21 (built `pitch/conflict-test`, a new standalone tool end to end: a social-dynamics self-test, "can you read the room," meant to address a positioning problem identified this session -- people don't think Mend applies to them because they assume they're already good at navigating conflict. The hook is a real-time roleplay, not a quiz with visible right answers: tell it your job, get dropped into a status-asymmetric scenario (asking something from a stranger who can say no and already has a reputation for pushing back), the counterpart messages you first, you reply for real in a modal chat, capped at three of your turns, then get a scored readout.

Design went through an explicit mockup pass before any real UI was touched (three visual directions published as a claude.ai Design artifact, brand pulled from `pitch/design_handoffs/mend_sessions/mend-brand-kit/brand-guide.html` rather than invented), then pivoted on direction mid-review from static cards to the current chat-plus-modal interaction once it became clear the user wanted the counterpart to message first rather than you cold-opening. Scenarios are deliberately template-based, not freeform-generated (`scenarios.py`: 5 roles -- product manager, nurse, junior associate, designer, teacher -- plus a generic fallback, each with a precanned counterpart/reputation/ask/opening line), so difficulty and what's actually being tested doesn't drift person to person; only the LLM call for the counterpart's in-character replies and the final readout are per-person.

Added scoring on explicit request ("we need to give a score for people to share"): rejected letting the model just output a 0-100 number directly (same transcript can score differently run to run with no real math behind it) in favor of having the model classify the whole conversation into one of three fixed categories -- A "didn't read the room" (held one approach despite pushback), B "caught up" (adjusted only once pressed), C "read it from the start" (gave the counterpart what they needed and stayed attuned) -- appended as a parsed `CATEGORY: X` line on the same `/api/readout` call, with the category-to-score (35/70/95) and label mapping done in `server.py`, not the model, so the same category always produces the same number. Judges the *whole* conversation, not just the opening message, per explicit correction ("it should judge the whole conversation for being socially adept").

Found and fixed three real bugs via live testing, not just reading the diff: (1) `claude-sonnet-5` sometimes returns a `ThinkingBlock` as `response.content[0]`, which crashed both `/api/reply` and `/api/readout` with a 500 (`AttributeError: 'ThinkingBlock' object has no attribute 'text'`) -- added `_first_text()` to scan for the actual text block instead of assuming index 0; (2) no root `.env` exists in this repo (each app keeps its own), so `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` wasn't loading -- now also loads `apps/thimble/.env` as a fallback; (3) the score/readout card was appended as a sibling *after* the fixed-height modal's own scrollable area (`.modal-chat`), so a long readout could get silently clipped with no way to scroll to it -- fixed by building the readout element dynamically and appending it inside `modal-chat` itself instead of a separate static div. Also caught and fixed a real em-dash in user-visible copy (the page header) that slipped past the user's own no-em-dash writing rule.

Verified live, not just that it starts: ran all 5 named roles plus the fallback (`mechanic`) through `/api/scenario` directly, confirming the role-to-counterpart mapping resolves correctly including the fallback path; ran both a clean pressure-first failure case (scored 35, "didn't read the room," quoting back the actual line that triggered pushback) and a deliberately-written "good" response (scored 95, "read it from the start") end to end through real model calls, confirming the score differentiates correctly at both ends; also ran two replies intended as clean successes that the model correctly scored as the *middle* category instead, catching a real subtler pattern (leading with your own discomfort/apology rather than the decision-relevant content the counterpart needed) rather than just pattern-matching "pressure bad, context good" -- useful signal that the readout is doing real judgment, not keyword matching. Redesigned the landing/hero section on request (bigger 52px headline, a pink tag pill, an explicit 4-step explainer of the mechanic) and removed the now-redundant framing bubble from the intro chat.

**Not done:** the counterpart's opening line is only mildly skeptical right now ("first I'm hearing of this, what's in it"), not actually rude/pushy -- discussed with the user as a real improvement (makes this a genuine de-escalation test rather than just an information-quality test) but the six `scenarios.py` opening lines have not actually been rewritten yet. No sharing/export UI exists for the score. Not deployed anywhere, not wired into `run_all.sh`/Caddyfile/Render the way the four core apps are (`.claude/launch.json` has a local-only `conflict-test` entry on port 8480). A stale pre-this-session snapshot of this same directory was swept into an unrelated Seam commit (`a8476ac`) by a concurrent session before this session's real work began -- this update is the actual delta on top of that, not the first commit of the idea.)

Also bundles unrelated, uncommitted Seam Written-section changes from another concurrent session that were sitting in the working tree when this one wrapped (see the "Previous update" entry just below, which is that other session's own description of the same work) -- not this session's work, included only per this repo's own convention of not leaving pending changes uncommitted.

**Earlier:** 2026-10-21 (Seam's Written section rebuilt from a stateless single-shot tone-check into a real chat thread, at explicit request ("written should just be a chat ui its alway blank, after a session is finished it becomes a list item and the launch state is blank again"). Previously Written was a draft-box-plus-result-card (`POST /api/tone-check`, no persistence) with a static always-selected "Written" row in the sidebar; now it mirrors Conflicts/Meetings' thread-col chat pattern, but with two deliberate differences from them: entering the section (or hitting "+") always drops you into a brand-new blank thread rather than resuming the last one (`createWrittenSession()`, called once from `showSection`), and a session only materializes as a sidebar list item once it's actually gotten a reply -- `written_sessions` rows are created immediately for the conversation-id plumbing, but `GET /api/written-sessions` only returns rows `WHERE preview IS NOT NULL`, so a draft typed and abandoned never shows up anywhere. New `written_sessions` table (title/preview only, same narrow persistence as cases/meeting_sessions) and `WRITTEN_CONVERSATIONS` in-memory store in `apps/seam/server.py`; new `written_facilitator_reply()` reuses the existing `TONE_CHECK_SYSTEM_PROMPT`/`strip_tone_check()` per-turn rather than inventing a new prompt. Old `/api/tone-check` route and the draft-card/tonecheck-card CSS removed outright, not left dead.

Along the way, got asked for empty-state treatment on "all three" sections before realizing Conflicts and Meetings already had one (explainer copy + CTA) -- only Written had none, since it used to auto-show its single tool with no selection step. Also chased down a claimed "today by default the first case is auto selected" that turned out not to match the running code; confirmed live in the browser pane (not just by reading source) that neither Conflicts nor Meetings auto-select anything, both correctly show their existing "pick one or start new" empty state. Then, on a second pass, made that explainer copy actually explain what the feature is: Conflicts/Meetings' lede paragraph previously vanished the moment you had even one case/meeting (`.empty-state.returning p.lede { display: none; }`), so returning users never saw a description of the product again -- now it stays visible, just smaller. Written's one-line hint previously only described the privacy mechanics ("only a short preview is kept"); now it opens with the actual purpose ("Check how a message reads before you send it.") and hides once the thread has messages so it doesn't clutter an ongoing conversation. Separately verified the reverse-chron sort order concern directly via curl against the live API (two sessions created back to back, newest returned first) rather than guessing -- it was already correct; the behavior the user saw was almost certainly the long-running port-8460 server still serving pre-change code, restarted multiple times this session for exactly that reason.

Verified live in the browser pane throughout, not just read back: blank-launch Written thread, a real draft sent and a real tone-check reply rendered in-thread, the session appearing in the sidebar list with title/preview after that reply, "+" correctly starting a genuinely fresh blank thread, Conflicts/Meetings unaffected by any of it. **Not done:** no test suite exists for any of this (same open item as before); this session's own verification ran against a throwaway port (8461/8462) to avoid colliding with another chat's live 8460 process, then the real 8460 was restarted with the final code -- a real end-to-end click-test on 8460 itself, by the user, hasn't happened yet.)

**Earlier:** 2026-10-21 (first item of the manager-user-test punch list: wired Seam into the shared deploy/local-proxy infrastructure, which it was never part of before -- `apps/seam` only ever ran standalone on its own port. Confirmed via `run_all.sh`/`Caddyfile`'s own prior comments that this was a real, known gap ("apps/manager-app and apps/group-session-app still aren't wired into run_all.sh/Caddyfile's unified local proxy"), not something already done. Added `/manager/*` -> `:8460` to both the local `Caddyfile` and the production `deploy/render.Caddyfile`, added `seam` to `run_all.sh`'s and `deploy/start.sh`'s app-start lists, and added `apps/seam/requirements.txt` to `deploy/Dockerfile`'s pip install (no new env vars needed -- Seam only reads `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, already in `render.yaml`, and shares the already-pre-downloaded `tiny.en` Whisper model).

Found and fixed a real bug this would have caused in production: `apps/seam/static/index.html` had every `/api/...` fetch (17 call sites) and the joint-session-explainer `<iframe>` src hardcoded as an absolute path, which works standalone at `:8460`'s own root but breaks the instant the app is reverse-proxied under `/manager/` -- the exact same class of bug `apps/thimble/static/session.html` hit and documented in its own `BASE_PATH`/`apiPath()` comment ("the hardcoded '/' version ... sent every call straight to mend-mvp.onrender.com/api/start, a 404 ... a real production outage, not just a local quirk"). Ported the same fix: since `index.html` is served at Seam's actual mount root (confirmed via `server.py`'s `@app.get("/")`), `BASE_PATH = location.pathname.replace(/[^/]*$/, '/')` derives correctly whether standalone (`/`) or proxied (`/manager/`), same as Thimble's own index-level page. Wrapped all 17 fetch call sites in `apiPath(...)`, and moved the explainer iframe's `src` out of static markup into a small JS snippet (`frame.src = apiPath(frame.dataset.srcRel)`) that runs once at load, so it goes through the same helper. **Earlier:** 2026-10-21 (built Suture's own private pre-join intake, a real feature now, not just the aspirational framing `facilitator-joint-session-system-prompt.md`'s opening line already described ("...who each already completed a private initial side conversation with you..."). Each employee's own intake now starts the instant THEIR link connects -- it no longer waits for the other person, so nobody sits on a blank "waiting for the other participant" screen before anything happens. Server-side (`apps/suture/server.py`): `Session` gained a fully separate per-role conversation thread (`intake_conversation`/`intake_started`/`intake_done`/`intake_summary`) that never merges with the shared joint `conversation` or the other role's own thread -- `intake_facilitator_reply()` is a parallel sibling to `facilitator_reply()`, same rollback-on-failure convention, different system prompt. `maybe_start_intake()` fires per-role on websocket connect; `maybe_start()` now gates the joint room's opening on both roles being connected AND both having finished their own intake, not just both connected. The joint kickoff turn (`build_kickoff_turn()`, replacing the old static `KICKOFF_TURN` constant) is now built from both employees' `intake_summary` -- Thimble opens the room actually informed by both sides, explicitly instructed never to repeat either person's private words back to them verbatim or reveal it's drawing on something said privately.

Frontend (`apps/suture/static/index.html`): the two-tile `.participants-row` stays hidden until the joint room actually opens (`enterJointPhase()`, triggered only by the un-prefixed `thinking`/`message` events, never the new `intake_`-prefixed ones) -- before that, the existing avatar + chat-panel UI doubles as a single-employee intake chat, no new screen needed. New `intake_thinking`/`intake_message`/`intake_complete` websocket event types; the employee's own sent intake text is echoed client-side (`sendMessage()`) rather than server-side, since that thread has exactly one viewer and nothing to keep in sync, unlike the joint room's broadcast echo. A new `waiting_other` avatar/header state ("Almost there…" / "Waiting for the other person to finish up.") covers the gap between finishing your own intake and the room actually opening.

**Earlier:** 2026-10-19 (long pitch-strategy brainstorm session, doc-only -- no app code touched. Three pieces of real work:

1) **Verified the chatbot-usage data case, not just repeated it.** New [docs/pitch-strategy/23_CHATBOT_SOCIAL_DYNAMICS_USAGE_EVIDENCE.md](docs/pitch-strategy/23_CHATBOT_SOCIAL_DYNAMICS_USAGE_EVIDENCE.md): pulled OpenAI's full "How People Use ChatGPT" paper (the actual PDF, read page by page, not a secondhand summary) for its 24-category taxonomy, Anthropic's affective-conversations report, Pew's exact age-breakdown chart, and traced the BYU "Secret Soulmates" and Hily dating-dealbreaker surveys to their primary sources, correcting a couple of numbers along the way. Also ran an actual mini-study: pulled ~85 real Google Play reviews across the ChatGPT/Claude/Gemini apps looking for relationship-conflict mentions, found zero, and documented *why* (review UIs select for product-friction complaints, not use-case content) as a real finding rather than a failed pull. Separately, checked and explicitly rejected a fabricated "~40% of workers use AI for workplace tone-buffering (Workhuman)" stat that had been pasted in from a different AI session -- flagged in the doc so it never quietly resurfaces in pitch copy.

2) **Added source-verified HR-reporting/backchannel data** to [docs/pitch-strategy/20_REMOTE_CONFLICT_RESEARCH_AND_STATISTICS.md](docs/pitch-strategy/20_REMOTE_CONFLICT_RESEARCH_AND_STATISTICS.md) §5 (HR Acuity 2023 survey, n=1,980: only 58% of people who experience/witness workplace misconduct report it, down from 64% in 2019; Talk to Spot/Dr. Julia Shaw study, n=1,000+: 77% of witnesses never tell HR but 67% tell friends/family instead) -- the rest of that doc is still flagged unverified, this section specifically is not.

3) **Shifted primary product focus from Thimble to Seam** (manager-facing app), captured in new [docs/pitch-strategy/24_SEAM_PRODUCT_PITCH_AND_ADOPTION_STRATEGY.md](docs/pitch-strategy/24_SEAM_PRODUCT_PITCH_AND_ADOPTION_STRATEGY.md): the stigma-as-real-adoption-blocker analysis, three independent manager incentives to recommend it downward (self-interest / skip-level cascade / teach-to-fish), a proposed Thimble+Suture+Loom+Hem bundle under Seam with a two-part reason why manager-initiated Suture sessions work where peer-initiated ones don't, and a growth-vs-crisis user segmentation. The real substance is architectural: identified a genuine privacy problem (employees would rightly be furious about a persistent conflict log existing about them, even anonymized -- a disappearing conversation is the norm they already accept, a queryable trail is not) and resolved it -- Seam never retains the conversation, only the resulting action item ("talk to employee X about Y"), encrypted at rest and decrypted only transiently inside an isolated/enclave process, same pattern already named in the Seam roadmap for edge audio. Also caught and corrected an overclaimed privacy promise ("encrypted so no one can see it, including the manager") before it could reach external pitch copy -- not literally true if the model needs the content as context; replaced with the honest, narrower, still-strong version. [apps/manager-app/seam-product-roadmap.md](apps/manager-app/seam-product-roadmap.md)'s Phase 2 `cases.db` item is now flagged RE-SCOPE NEEDED and struck through pending that resolution. Also built a 14-slide pitch-deck Artifact walking through the whole narrative (lives on claude.ai, not in this repo).

**Not done:** safety-floor adversarial testing against subtle harassment framings (flagged as a hard pre-launch dependency for the "HR can defer to Seam first" pitch, not started); outcome-tracking system (idea stage only, no design); server-side-encrypted vs. device-side-only storage for the action-plan data not decided; the pitch deck's moat/HR-angle/risks slides still reflect the framing from before the §7-8 trail-problem work and haven't been updated to match.)

**Earlier:** 2026-10-18 (Thimble's Voice-view session, `apps/intake-app/static/session.html`. Two real things shipped on `main`: 1) the opening greeting and the hands-free hint message now reveal word-by-word (`addMessage`'s new `animate` flag + `revealMessageText`) instead of landing as a complete block instantly, matching how every other reply already streams in. 2) Found and fixed a real bug: `avatarState` was being set to `'thinking'` *after* calling `feedSpeechText`, which meant that whenever TTS was muted (`ttsMuted`, persisted across reloads via `localStorage['mendTtsMuted']`) `feedSpeechText`'s own correct early-return (`avatarState='idle'`) got immediately clobbered back to `'thinking'` with nothing left to ever move it off again -- reported live as "stuck starting in thinking mode," root-caused via a timestamped `avatarState`/audio-event trace run in the tester's own browser, not guessed. Fix: set `'thinking'` *before* calling `feedSpeechText` in both the text- and voice-mode greeting branches, so its own correction (if any) is what sticks. Also added an 8s safety-net fallback (`stuckCheckGen`/`replyGen`-guarded) for the separate case where TTS genuinely never starts for environment reasons.

Separately: a full redesign of the Listening-state "hearing" ripple (replacing the old sine-decay polygon-deformation engine with a spokes-and-balls concept, 3 traveling waves, tuned live against a standalone artifact) was built, iterated on extensively, and ultimately **reverted off `main`** after real instability (a CSS `border-radius` transition fighting the clip-path, SVG `<clipPath>` union silently failing inside a `<g>` wrapper, several rounds of visual mismatches) made the session unproductive. That work is preserved, not lost, on branch `thimble-ripple-wip` (commit `c92d7fe`) for a future session to pick up with a clearer run at it -- do not merge that branch to `main` as-is, it was explicitly parked mid-debug. Two small layout fixes also shipped on `main` independent of the ripple work: the floor shadow/state-pill vertical gap (was ~4px, now 44px), and the hold-to-talk button's corner inset made symmetric (`right` now equals `bottom` at both breakpoints). **Not done:** the ripple redesign itself remains unshipped; `thimble-ripple-wip` is where to resume it.)
