- **`manager-app`/Loom: removed the "Draft update for your boss" button and its `POST /api/cases/{id}/managing-up` endpoint, at explicit request -- the manager-up translator stays purely conversational.** The facilitator prompt's existing "Managing up, on request" section already produces the scripted upward update whenever the manager asks for it in chat; the dedicated button was redundant with that, not the only way to reach it. `MANAGING_UP_PROMPT` and the endpoint deleted from `server.py`, the button/its wiring/`draftManagingUp()` deleted from `static/index.html`, `README.md` updated to say so explicitly rather than silently going stale. Also restyled "Close case" from a slightly-rounded rectangle to a real pill (`border-radius: 999px`), the only button left in the thread header now. Verified live: button gone, Close case renders as a pill, reopening an existing case still works end to end, no console errors.
- **`manager-app`/Loom: real names the manager types are now redacted live to "Employee A"/"Employee B" (etc.), Thimble can add plan steps itself (not just the manager), and cases get a short Loom-authored title separate from the fuller "Inciting incident" sentence.** Three related additions, all driven by new `<mend_signal>` fields: (1) `names` -- Thimble tags each real name with a stable slot letter and a short role descriptor; the server keeps an in-memory per-case roster (never written to `cases.db`, same treatment as the raw conversation text already gets) and redacts every occurrence across the manager's own messages, both retroactively in the just-sent bubble (a brief redact-reveal flash) and on reload; each "Employee A" tag is clickable and shows the descriptor in a tooltip. Also applied defensively to the assistant's own reply text, which caught a real leak during testing -- the model said a name directly despite the prompt's existing rule against it, and the live `/messages` response wasn't being redacted before this fix even though the reload path already was. (2) `plan_steps_to_add` -- Thimble adds a concrete plan step itself once beat 3 lands on one, server-side de-duplicated by exact text since the model isn't shown the current plan list; steps added this way carry a small "added by Loom" tag distinguishing them from manager-added ones. (3) `case_title` -- a short 3-6 word title shown in the sidebar/thread header, separate from `tension_summary` (which now does one job: the "Inciting incident" panel text). New `title` column on `cases`, migrated in place like the others. Verified live end-to-end: a full conversation correctly tagged two names consistently across turns, the tooltip showed the right descriptor, the assistant-reply leak was caught and fixed, Loom added two real plan steps at the point of agreement, a short case title appeared distinct from the inciting-incident text, and all of it survived a page reload. One real friction point hit while verifying, not a code bug: this dev server's `--reload-include` for the system prompt `.md` file silently does nothing (`watchfiles` isn't installed, so only `.py` changes auto-reload) -- a prompt-only edit needs a manual full server restart to actually take effect, confirmed by calling the model directly with the same prompt text outside the app and seeing it behave correctly there while the live server (still running the pre-edit prompt) did not.
- **`manager-app`: gave the app a product name ("Loom") and a real onboarding empty state + help modal, replacing the bare "Pick a case on the left, or start a new one" and the placeholder "M" circle.** The directory and file names stay `manager-app`; only the UI shows "Loom" -- page title, a new brand lockup (the real two-circle Mend mark, same inline SVG path `voice-transcript-split.html` already uses, plus the "Loom" wordmark) in the sidebar header, and a "?" help button next to it that reopens the same explainer any time, since the empty state itself disappears the moment a case is open. The empty state differentiates first-time (full explainer: what Loom is, the four-step unpack -> plan -> act -> report-back flow, the names-stay-hidden privacy promise, a prominent CTA) from returning (shorter prompt, same CTA relabeled "Start a new case") via a `hasCases` check rather than one static message for both. Verified live in the Browser pane: both empty-state variants, the help modal (open via the "?" button, close via both the X and a click outside the modal), and no console errors; the real Mend mark SVG renders correctly at both the small sidebar size and the larger empty-state badge size.
- **`manager-app`: wired the "managing up" translator and the peer-session hand-off into real actions, closing out both items `README.md` previously listed as "not yet built."** Both were previously just prompt text telling Thimble to *offer* them in conversation, with no actual affordance behind that offer. (1) A "Draft update for your boss" button in the thread header, available any time a case is open, hits a new `POST /api/cases/{id}/managing-up` endpoint that sends a hidden system-note turn asking Thimble for only the 2-4 sentence scripted upward-facing update (no preamble), reusing the same translation habit from the prompt's beat-3 instructions. (2) The facilitator prompt gained a new `handoff_ready` boolean in its `<mend_signal>` block, set `true` only on the turn its reply text actually makes the hand-off offer (not before); a new "Start peer session" banner appears in the UI once that flips, and hitting "Start peer session" calls a new `POST /api/cases/{id}/handoff` endpoint that mints a fresh `joint-session-app` room code and returns both role links (`?code=...&role=a`/`role=b`) -- the same manual-pairing link pattern `joint-session-app` already exposes, confirmed directly from its own module docstring rather than assumed. No case context crosses over to the peer session, matching `WORKFLOW.md`'s "zero context leakage" design -- it's genuinely just two fresh anonymous links into a brand-new room. The code is stored on the case (`handoff_code`) and the endpoint is idempotent, so reopening the case or re-clicking the banner shows the same links instead of minting a new room each time. `cases` table migrated with a plain `ALTER TABLE` guarded by a `PRAGMA table_info` check, so an existing local `cases.db` from before this change upgrades in place rather than needing a wipe. Added a `mend-manager-app` entry to `.claude/launch.json` (port 8426) since one didn't exist yet. Found and fixed one real bug while verifying live in the Browser pane: `updateCaseFromResult()`, called after every message/managing-up reply, updated the sidebar and thread title but never re-rendered the handoff banner, so `handoff_ready` flipping true mid-conversation was invisible until a manual page reload -- confirmed via a direct `fetch('/api/cases/{id}')` showing `handoff_ready: true` server-side while the banner stayed hidden client-side; fixed by having `updateCaseFromResult()` call the same `renderHandoffBanner()` the initial page-load path already used. Verified end-to-end against the real Claude API: a full conversation (initial report -> fact-finding -> both parties' real explanation surfacing -> delivery-mode follow-up -> agreement) correctly walked through all three beats before Thimble offered the hand-off (didn't jump to it early), the banner appeared, "Start peer session" produced two distinct, correctly-formed role links, and reloading the case reused the same code rather than minting a new one.
- **`intake-app`: built `static/voice-transcript-live.html` -- a real, working version of the `voice-transcript-split.html` design reference (permanent side-by-side voice stage + transcript, vs. `index.html`'s own tab-toggled single view), served at `/voice-live` (new route in [server.py](intake-app/server.py), `@app.get("/voice-live")`, same pattern as `/survey` -- needed because `apiPath()`'s `BASE_PATH` derives from `location.pathname`, which only resolves to the real `/api/...` routes when the page itself loads from a root-level path; confirmed live as the cause of a wall of 404s before the fix).** Deliberately NOT a from-scratch rebuild of the reference page's fake markup/JS -- that page had no mic, no TTS, no websocket by design (a layout reference only). Instead, this duplicates `index.html` (the real, battle-tested call engine: Deepgram STT, Claude streaming replies, OpenAI TTS, the full Thimble avatar motion system) and changes only what the split-view layout actually requires, keeping everything else -- same element IDs, same functions -- untouched: `ui` defaults to `'voice'` instead of `'text'` (the Voice tab's real tap-to-talk/avatar/state-pill machinery exists in `index.html` already but is currently unreachable in production, since the Voice/Text tab toggle itself is hidden -- this page is what finally exercises that dormant code path for real); `#voicePanel`'s own `hidden` attribute is dropped and a new `.live-split` CSS override reflows it and `#chatPanel` into a permanent side-by-side pair instead of index.html's exclusive single-pane-at-a-time layout; the now-redundant duplicate controls (`#voicePanel`'s own built-in transcript toggle/panel, the composer's separate hold-to-dictate mic) are hidden, since the chat-panel is now always the one visible transcript and push-to-talk has exactly one affordance instead of two competing ones.
  Per explicit design decision (asked directly, since this conflicted with `index.html`'s own prior explicit decision that Voice mode is hands-free-only and push-to-talk belongs only in the composer): **this page's hold button is real hold-to-record/release-to-send push-to-talk**, not hands-free VAD and not the dormant app's tap-to-toggle. `talkMicBtn`'s click handler was replaced with `pointerdown`/`pointerup`/`pointercancel` listeners that call the exact same `startRecordingTurn(true)` / `mediaRecorder.stop()` pipeline tap-to-talk already used (same `holdControlled` flag, same real transcription+reply+TTS cycle) -- only the gesture that starts/ends it changed. Restyled from a small text pill ("Tap to talk"/"Tap to stop") into an 88px circle with the text visually hidden (`font-size:0`, still the real accessible name) behind a centered icon that swaps mic/waveform via the existing `.recording` class, reusing `talk-mic-pulse` verbatim -- same recording-color language as the rest of the app.
  One real bug found and fixed by actually loading it, not just reasoning about the port: `feedSpeechText`'s own TTS gate (`if (ui !== 'voice' && (!currentReplyShouldSpeak || ttsMuted))`) was written for `index.html`, where `ui` is normally `'text'` -- with `ui` now permanently `'voice'` on this page, that condition was never true at all, which silently meant `ttsMuted`/the mute button had nothing left to gate (every reply would have kept speaking even after muting). Reordered to check `ttsMuted` unconditionally first.
  Verified live end-to-end in the Browser pane against the real dev server: the real greeting played with full avatar motion and captions; a real text turn sent through the composer got a real streamed Claude reply with synced TTS (`/api/speak` calls firing, captions advancing); the hold button's `pointerdown` correctly invoked the real `getUserMedia`/recording pipeline and, since this sandboxed browser has no mic to grant, hit the same graceful "Microphone blocked -- Try again" error path `index.html`'s own tap-to-talk already has (confirms the wiring reaches real app code, not a mock) -- the full audio round-trip itself needs a real mic to confirm, same limitation noted elsewhere in this log for anything mic-dependent. `index.html` itself is untouched (confirmed via `git diff --stat`); the new page is fully additive (`server.py`'s new route, plus the new file). Not yet tested: the TTS-mute fix specifically with a muted turn, and the hold button against a real microphone.
  **Two follow-up correction rounds, both direct live feedback against a wireframe the user had already supplied (the same one `voice-transcript-split.html` was itself built from):** (1) The first pass above reused `index.html`'s own `call-header`/`control-bar` chrome instead of that approved wireframe's actual layout -- corrected by rebuilding the voice pane to match it: a Mend logo + "Private" chip top bar (ported from `voice-transcript-split.html` verbatim, replacing the Thimble-name/lock-tooltip row), the voice stage set to fill whatever width the transcript sidebar doesn't use (`flex:1 1 auto` vs. the sidebar's fixed `flex:0 0 340px`) instead of splitting evenly, hold-to-talk relocated out of the control bar into a bottom-right `.hold-dock` with a real progress ring -- deliberately driven by actual mic level (`updateHoldRing`, hooked into `animateAvatar`'s existing per-frame `level` calculation) rather than a decorative fixed-duration fill, an honest "it's hearing you" indicator instead of a fake timer -- mute relocated from the control bar onto the avatar's own corner (real `#muteBtn`, same click handler, just moved in the markup), and working Hide/Show toggles on both panes ported from `voice-transcript-split.html`'s own `wireLivePaneToggle` pattern, adapted to toggle the real `#voicePanel`/`#chatPanel` elements -- including a real `[hidden]`-vs-author-CSS `display:flex` specificity bug caught live (both elements set their own `display` in the stylesheet, which silently beat the UA stylesheet's `[hidden]{display:none}`; fixed with an explicit `!important` override) and a second pass once `flex:0 0 340px` was confirmed to leave the chat pane pinned narrow even after the voice pane was hidden, fixed with a `.live-split.voice-hidden` class flip so the one remaining pane actually fills the freed width. pauseBtn/captionsToggleBtn/the old built-in transcript toggle are dropped from view (not in the wireframe), kept in the DOM rather than deleted. (2) Reported live immediately after: still not actually full-bleed -- `index.html`'s own `.wrap` caps at 1200px, centered with side padding, which is correct for a windowed single-call UI but wrong for this page's whole premise (a full-screen frame). Fixed with a `body:has(.live-split) .wrap` override (max-width/margin/padding cleared, real viewport height) and dropped the panels' own rounded-corner/border chrome, which read as a smaller floating card inside the now-full-bleed frame instead of being the frame itself. Both rounds verified live in the Browser pane against the real dev server, including a full Hide/Show round-trip in both directions after the fix.
- **`intake-app`: new `static/voice-transcript-split.html` -- a standalone design-reference page (same convention as `thimble-states.html`/`listening-options.html`: self-contained, not wired to a real mic/TTS/websocket) exploring a permanently side-by-side voice-stage + transcript layout, vs. `index.html`'s current toggled transcript panel.** Started from a wireframe the user supplied for placement only, then went through several real design/iteration rounds, each driven by live follow-up feedback: (1) first pass placed real app components (Thimble avatar engine, state pill, mute button) at the wireframe's positions but didn't design anything -- corrected after explicit "you don't need to redesign this... reference the other apps for look and feel" and "it does not need to say that, work on the interaction when its being held down" feedback, which led to checking every sibling app (`joint-session-app`, `post-session-app`, `manager-coaching-app`) and the `design_handoffs/` originals to confirm the real established "recording" language is just `talk-mic-pulse`'s box-shadow pulse + the composer's 4-bar wave, copied verbatim rather than the ripple-ring/5-bar waveform originally invented. (2) The hide/show toggles for each pane (voice vs. transcript) were found to have a real bug: the control that reopens a hidden pane lived inside that same pane, so hiding it removed its own way back -- fixed with separate `showVoiceBtn`/`showTranscriptBtn` rail buttons plus mutual `ctrl-hidden` logic so a pane can't be hidden if it's the only one left visible. (3) The "Mend" identity (logo mark -- the real two-circle "m" from `mend-brand-kit/logo/`, lowercase wordmark, Private chip) went through three placements before landing correctly, per direct correction each time: first duplicated per-pane, then moved to a full-width bar inside the window, finally settled as a small floating badge entirely OUTSIDE the communications window, above its top-left corner, present regardless of which pane is shown or hidden. Thimble's own avatar (a mini instance of the same engine, driven by the same animation loop as the main avatar) was separately added inside the transcript pane's own header, top-left, shown only once voice is collapsed to text-only -- like a normal chat app's header avatar -- which required moving the `showVoiceBtn` rail button from top-left to top-right to stop it overlapping that new avatar. All interaction states (mute dim/pause, hold-to-talk press/pulse/wave/ring-fill, both hide/show directions, composer send-button activation) verified live in the Browser pane, including dispatching real `pointerdown`/`pointerup` events to check the held state. Served via the existing `mend-intake` dev server at `/static/voice-transcript-split.html`; not linked from `index.html` or any nav.
- **`intake-app`: found and fixed two real bugs in the composer's hold-to-dictate recording, both confirmed against the user's own mic and hardware, not just reasoned about -- plus a third tuning fix confirmed live.** (1) The 400ms minimum-hold floor (`COMPOSER_MIN_HOLD_MS`, meant to avoid a near-empty clip on a fast release) was measured from `composerPressedAt`, the original button-press timestamp, in `composerRelease()`'s normal release path -- but that's *before* `getUserMedia()` resolves, so real getUserMedia latency silently counted toward the floor without the mic actually being open yet. A real, deliberate hold could still produce a near-empty clip (confirmed via the server's own log: a genuine "He's a peer" utterance uploaded as `audio_duration_s=0.3`). Fixed by adding `composerRecordingStartedAt`, set only once `mediaRecorder.start()` actually runs, and measuring the floor from that instead. Verified synthetically (a simulated 900ms getUserMedia delay + 350ms real hold correctly did NOT trigger an immediate stop under the new logic, where the old logic demonstrably would have) and by the user's own repeated real-mic retest, landing on "it finally got it right." (2) Reported live immediately after: a quick press-and-release with no real hold left the new recording waveform animation (see below) stuck on indefinitely. Root cause: the waveform was switched on unconditionally once recording actually began, with no check for whether the user had already released by then, and nothing in the release path (which had already run, before recording even started) was left to ever turn it back off. Fixed by skipping the "recording" visuals in `startComposerRecording()` when `composerReleaseRequested` is already true, and moving the wave/button-active cleanup into `mediaRecorder.onstop` itself -- the one point guaranteed to run whenever a recording genuinely ends regardless of release timing. Verified with a simulated quick tap (a fake `MediaStream` from an `AudioContext` destination node, since this environment has no real mic to test with) -- press, release after 30ms, confirmed the wave/button/label all cleanly reset once the floor-delayed stop completed instead of hanging. (3) Separately reported live: the last word of a real short phrase ("that's the main thing" -> "thing" dropped) still going missing even after fix (1). The composer's post-release Deepgram drain/grace window (lets buffered audio flush before the socket closes) was 300ms, covering roughly one `onaudioprocess` flush but not Deepgram's own recognition lag on a word with no natural trailing silence after it (the button release *is* the silence here, unlike hands-free which gets a real pause to key off). Raised to 600ms; confirmed fixed by the user's retest ("it seemed to work"). Per explicit follow-up direction ("err on the side of caution not speed... i really dont want it to miss anything"), saved as a standing rule in memory: future tuning of any mic cutoff/grace/endpointing delay in this app should default toward longer/safer, not snappier, unless explicitly reported as feeling slow.
- **`intake-app`: UI polish pass on the composer's hold-to-dictate experience, driven by live back-and-forth feedback in the same session as the bug fixes above.** The instructional tip ("Press and hold, then wait to hear the beep...") now shows once ever -- on hover if that happens first (desktop) or on the first real hold otherwise (touch, which never fires a hover event) -- instead of on every page load or every press; persisted via `localStorage('micTipSeen')`. The composer mic button widened 44px -> 64px and switched from its own `#8C3B2E` red to the same pink/ink recording color and pulse animation Voice view's own tap-to-talk button already uses (`.talk-mic-btn.recording`) -- one recording-color language across both UIs. A new CSS waveform-bars indicator slides out of the mic button into the input field while actually recording, gated to start at the beep (not on press, which would claim audio capture before the mic is actually open) and correctly suppressed/cleared per the stuck-state fix above. A new mic-lifecycle label ("Starting..." / "Stopped recording") fills the two gaps the waveform doesn't cover -- the `getUserMedia` setup delay after press, and the moment right after release before "Transcribing..." takes over the input's placeholder. Replies now speak aloud (TTS) when they follow a dictated voice-memo turn specifically, not a typed one -- the reasoning being that someone who just spoke out loud to dictate isn't worried about being overheard, so hearing the reply back costs nothing new. Implemented as a `composerDictated` flag, set on a successful dictation and consumed by `sendText()` into a per-cycle `currentReplyShouldSpeak` flag that `feedSpeechText()` checks alongside the existing `ui === 'voice'` gate -- reuses the existing sentence-streamed TTS queue as-is, does not re-enable hands-free VAD or barge-in. Verified live: a simulated dictated turn fired real `/api/speak`+`/api/audio` calls and played audio; a follow-up typed turn fired none. The header's mini Thimble avatar now also nods and moves its mouth off the real mic level while dictating, matching Voice view's hands-free listening animation at a smaller scale -- turned out to need no new animation code, since both avatar instances already share one `avatarState`-driven motion system; composer recording just wasn't setting `avatarState = 'listening'` before. Also in this pass: the opening line ("Welcome to Mend.") now breaks onto its own line from the rest of the intro (display-only via `white-space:pre-wrap`, doesn't change what TTS actually speaks); the second onboarding message no longer promotes a "Go hands-free" button, per the broader move toward the composer as the primary path; the Deepgram Aura TTS A/B option is hidden from the overflow menu and `ttsProvider` forced to `'openai'`, at explicit request ("it was worse than openai") -- code kept, not deleted; and the composer's placeholder text changed from "Tell Thimble what happened..." to "Explain what happened..." so it still reads correctly past the first turn.
- **`manager-app`: built and live-tested a real FastAPI + SQLite backend plus a plain HTML/JS frontend, implementing the design in `PHILOSOPHY.md`/`WORKFLOW.md`/`THIMBLE_MINDSET.md` for real.** New `facilitator-case-plan-system-prompt.md` at the repo root (Thimble's actual running prompt for this app) integrates those three docs' core axioms, the "banish conflict resolution language" reframe table, and the three-beat facilitation architecture (perspective calibration -> structural alignment -> operating protocol), plus the repo's standard mandatory-floor/hard-boundary/em-dash-ban conventions already used by the other `facilitator-*-system-prompt.md` files. Persistence is deliberately narrow, matching `WORKFLOW.md`'s anonymity design: `manager-app/cases.db` (SQLite, git-ignored) stores only each case's role-anonymized state anchor (`tension_summary`/`last_action`/`next_action`, written by the facilitator itself via its `<mend_signal>` block, overwritten each turn) and its plan steps -- never a transcript, never a name. The actual back-and-forth conversation text lives only in the running process's memory and is lost on restart; reopening a case re-seeds a fresh conversation from the stored anchor + plan via a hidden context message, so Thimble picks the case back up without the exact prior wording. Frontend (`static/index.html`, no build step, same pattern as `../intake-app`) implements the mockup's two-pane chat layout (case list left, one thread right) plus the right-side "plan so far" checklist, with real add/edit/toggle/note interactions, not static mockup chrome. Two real bugs found and fixed by actually running it against the live API, not just reasoning about the code: (1) an early prompt version let the facilitator's own reply text echo back a real name the manager had typed ("what did Marcus's feedback look like"), even though the persisted anchor was already correctly role-based -- the persisted *data* being clean isn't the same as the *conversation* being clean, and only the former was being checked at first. Fixed by making the prompt's "names stay out of it" rule explicit that it covers every reply, not just what gets saved; re-verified across a fresh multi-turn conversation with no leak. (2) The `<mend_signal>` control block was correctly stripped from the *immediate* reply returned by `POST /messages`, but a case's full history as returned by `GET /api/cases/{id}` (used on reload) was reading the raw stored turn, signal block and all -- invisible until actually reloading the page and looking, caught via a live Browser-pane screenshot showing the raw JSON block sitting in a chat bubble. Fixed by storing a separate display-safe `visible` copy of each assistant turn alongside the raw-with-signal copy the model's own conversation history needs, and reloading in the browser confirmed the fix. Verified live end-to-end via the Browser pane: new case creation, the opening line, a multi-turn conversation that built up `tension_summary` while never storing a name, plan-step add/toggle (checkbox fills and strikes through correctly)/note-placeholder-text-changes-on-toggle, and a page reload correctly replaying clean history. Not yet built: the "managing up" translator and the decoupled hand-off into a peer-to-peer session, both described in `WORKFLOW.md` -- the prompt tells Thimble to *offer* the hand-off in conversation, but there's no button, endpoint, or actual peer-session creation wired to it yet.
- **`manager-app`: wrote up the product philosophy, concrete workflow, and a draft facilitator mindset as three new docs (`PHILOSOPHY.md`, `WORKFLOW.md`, `THIMBLE_MINDSET.md`), from a design conversation rather than new code.** Still a static mockup with no backend, same as before; this just captures the thinking in writing so it isn't stranded in chat history. `PHILOSOPHY.md`: why a manager-facing tool sidesteps the embarrassment of an employee-facing conflict app, conflict as task/process/relationship (Jehn's taxonomy), why task conflict mutates into relationship conflict under asymmetric KPIs and low psychological safety, why peer-built agreements outlast managerial arbitration (self-determination theory, the IKEA effect, facilitative-vs-arbitrated ADR outcomes, the triadic threat response), and the core reframe -- present the work as a forward-looking action plan rather than "conflict resolution," with interpersonal harmony as a hoped-for byproduct, never a requirement. `WORKFLOW.md`: the concrete flow (open a case -> unpack -> plan -> act off-app -> report back), a worked four-step staging-plan example, a "managing up" scripting example, how case state can persist across a multi-day intervention without breaking the no-real-names rule (role de-identification, a structured tension/last-action/next-action anchor instead of a transcript, workspace partitioning with zero HR/exec query access), and how a manager can spin off a decoupled peer-to-peer session once both sides agree on the actual blocker. `THIMBLE_MINDSET.md`: draft system-prompt material (four core axioms, a language-substitution table banning "conflict resolution" phrasing, the three-beat facilitation architecture, manager-coaching-track behavior, and the safety-floor boundary) written to be integrated once this app gets a real backend and facilitator, parallel to the existing `facilitator-*-system-prompt.md` files at the repo root. Nothing here is wired into a running prompt yet.
- **`about.html`: fixed a real mobile-wrapping bug, flattened card chrome, and restored the hero/CTA Thimble avatar, which had been silently broken since 2026-10-06.** Found by actually loading the page at a 375px mobile viewport (a Browser-pane screenshot glitch first made it look worse than it was -- a scroll+screenshot race that left stale/blank captures; confirmed real vs. artifact each time via `getBoundingClientRect`/computed-style checks, not screenshots alone, per the existing false-alarm lesson in memory). Real bug: `.compare-row`/`.privacy-row` (the "Cost of the Usual Options" and "Radical Zero-Trace Privacy" tables) used a fixed label+description two-column flex layout with no stacking breakpoint, squeezing descriptions into a ~150px column. Per direct follow-up feedback, this and the matching `.why-columns` two-column grid were changed to stack unconditionally (not just under a mobile breakpoint -- the same side-by-side pattern was bad on desktop too). Also flattened the box chrome (border/shadow/background) off the "Three Ways to Use Mend" cards and the two table containers on mobile, since stacking three full-bleed bordered cards read as visually overwhelming -- desktop keeps the card treatment. Separately found: the hero and closing-CTA Thimble avatars had been rendering as an empty glow with no shape at all since `3100457` deleted `design_handoffs/thimble_avatar-new-mow/thimble-avatar.js` while `about.html`'s `<script src>` still pointed at it (a silent 404, `window.ThimbleAvatar` undefined) -- recovered the original file verbatim from git history (`git show 3100457~1:...`) and restored it at the same path, no about.html script changes needed. Also fixed the "Our Manifesto" button, which was an unstyled native `<button>` (default browser box showing through a CSS rule that only set `border-bottom`) -- now a proper pill matching the rest of the page's pill buttons. Verified live in the Browser pane at both 375px and desktop widths after each fix.
- **New product idea sketched and mocked: `manager-app`, a conflict-case manager for people managers (not employees).** Different audience than the existing apps here, which all face the employee/joint-session side -- this one is for the manager who sees a direct report in conflict (with another report, or with someone on another team) and doesn't know how to navigate it. Core framing: resolving interpersonal conflict is a normal, non-shameful part of the manager job, so the product should feel like a record worth keeping, not a confession. Flow settled on after a few iterations: open a case -> unpack what's going on -> plan the next step together (collaborative, not a canned tip list) -> act off-app -> report back on what happened, looping back to planning if unresolved. Two real design decisions baked in before any mockup: (1) names of the people involved stay hidden by default (role labels like "your report" / "design team lead" instead), since the goal is managing the conflict, not building a record on the individuals; (2) the whole interface is one continuous chat thread, not a multi-step form wizard -- tried a 4-screen stepper-with-progress-dots version first, explicitly rejected as feeling "administrative" / "enterprise," rebuilt as a single two-pane messaging-app layout (conversation list left, one thread right) matching how people already have messy human conversations elsewhere. A right-side "plan so far" panel was added on top of the chat (checklist of agreed steps, each editable with a pencil icon, a note field for what happened when a step is marked done, "+ add a step") -- the one piece of visible structure in an otherwise unstructured conversation. Static HTML mockup only, no backend: `manager-app/App.dc.html` + `manager-app/canvas.json`, originally built and iterated as a Design-type Claude Artifact (https://claude.ai/artifact/8HrMPhjifgqAUiKVzPHx2L) and copied into the repo for reference. Not wired to any app or server here yet -- purely a UI/flow exploration.
- **`intake-app`: moved push-to-talk from the Voice panel into the text composer as a hold-to-dictate mic button, at explicit request -- Voice mode is hands-free only now.** Removed `pttMode`, the Auto/Hold toggle, the big circular Voice-panel mic button, and every `!pttMode` guard across hands-free's own VAD/backstop/interrupt-monitor logic (unconditional now, since Voice has only one mode left) -- replaced with a single `holdControlled` flag distinguishing "ends on silence" (hands-free) from "ends on manual release" (the composer). New `composerPress()`/`composerRelease()`/`startComposerRecording()` reuse the same `mediaRecorder`/`dgSocket`/`chunks` machinery hands-free already uses, safe because the two are mutually exclusive by construction. Holding the composer's mic fills the text input for review (not auto-send, confirmed preference -- dictation should be editable before it goes out). Factored the Deepgram drain-wait and dgText/Whisper-fallback resolution into two shared functions (`drainDeepgramSocket`/`resolveTurnTranscript`) since both recording flows need them now; transcription/mic-permission errors surface via the composer's own placeholder since the Voice panel's `statusEl`/`micErrorBanner` are invisible on the Text tab. Found and fixed one real latent bug while verifying live: the release-before-recording-started race fix measured its minimum-hold floor from the original button press, not from when recording actually started -- if `getUserMedia()` took longer than that floor to resolve (confirmed via an isolated repro, and >1900ms in one observed headless run), the floor did nothing and `mediaRecorder.stop()` fired within milliseconds of `.start()`, capturing a near-empty clip. This was latent in the old Voice-panel push-to-talk too, just never triggered by ordinary human timing. Test suite updated to match (four obsolete push-to-talk checks replaced with composer-scoped equivalents in `button_smoke_test.py`; `ptt_roundtrip_test.py` rewritten for the composer's "fills chatInput" outcome). All suites verified passing live: `button_smoke_test.py` (7/7), `ptt_roundtrip_test.py` (2/2), `voice_backstop_test.py` (6/6, confirming hands-free's own backstop logic unaffected), `safety_floor_test.py` (4/4).
- **`intake-app`: push-to-talk follow-up session -- fixed a real release-before-recording-started race, a drain-wait latency bug, redid the You-tile's talking animation, and added true end-to-end mic-driven test coverage.** (1) Reported live right after the hold-tooltip landed: a real hold/release could land entirely inside `pttPress()`'s `await getUserMedia()` window (setup can take real time), and the old `pttRelease()` only knew how to stop an already-recording `mediaRecorder` -- a release that arrived first was silently dropped, so recording then started moments later with nothing left to ever end it ("it kept listening," then a 110-byte clip and "couldn't transcribe audio," confirmed directly from a new byte-size log on `/api/voice`'s upload). Fixed with a `pttReleaseRequested` flag honored the instant `startRecordingTurn()` resolves. (2) Reported live next: "when im using the hold option and i speak in a short session it takes a long time to get a response... thinking much longer than when i send a lot of text." Root cause: the post-release drain wait (`onFinalHint`, added for an earlier dropped-trailing-word fix) only resolved early on a real `is_final`/`utterance_end` message, which normally needs Deepgram's own 1400ms endpointing silence -- hands-free naturally already has that silence by the time it decides to stop, but push-to-talk's whole point is ending the instant the button releases, so this wait was paying its full 1200ms on nearly every single push-to-talk turn. Renamed to `onMessageHint` and widened to fire on any Deepgram message (interim text is already what's read regardless) -- verified both the latency fix (new `tests/ptt_roundtrip_test.py`, release->Thinking ~1.3s vs. the old near-guaranteed 1.2s+whisper-fallback stall) and that the original dropped-word regression is still fixed (`button_smoke_test.py`'s trailing-words check still passes, full phrase intact). (3) You-tile talking animation redone twice more from live feedback: first replaced the old hexagon-wobble `thimblePolygon` clip-path (same shape language already pulled off Thimble itself for reading "weird") with a Honey-colored soft glow matching Thimble's own simplified talking glow, then made both the wobble and glow lean much more directly on real mic level after "there does not seem to be a glow that changes... animation is too subtle," then switched from a scaleX/scaleY squish (read as a stretched oval) to a uniform-scale-plus-position-jiggle wobble after "i want the circle to wobble not stretch." (4) New `tests/ptt_roundtrip_test.py`: true end-to-end push-to-talk coverage (press, hold through real fake-mic speech, release, assert a real reply actually arrives, not just that the transcript bubble shows the right words) plus a latency regression guard, requested directly ("is there anyway to create tests to qa that the functionality works the way its supposed to that uses the mic?") -- building it caught a bug in the TEST itself (waiting for a genuine "Listening" state before switching to Hold mode reliably raced an in-flight hands-free auto-recording), fixed by switching to Hold immediately on page load instead, matching `button_smoke_test.py`'s own existing check.
- **`intake-app`: fixed the Deepgram-path "live" voice turn getting stuck or cut off, through three live-reported bugs in a row, each fixed and then caught introducing the next one -- new `tests/voice_backstop_test.py` now locks in all three.** Reported live: a one-word answer ("peer") left the mic stuck on "Listening" indefinitely. Root cause confirmed directly against the real Deepgram API (a one-off websocket probe, not guesswork): a clean one-word utterance's own `speech_final` lands ~1.5-2s after the word ends, but the only fallback for when Deepgram's own VAD misses a short/quiet real word entirely was a flat `DG_HANG_BACKSTOP_MS` (4000ms) -- the full worst case, felt exactly like "just keeps listening." (1) Lowered it to 2200ms -- immediately reported live as a regression: "it just interrupted me," a full answer now cut off before finishing. The single constant counted quiet time from recording START, so shortening it for faster post-speech turnaround also shortened the PRE-speech reaction-time grace, which real answers (especially after a "peer or manager?"-style question needing a beat to think) routinely exceed. (2) Split into `DG_HANG_BACKSTOP_PRE_MS` (8000ms, before any speech heard) and `DG_HANG_BACKSTOP_POST_MS` (2200ms, after speech then silence), gated on a new `dgSpeechHeard` flag -- reported live immediately after: "it also just cut me off," still too fast to be a real answer. A single loud frame (room-tail echo off Thimble's own just-finished voice, even with `echoCancellation` on) was enough to flip `dgSpeechHeard` true and arm the short timer before the person had said anything. (3) Debounced: `dgSpeechHeard` now requires `DG_SPEECH_CONFIRM_MS` (250ms) of *sustained* level above threshold, not one frame -- same idea `checkVad` already uses for its own speech-start detection, applied here to the Deepgram-path backstop. New `tests/voice_backstop_test.py` (3 fixtures built with `say`+ffmpeg: a one-word answer, a 6s pre-speech pause before a full sentence, and a 150ms blip before a real word) directly reproduces and guards all three regressions -- passed 2/2 on each when this landed. Also fixed in passing: a single stuck-"couldn't transcribe audio" report during this same debugging turned out to be unrelated -- the account's OpenAI TTS key had run out of credits (`429 insufficient_quota`), adding 8-15s of failed-request latency to *every* line Thimble speaks and making unrelated turns look broken too; disabled locally in `.env` (commented, not deleted) to fall through to the existing macOS `say` fallback until credits are topped up. Also fixed `ws_voice`'s own finally-block: it only caught `RuntimeError` on an already-closed socket, but this Starlette version raises `WebSocketDisconnect` instead, logging an unhandled-exception traceback on every single voice turn's teardown -- cosmetic, but noisy enough to obscure real errors while debugging the above.
- **`intake-app`: wired any-of-k self-consistency voting into the real conversation flow, with two real bugs found and fixed by actually running it, not just reasoning about the code.** Built on the per-session architecture fix below. (1) First version fired the extra voting calls whenever the primary call's signal simply hadn't already met the floor -- true on nearly every ordinary turn, confirmed live firing on almost every message. Fixed by giving the facilitator a genuine third option in its own signal: `screen_result` can now be `"uncertain"` (new, alongside null/cleared/floor_met), with explicit prompt instructions for when to use it -- a real coin-flip case, not a hedge for anything merely sensitive. Voting now only fires on an explicit `"uncertain"`, confirmed firing exactly once across the whole existing ~12-turn test suite. (2) Even gated correctly, requiring an extra vote to hit a hard `floor_met` to count as "flagged" meant the ensemble almost never fired -- extra votes judge the same ambiguous input the primary couldn't decide, so they also mostly land on `"uncertain"` themselves (confirmed: 15 clean single-turn samples of a known-ambiguous case came back ~1 floor_met, ~9 uncertain, 0 confidently cleared). Fixed with `vote_flags()`, broadening "flagged" to floor_met OR uncertain -- catch rate on the known ambiguous case went from 1/10 before this fix to ~60% (6/10, then 5/8) across two independent runs after. When an extra vote flags it, the primary's reply is discarded and a fresh one requested via a system note, letting the facilitator's own escalation-disclosure instructions produce the actual words, consistent with "never silently." New `tests/voting_catch_rate_test.py` locks in a >=30% minimum catch-rate floor (well below the observed ~60%, to absorb real sampling variance while still catching a regression toward either bug above). Scoped to `/api/message` only, not the streaming endpoint. All three existing regression suites unaffected: `safety_floor_test.py` (4/4), `concurrent_sessions_test.py` (5/5), `button_smoke_test.py` (9/9, browser-driven, never touches `session_id`).
- **`intake-app`: gave conversation state per-session scope, the prerequisite for real-time self-consistency voting on ambiguous safety-floor cases.** `server.py`'s `conversation` was a single module-level list shared by every request -- fine for the real single-user app, but it meant firing several independent "judge" conversations concurrently (the actual point of voting -- fast, no session halt) would have raced each other's state. Replaced with `get_conversation(session_id)`, keyed by an optional `session_id` defaulting to the same implicit session the real frontend already uses -- additive, not a behavior change, confirmed by re-running the existing `safety_floor_test.py` (4/4) and `button_smoke_test.py` (9/9) unchanged, including a bare `POST /api/reset` with no body. New `tests/concurrent_sessions_test.py` fires 5 real conversations as genuinely concurrent requests (`asyncio.gather`, not sequential awaits) with distinct scenarios and confirms zero cross-talk -- each session's reply and `floor_triggered` value landed on its own scenario, 5/5. Scoped to only the text-conversation path; voice/video/survey/analytics endpoints don't touch `conversation` and are untouched. Does not fix the separate, pre-existing `/api/reset`-racing-an-in-flight-reply bug within one session -- only isolates different sessions from each other.
- **`intake-app`: added anonymous usage analytics** (session count, turns per session, text-vs-voice choice, barge-in/interrupt count), at the user's request to see engagement patterns without recording any conversation content. New `POST /api/analytics` endpoint validates a fixed event set (`session_start`/`turn`/`interrupt`/`session_end`) plus an optional `mode` (`text`/`voice`) and `turn` number, keyed only by a random `session_id` the client mints fresh per page load (`crypto.randomUUID()`, not tied to any identity) -- same "nothing is recorded" boundary the rest of the app follows, since no transcript text or audio is ever sent. Appends to a second `events` worksheet in the same Google Sheet the survey already uses (`get_events_sheet()`, created on first use), falling back to local `analytics_events.jsonl`/stdout the same way `get_survey_sheet()` already does. Client-side `logEvent()` fires from the four points that answer the actual questions asked: `start()` on a genuinely fresh session (not a reload/resume) for `session_start`; `sendText()` and the voice-turn-commit point in `startRecordingTurn()`'s `onstop` handler, each tagging `mode` and an incrementing `turn` counter; `interruptNow()` for barge-ins; and a `pagehide` listener (`keepalive: true` so the fetch survives the actual unload) for `session_end`. `/api/analytics` added to the existing rate-limiter's exempt-paths set, same reasoning as `/api/survey` -- it never calls a paid API and firing it shouldn't eat the budget real `/api/message` calls need. Verified directly: ran the real server, sent a text turn through the actual browser UI, and confirmed `session_start` then `turn` (mode: text, turn: 1) both landed in `analytics_events.jsonl` under the same session id, and a bogus event name correctly 400s.
- **`facilitator-intake-system-prompt.md`: closed a real harassment-floor gap found by a boundary sweep, found and fixed in direct response to a flip-flopping result in the safety-floor test above.** New `tests/safety_floor_sweep.py` ran the floor across 13 scenarios (threats, illegal activity, safety risk, 4 other protected-characteristic categories, and a 4-level explicitness ladder within one harassment scenario), each repeated 3x to measure trip-rate rather than asserting a single expected answer. Finding: everything except the appearance/unwanted-attention ladder was rock solid (0/3 or 3/3, both directions) -- the instability was narrow and specific to unwanted-attention cases that never explicitly name a protected characteristic or sexual framing, and counterintuitively keyed more on literal "I told him to stop" phrasing than on severity language ("invasive," "doesn't respect boundaries"). Added one sentence to the employee-conflict path's mandatory floor: unwanted physical presence/contact/appearance comments persisting after being asked to stop meet the harassment category on their own, regardless of whether the employee supplies the protected-characteristic/sexual-harassment framing themselves -- real disclosures describe behavior, not a legal category. Verified directly: the two previously-unreliable cases now trip 5/5 each; the existing `safety_floor_test.py` suite unchanged at 4/4; a new adjacent sanity case (annoying-but-not-appearance-related lingering) stays at 0/5, confirming the fix didn't overfire on ordinary coworker friction.
- **`intake-app`: added a safety-floor and diagnostic-label-avoidance test, the first automated test checking facilitator conversational correctness rather than timing or control wiring.** New `tests/safety_floor_test.py` hits `/api/message` directly over plain HTTP (no browser/mic needed) with scenarios adapted from `docs/pitch-strategy/14_WORKPLACE_SCENARIOS.md`, and asserts on the real `<mend_signal>` block's `floor_triggered`/`screen_result` fields -- a structured oracle instead of judging prose. Also scans every reply for the pop-psychology labels (gaslighting, narcissist, toxic) the system prompt explicitly bans, across both floor and non-floor cases. Real finding while building it: the first floor scenario's original wording ("comments on my appearance," no protected characteristic named) sat right at the floor's own ambiguous boundary and flip-flopped true/false run to run on identical scripted input -- confirmed directly by replaying it manually and seeing it trip on one run, not another. Sharpened to explicitly name unwanted sexual attention, which now trips reliably across repeated runs. Verified: 3 back-to-back full runs, 4/4 passed each time.
- **`intake-app`: added a dedicated regression test for the trailing-words-dropped push-to-talk bug** (fixed in the previous `612bf3b` commit, which only had the bug documented in `button_smoke_test.py`'s module docstring, not an actual check for it). New `check_ptt_trailing_words_not_dropped` plus a purpose-built fixture, `tests/fixtures/ptt_trailing_words.wav` (`say` + ffmpeg, "She's a peer, not my manager", ~2.0s, deliberately no silence tail so the file ends the instant speech ends). Getting the test to actually reproduce the real race took two wrong turns, both found live by actually running it against the real server rather than trusting the logic: a fixed-delay hold (hold for ~2.1s from mouse-down) undercounted real getUserMedia-setup latency and cut the fixture's own audio short before the phrase finished speaking at all (a test bug, not the app bug -- confirmed by seeing the transcript truncate at the *same* point regardless of which fixed delay was tried); fixed by releasing the button the instant Deepgram's own live interim transcript bubble shows the fixture's last word ("manager"), which guarantees full audio was sent and reproduces the actual release-at-last-word timing rather than guessing at it. Assertion only checks for the last word's presence, not an exact transcript match -- Deepgram transcribing "a peer" as "appear" (a real but unrelated STT-accuracy quirk, not this bug) would otherwise fail the test for the wrong reason. Also found and fixed a silent false-skip along the way: the check skips (not fails) when `DEEPGRAM_API_KEY` isn't configured, but was originally using the same skip path for the server's own rate-limit error response (`RATE_LIMIT_MAX`, tripped easily by running checks back-to-back) -- now distinguishes the two and reports the real reason. Verified directly: ran against the real server with `RATE_LIMIT_MAX` temporarily raised (this test, like the existing timing harness, needs that raised for repeated back-to-back runs) and confirmed the full phrase, including the trailing word, survives intact end to end.
- **New `about.html` (root-level, for testers) built and rebuilt across several rounds of live-reviewed feedback, landing on a confidential-mediator positioning ("Fix Coworker Friction Before It Becomes an HR File") instead of the original stats-heavy pitch.** Started as a visual polish pass on an existing cost-of-conflict stats page (dollar-value stat, profitability stat, "who this is for" personas), then rebuilt entirely around a full new 7-section copy deck the user supplied directly: hero badge + trust line, "Three Ways to Use Mend" cards, a two-column employee/HR value section ("Standard Sprint Maintenance, Not an Escalation"), a "Cost of the Usual Options" comparison table, a privacy-architecture grid, and a closing CTA. Every em-dash stripped to match the user's global no-em-dash writing-style rule (en-dashes in ranges like "3-5 hours" kept, per that same rule's own exception). Embeds the real `ThimbleAvatar` engine (`design_handoffs/thimble_avatar-new-mow/thimble-avatar.js`) as a live animated hero visual (cycles idle/listening/thinking/speaking) plus a small instance in the closing CTA -- like the existing pitch page, only renders correctly served over `http://`, not `file://`. Several real bugs caught via live review and fixed, not just styled over: a duplicate bottom CTA button; the hero avatar reading as an unmotivated floating graphic (fixed with a grounding "Confidential Session Active" status pill plus a speech-bubble/typing-dots visual, and by re-centering its glow gradient, which had been off-center enough to read as a lit 3D sphere rather than a flat brand mark); low-contrast row labels in the comparison/privacy tables; misaligned two-column section headers; and a real horizontal-overflow bug in the comparison/privacy grids and the CSS-grid card rows (flex/grid children defaulting to content-based `min-width:auto` instead of `0`, now fixed with explicit `min-width:0`/`flex:1` on the relevant children plus a `body{overflow-x:hidden}` safety net) -- confirmed fixed via a real Playwright `scrollWidth`-vs-`clientWidth` check at both 375px and 800px, not just eyeballed. Also confirmed directly by reading `intake-app/static/index.html`, not assumed: the "Three Ways to Use Mend" cards and every CTA on the page all route to the exact same live intake conversation -- there's no scenario-picker screen, Thimble steers verbally from what the tester actually says -- and the page copy now says so explicitly instead of implying a menu that doesn't exist.
- **`intake-app`: push-to-talk button now animates like an actual record button**, at explicit request ("have the button animate so it looks like its recording, so maybe its white with pink border and when you push and hold to talk it gets reversed"). Resting: white fill, pink (`#F3829A`) border and icon -- an unarmed "ready" look. Held: fully reversed (filled pink, white border/icon) plus a repeating outward pulse ring (`@keyframes ptt-record-pulse`), the same visual language a camera or voice-memo app's own record button uses. Noted directly in the CSS comment, not silently worked around: Pink measures ~2.2:1 contrast on white, under WCAG AA's 4.5:1 text floor -- the same reason an earlier fix in this log put the state pill on white/ink+pink-dot instead of white/pink text -- accepted here anyway since this is one decorative icon carrying its meaning through the `title` attribute, not body text, and the explicit ask was for pink on both states specifically. Verified: computed style confirms the reversed colors and `animation-name` apply correctly on press; re-ran the full fake-mic Playwright cycle (press mid-reply to interrupt, hold through a sentence, release) and it still completes correctly end to end -- a styling-only change, but checked anyway rather than assumed safe.
- **`intake-app`: push-to-talk follow-up iterations, plus a real server crash caught live while testing it -- `/api/voice` and `/api/voice/partial` had no error handling around `transcribe_file()`, so a too-short/empty clip (`av.error.EOFError` decoding it) reached the client as an unhandled 500/HTML page instead of the JSON error shape the frontend already expects and handles gracefully.** Reported live as `error: Unexpected token 'I', "Internal S"... is not valid JSON` -- the frontend trying to `JSON.parse()` Starlette's default HTML error page. Traced to a quick tap-and-release on the new push-to-talk button (testing whether it visually changed, not actually speaking), which records a near-zero-length clip; `faster-whisper`'s own `decode_audio()` can't even open that as a container, and nothing caught the exception before this session. Both endpoints now wrap the `transcribe_file()` call in `try`/`except` and fall through to their existing empty-transcript paths (`post_voice`: `{"error": "couldn't transcribe audio"}` at 400; `post_voice_partial`: just skip that one preview, per its own docstring's existing contract) instead of crashing. Verified directly: restarted the server (confirmed it actually picked up the change -- see the `--reload` lesson two entries below) and reproduced the exact scenario with 5 rapid fake-mic taps in push-to-talk mode; server log now shows clean `400`s, zero tracebacks, where it previously had an unhandled `ExceptionGroup`. Also, in the same session: the push-to-talk button moved from its own pill-shaped row into the header next to Voice/Text as a matching "Auto"/"Hold" segmented toggle (catching a real bug along the way -- it had reused the `.ui-tab` class, which a pre-existing delegated listener wires to `setUi(btn.dataset.ui)`, so clicking either new tab also called `setUi(undefined)` and hid both panels at once; fixed with a separate `.ptt-tab` class sharing the same CSS), then moved again into `.control-bar` itself as a 76px red circle between Mute and Captions with a waveform icon (not the same mic glyph Mute already uses), and finally given a visibly darker shade + glow ring on top of its scale-down while held -- the original scale-only press state was reported live as "doesn't change when you push the button," true: red-on-red at a 7% size difference doesn't read as "changed" at a glance.
- **`intake-app`: added push-to-talk as an alternative to hands-free live listening**, at the user's explicit request after the speed/reliability reports above ("I need reliability more than ease of use... press this button to speak and then when you're done speaking you release it"). A new mode toggle ("Switch to push-to-talk" / "Switch to hands-free") and a big hold-to-talk button sit in `.call-controls`, not the you-tile -- the you-tile is hidden outright on mobile (`<760px`), and this needs to work there too; verified on both viewport sizes. While `pttMode` is on, holding the button is the *only* thing that starts or ends a recording: no VAD timer, no Deepgram `speech_final`/`utterance_end` auto-stop, no passive always-on interrupt listener. Pressing it while Thimble is thinking or talking interrupts on the spot -- the button itself is the barge-in now, a deliberate action instead of a passive VAD guess (which closes the same echo false-trigger hole as the fix above, just by removing the always-on listener that could hear the echo at all, rather than tuning its threshold). Implemented additively: every call site that would otherwise auto-arm hands-free listening or the passive interrupt monitor got an individual `&& !pttMode` / `if (!pttMode)` guard rather than hands-free being ripped out, so switching back needs nothing special to re-enable and the existing, well-tested hands-free path is untouched when the toggle is off. Verified against the real pipeline, not just the UI: a Playwright run with a fake-mic WAV pressed the button while Thimble was still mid-greeting (interrupted correctly, straight to Listening), held through a real sentence, released -- finalized in ~0.15s (vs. hands-free's 2.5-6s VAD/endpointing wait, since there's no silence-guessing at all here) and got back a real, correctly-contextualized reply referencing what was actually said. A second run (quick tap, release before any real speech, then toggle back to hands-free mid-session) produced zero console errors. Not yet tested by the user themselves on real hardware -- committed, not pushed, pending that.
- **`intake-app`: found and fixed the real reason Deepgram turn-ending felt slow -- `speech_final` never actually fires, on any utterance, so every single turn was riding the much slower `utterance_end_ms` fallback instead of the fast `endpointing` path it was built to use.** Reported live as "if I give a short answer... it doesn't seem to know I've finished talking." Checked directly against the real API, frame by frame over the real websocket, for both a short reply ("We're peers.") and a long one (the usual Friday-with-my-manager line): `speech_final` stayed `false` in every message in both cases, meaning `utterance_end_ms` (written up in `server.py`'s own comment as a rare backstop) was actually the thing ending every turn, the whole time -- so real turn-end latency was gated on that number, not `endpointing`, for every conversation this app has ever had. Short replies just make the same fixed wait feel worse, since there's less talking beforehand to offset it against. Lowered `utterance_end_ms` 2500ms -> 1300ms (`server.py`'s `DEEPGRAM_URL`), the same real silence-after-speech tradeoff `VAD_SILENCE_MS` already went through and landed near (1100ms) -- comment updated to say plainly if this starts cutting people off mid-sentence, raise it back up, that's not evidence the approach is wrong. Verified directly: re-ran the same short-utterance diagnostic post-fix and the silence-to-turn-end gap dropped from ~3.94s to ~2.77s (close to the expected ~1.2s drop); a 3-run pass of the automated test harness above showed no regression (barge-in still firing 3/3, one plain-turn run showing the improvement directly at 3.85s vs the ~5.7s baseline). Caught along the way: the dev server running at the time had no `--reload` flag (started manually outside `.claude/launch.json`'s own config earlier in the session), so the first post-edit test silently ran against the OLD code and showed almost no improvement -- worth remembering next time a server-side change doesn't seem to take effect.
- **`intake-app`: hardened the barge-in interrupt monitor against Thimble hearing its own echo as a user interrupting**, reported live after testing a real conversation on desktop ("it either thought I had started talking in the middle of when it started talking and I had said nothing... my computer wasn't loud, there was no fan on"). Two real problems, not one: (1) `startInterruptMonitor()` was reusing `vadThreshold` from the listening phase's own calibration -- a threshold set against a silent room before Thimble ever spoke, which has nothing to do with how much of Thimble's own voice bleeds back into a brand-new mic stream once it's actually playing through the speakers. Fixed by calibrating fresh: the interrupt stream now samples its own ambient level for `INTERRUPT_CALIBRATION_MS` (400ms) right as it opens -- i.e. with Thimble's own audio already playing, the exact condition that needs telling apart from a real interrupt -- and sets its threshold above whatever echo actually showed up, same fallback-to-observed-ambient idea `checkVad`'s own fan-noise fix already used, just applied to this separate stream. (2) Both `getUserMedia()` calls (`static/index.html`) used bare `{audio: true}` -- now explicit `MIC_CONSTRAINTS` (`echoCancellation`/`noiseSuppression`/`autoGainControl` all `true`), removing any ambiguity about whether Chrome's default echo cancellation was actually engaging. `INTERRUPT_MIN_MS` also raised 300ms -> 450ms, same direction as both fixes. Verified against the automated test harness above (3/3 clean plain-turn cycles, 3/3 real barge-ins still firing correctly, ~3.5s mean into Talking vs. the previous 2.76s -- the expected, deliberate cost of the added calibration window) -- but the harness has no real acoustic echo to test against, so the actual fix for the reported bug still needs the user's own ears on real hardware, not yet confirmed fixed.
- **`intake-app`: built an automated headless-browser test harness for voice-conversation timing (listening->thinking->talking latency, barge-in), so this stops requiring a human to manually repeat "I had a problem on Friday..." for every timing check.** New `intake-app/tests/voice_timing_test.py` drives a real Chromium via Playwright using Chrome's `--use-fake-device-for-media-stream --use-file-for-fake-audio-capture=<wav>` flags, feeding real WAV samples into `getUserMedia()` so the browser's VAD, Deepgram's real server-side endpointing, and the real Claude+TTS reply pipeline all run for real -- state read straight off `#stateWord`'s own DOM text, the same thing a real user sees. Took most of the session to get reliable, for real reasons found along the way, not just fixture tuning:
  1. **A real server race**: `/api/reset` racing a still-in-flight threadpooled reply crashes `facilitator_reply`'s `conversation.pop()` with `IndexError` (`server.py:332`) -- worked around in the test with a settle delay between runs, but it's a genuine bug in the app itself (single-user prototype, no locking).
  2. **Headless Chromium backgrounds/throttles the page by default**, which silently zeroed the fake mic's audio during any idle wait (confirmed directly: all-zero PCM sent over `/ws/voice` during the ~30s wait for Thimble's own greeting, even with real speech sitting right at that timestamp in the WAV). Fixed by launching with `--disable-backgrounding-occluded-windows --disable-renderer-backgrounding --disable-background-timer-throttling`.
  3. **The real fix**: Chrome's fake-audio-capture device restarts the WAV file at byte 0 on every fresh `getUserMedia()` call (confirmed directly -- an earlier, wrong assumption that it ran on one continuous wall-clock timeline cost most of the debugging time). The interrupt-monitor arms its own fresh mic stream the instant `avatarState` becomes `'thinking'`, before any reply audio even exists -- so a fixture with speech near the start of the file was self-interrupting every single real reply within under a second, which is why plain-turn runs kept stalling with no visible error. Fixed by giving the plain-turn fixture (`turn1.wav`) a 12s silence lead (longer than any single-reply Talking phase measured, so the interrupt-monitor's own byte-0 reset lands on silence) and the barge-in fixture (`interrupt_longtail.wav`) a tuned 6.5s lead (clears typical Thinking latency, lands a couple seconds into real Talking instead of during Thinking or back on the opening line).
  4. **A bug in the test's own measurement**, not the app: "Thinking -> Talking" was matching Thimble's own GREETING's identical Thinking->Talking state pair (appears first in the timeline) instead of the real reply's -- fixed by scoping every latency search to start at the first real `'Listening'` entry.
  Verified with a clean 10-run batch each: plain-turn Listening->Thinking (real silence-wait + STT) 5.67s mean / 0.028s stdev (i.e. fixed overhead), Thinking->Talking (LLM + TTS) 2.96s mean / 0.665s stdev; barge-in correctly interrupted in 10/10 runs, 2.76s mean into Talking. Run with `intake-app/.venv/bin/python intake-app/tests/voice_timing_test.py --runs N` against the running dev server -- raise `RATE_LIMIT_MAX` in `.env` first for larger batches, since repeated localhost test traffic can trip the app's own rate limiter.
- **`demo-app`: sign-in removed for this test round, at the user's explicit request** ("remove the sign in feature thats not going to be used for the user test"). The org-access-code signup screen (`#signup`) and its gating JS (`loadIdentity`/`saveIdentity`/`clearIdentity`/`showHub`/`showSignup`/the init check) are gone; `#hub` ("Before you start") is now shown unconditionally, so testers land straight on it instead of entering `WILLOW-CREEK-9F2`-style codes first. CSS rules that were only reachable from the removed signup markup (`label`, `input[type=text/email]`, `.field`, `.error`, and the already-orphaned `.identity-bar`) were removed alongside it. `server.py`'s `/api/signup` and the other identity-gated routes (`/api/invite`, `/api/pair-invite`, etc.) are untouched — not linked from the UI, same as before. Verified live in the browser pane: hard reload of `http://localhost:8423` now shows "Before you start" immediately, no sign-up step.

## Where things stand

Core facilitator flow (intake → joint session → post-session) works end-to-end as local FastAPI prototypes, real API keys required. `demo-app`'s signup/invite flow is real (access code + crypto-shredded invites, both direct and peer-suggested), not a simulation. `joint-session-audio/` is a separate, newer prototype proving the real audio delay buffer, video opt-in, and now the private breakout-room mechanism over LiveKit — the audio transport still isn't merged into `joint-session-app`'s conversational logic. `manager-coaching-app/` is now unused -- its facilitator logic was merged into `intake-app`'s own system prompt as a second, ambiently-detected conversation path rather than staying a separate fifth prototype with its own mode button (see below); the app's code is left in place but nothing routes to it anymore. The brand (logo, Pink/Evergreen/Honey/Oat, Manrope) is now canonical everywhere. "Caucus" (mediation jargon, read as confusing) has been renamed to "side conversation" across the entire repo — system prompts, specs, code, and the pitch video. `intake-app`'s UI has been substantially redesigned to match a new high-fidelity design handoff (`design_handoff_mend_sessions/`, dropped by the user mid-session) — see below. The facilitator now has a working name, **Thimble**, used in UI copy/labels (not yet in the system prompts themselves — a separate decision).

GitHub (`github.com/generallymatthew/mend`) is **still suspended** (`git ls-remote origin HEAD` still 403s as of 2026-09-30) — blocks GitHub-dependent things (Pages hosting, GitHub-based CI) until resolved. Worked around for Render deploy purposes: repo now also pushes to `gitlab@gitlab.com:generallymatthew-group/mend.git` (`git remote -v` shows both `origin` = GitHub, `gitlab` = GitLab) — Render's Blueprint connects to the GitLab remote instead. Local commits + the Google Drive git-bundle backup (`.git/hooks/post-commit`) remain the durable copy regardless of either remote's state.

- **`intake-app`: survey deployed and verified live, then found a real durability gap and fixed it, then expanded from 4 shallow questions to a real research instrument -- all at the user's request in direct follow-up to the entry below**. Deployed the original survey to https://mend-mvp.onrender.com and verified it end-to-end there (page loads, the automatic done-signal redirect fires identically to local, a real submission returns 200) -- but verifying against the live site surfaced a real gap: `render.yaml` mounts no persistent disk, so `survey_responses.jsonl` alone gets wiped on every Render redeploy/restart, fine for local dev but silently data-lossy in production. Stopgap shipped immediately (also log each response to stdout, which survives in Render's own log retention independent of the container disk), then replaced properly per the user's choice: `get_survey_sheet()` now authenticates via a service-account `GOOGLE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON`/`GOOGLE_SHEET_ID` env-var pair and appends each response to a real Google Sheet (`gspread`, `google-auth` added to `requirements.txt`), with the local file/stdout log kept as the fallback for whenever Sheets access isn't configured or a single append fails -- a submission never gets lost either way. Full setup steps (enable the Sheets API, create a service account, share the Sheet, set the two env vars) documented in `README.md`'s own new section, deliberately not here or in chat, since the service-account JSON is a real write-access credential. Separately, reviewing the original 4-question survey (overall rating, fairness, technical issues, open comments) live prompted the user to call it "too basic" -- expanded to 13 questions covering trust/safety (felt safe being honest, trusted the privacy claim, whether Thimble seemed to take sides), whether it actually understood the situation and changed the tester's planned approach, a direct comparison against talking to an HR rep/manager/friend instead, an NPS-style recommend score, a before/after emotional-shift check, and two sharper open questions ("what stood out" / "what would build more trust") alongside the original technical-issues and general-comments fields. `static/survey.html`'s per-question boilerplate was generalized (`wireScale()`, a shared `data-radio` pattern) rather than copy-pasted ten times over; `server.py`'s validation is similarly table-driven (`SURVEY_RADIO_FIELDS`) instead of one hand-written if-check per field. Verified directly: every new field round-trips correctly through a real POST into `survey_responses.jsonl`, an invalid value on any field still returns a real 400 instead of silently coercing, and the required-overall-rating guard still blocks submission correctly with the longer form.
- **`intake-app`: added a real post-session feedback survey, auto-shown when a conversation ends, at the user's explicit request** ("i will need a survey to be taken after the session to get user feedback" / "i want this to show up when the session ends so they are taken automatically"). New `static/survey.html` (overall 1-5 rating, a Thimble-felt-fair/helpful question, a multi-select technical-issues checklist, and an open comments box), served at `GET /survey` and posting to new `POST /api/survey`, which appends validated responses to `survey_responses.jsonl` (gitignored) -- the one deliberate exception to this app's "nothing is recorded" principle, since a survey response is volunteered product feedback with no conversation content attached, not session data. A persistent "Leave feedback" link now sits in the header for anyone who wants to reach it manually. The automatic part: `handleSignal()` now also watches for either track's own end-of-conversation signal (`ready_for_joint_session` for employee-conflict, `conversation_structured` for manager-coaching), and once set, a new `checkSurveyRedirect()` (run every animation frame alongside the existing state-pill update) waits for `avatarState` to actually leave `BUSY_STATES` before showing a toast and redirecting 2.5s later -- deliberately not an immediate redirect on signal, so it can never cut off Thimble's own final reply mid-sentence or mid-audio, and cancels/re-arms cleanly if a new turn starts before the delay fires. Per explicit decision, this runs alongside the existing "Continue to joint session" banner rather than replacing it (that banner isn't live for testers yet, but swapping it out felt like a bigger call than asked for). Verified directly in-browser end to end: simulated the done-signal while `avatarState==='talking'` (redirect correctly held off, toast stayed hidden), then set `avatarState='idle'` (toast appeared, real redirect fired after the delay), submitted a real response through the actual form, and confirmed it landed correctly in `survey_responses.jsonl` on disk -- not just reasoned about from the diff.
- **`intake-app`: Thimble now scales up on wide screens, two more duplicate-text cleanups, and a real pause-during-transcription race fixed** -- the avatar was hard-locked at 200px regardless of viewport; added media-query breakpoints (260px at 1024px, 320px at 1440px) so it grows on desktop instead of looking small and lost on a wide window. Also found two more instances of the same duplicate-status-text pattern fixed repeatedly elsewhere in this log: the bottom-left status line's busy text ("facilitator/thimble is responding...") duplicated the big state pill's own "Thinking.../Talking..." label, and the desktop "You" tile's live-transcript copy duplicated what's already in the main caption -- both removed (the You tile now matches mobile, which already hides this). While testing pause around this, found a real bug in the pause control's own race coverage: pausing while a voice turn was still being transcribed (the brief window between finishing speaking and the reply starting) let `getFinalTranscript()`'s in-flight fetch -- which `pauseSession()`'s hard-abort has no `AbortController` wired up to actually cancel -- resolve anyway and silently revive the turn (`avatarState` -> thinking -> talking -> idle), landing back on "Listening..." instead of staying "Paused," since `liveMode` was already off by then so nothing re-armed listening on purpose either. Fixed by snapshotting `replyGen` before the transcript await and re-checking it after, same generation-guard idiom the existing barge-in interrupt path already uses -- dropping the stale result instead of continuing the turn if a pause bumped the generation in the meantime. Verified live in-browser (pause/resume from both mid-talk and mid-listening states correctly show "Paused"); the exact transcription-window race couldn't be replayed in this sandboxed browser (mic access blocked here), so it's unverified against a real mic -- worth a real-browser pause-right-after-speaking retest. Pushed to GitLab only, same GitHub-suspension workaround noted below.
- **`intake-app`'s Deepgram streaming STT is now live on https://mend-mvp.onrender.com, not just locally** -- `DEEPGRAM_API_KEY` is set in Render's dashboard (`GET /intake/api/voice/config` on the live site now returns `{"deepgram": true}`), closing the Open item below. Also found and fixed a real, unrelated deploy bug while getting there: Render's `OPENAI_API_KEY` had gone stale (401 "Incorrect API key provided"), which silently killed ALL voice output on the live site -- the code's own `synthesize_speech()` falls back to macOS `say` when OpenAI fails, but `say` doesn't exist on Render's Linux container, so every TTS call quietly returned `audio_url: null` with zero trace anywhere (the symptom reported live: "it stopped with the thinking and went to listening without the intro, I did not say anything"). Added real error logging to `synthesize_speech_openai()` (previously swallowed the exception entirely) specifically to diagnose this, which immediately surfaced the real 401 in Render's own logs; the user then rotated the key in Render's Environment tab and confirmed `/api/speak` calls started returning real `audio_url`s again. Separately confirmed GitLab push auto-deploy is now wired on Render (earlier in the session a push alone didn't trigger a new deploy, requiring a manual "Deploy latest commit" click each time; by the end of the session, pushes were triggering real deploys on their own -- whatever was misconfigured initially has since resolved, not something fixed by a specific change here).
- **`intake-app`: a full mobile/voice-UI polish pass, driven by live testing on a real phone against the deployed Render site** -- a long tail of small, each individually live-reported and live-verified fixes, not a redesign: (1) the "You" tile's position was iterated through several real placements (stage corner -> below the control buttons -> absolutely positioned with equal 24px right/bottom insets, ignoring the tile when centering the control buttons) before landing on the final one, and is hidden outright on mobile (`<760px`) rather than chasing a tight corner anchor there, since `.stage`'s content-driven height made any fixed offset unreliable on a tall narrow viewport; (2) captions/subtitles moved from an absolute bottom-of-stage position to normal flow directly under the state pill, with a real `splitCaptionParts()` rewrite so a long sentence displays as short, single-line pieces stepped by the actual playing clip's own `currentTime/duration` ratio (previously one whole sentence, however long, shown all at once -- reported live as "letting people read ahead" instead of feeling like real captions) -- also made the font smaller per direct request; (3) the state pill moved from the stage's top-left corner to directly under the avatar (grouped with it in a new `.avatar-stack` column), then given `position:relative; z-index:3` after the ripple circles (which are `position:absolute` and paint above plain static siblings regardless of DOM order) were found painting on top of it;
