(4) **the real lip-sync bug**: the mouth's amplitude-driven height update fought `.thimble-mouth`'s own CSS `transition:height .35s`, which eased toward each new per-frame target instead of snapping to it, reading as laggy no matter how responsive the underlying JS envelope was -- fixed by excluding `height` from that transition specifically while talking; separately widened the visible pixel range and switched to an attack/release envelope (fast open, slower close) since the raw range was too subtle to read as movement at all; (5) **Pause mid-reply now resumes the same audio from where it left off** instead of discarding it and jumping to listening -- `endReplyCycle()`'s abort/`replyGen` bump used to orphan the speech queue; pausing while `avatarState==='talking'` now just pauses `audioEl` and releases the barge-in mic, leaving the queue intact for `resumeSession()` to continue; (6) **a real mobile-only bug, not a UI nit**: sentence-streamed TTS means every sentence is its own `audioEl.play()` call, and mobile browsers enforce autoplay-blocking far more strictly than desktop -- once one sentence got blocked (no user gesture since Voice auto-starts on load), every remaining sentence in the queue was blocked too, racing silently through the whole reply with zero audio and landing on listening with no visible sign anything had gone wrong (the live-reported symptom this surfaced as, independently of the stale-OpenAI-key version of the same symptom above). Fixed with a real "Tap to continue" banner that waits for an explicit tap instead of guessing the content away, verified by monkey-patching `audioEl.play()` to reject once and confirming both that the banner appears and that tapping it actually resumes real playback; (7) two duplicate-text cleanups (the mic-error banner's own description vs. a shorter repeat of it in the small status line; the state pill already saying "Paused" vs. the status line also saying "paused") and two accessibility/overflow fixes (the state pill asked to go white-with-pink -- pink-on-white measures ~2.2:1 contrast, well under WCAG AA's 4.5:1 floor for 13px text, so it's white/ink text with a small pink accent dot instead; the header's privacy pill overflowing the screen on mobile, fixed by showing just "Private." there instead of the full sentence, not by wrapping the header). Every fix in this pass was verified against either a real phone (the user's own repeated live tests) or a direct in-browser check (computed geometry, sampled real audio/analyser values, a simulated autoplay-block) -- not assumed correct from reading the diff.
- **`intake-app`: built the Deepgram streaming-STT integration scoped in the previous entry below** -- the next real lever on voice latency, now implemented (not yet real-mic verified, see Open/next up). New `/ws/voice` WebSocket route in `server.py` relays the browser's raw PCM mic stream straight through to Deepgram's live API (`nova-2`, `interim_results`/`smart_format`/`endpointing=500`/`utterance_end_ms=1500`) and forwards transcript/utterance events back as JSON; a new `GET /api/voice/config` tells the frontend whether a `DEEPGRAM_API_KEY` is configured at all, so a tester without one gets the existing local-Whisper path completely unchanged -- additive, not a replacement, per the original scoping. Deliberately did NOT rip out the existing VAD timer/`maybeSendPartial`/`getFinalTranscript` pipeline, both per the original scope note ("local Whisper kept in place as a fallback") and because it's still needed as a genuine fallback if Deepgram's socket drops mid-turn: `static/index.html` still collects `pcmChunks` the whole time regardless of which path is active, and `onstop` only skips the local path when Deepgram actually produced non-empty text for that turn. Wired to reuse the *existing* `MediaRecorder`/`onstop`/`discardNextStop` machinery rather than building Deepgram its own parallel stop/pause/mute handling: Deepgram's own `speech_final`/`utterance_end` events just call `mediaRecorder.stop()`, the same call every existing control path (Pause, Mute, manual stop, `exitVoiceLive`) already makes -- so Pause/Mute/barge-in all keep working on the Deepgram path for free, without a second copy of each. `checkVad`'s silence-timer and `maybeSendPartial`'s periodic re-transcription are both skipped outright when Deepgram is enabled (Deepgram's own server-side endpointing replaces both jobs at once). Needed `websockets>=12` added to `requirements.txt` -- this app's `uvicorn` had zero WebSocket support installed before this (no `websockets` or `wsproto` in the venv at all), so the server-side route wouldn't have worked even before reaching Deepgram. Verified directly against the real server: `GET /api/voice/config` correctly reports `{"deepgram": false}` with no key set; a real WebSocket client connecting to `/ws/voice` with no key gets a clean `1008` close with an explanatory reason instead of hanging or crashing; `node --check` on the extracted frontend script confirms no syntax errors from the edits; a full real browser pass (Text view: sent a message, got a real streamed Claude reply, nothing broken) confirms the unrelated existing paths are unaffected. **Now verified with a real mic and a real Deepgram key**: the user added their own `DEEPGRAM_API_KEY` to `.env` and tested a real voice turn in their own browser against the local dev server (this sandboxed browser still can't do that part itself) -- reported back unprompted as "this was so much better," a clear improvement over the local-Whisper/VAD path. Real turn-taking feel (Deepgram's own endpointing ending a turn promptly and cleanly) is the one thing that genuinely needed a human ear and a real mic to confirm, and it's now confirmed working, not just structurally sound.
- **`intake-app`: raised `VAD_SILENCE_MS` back up to 1400ms (from 500ms) after live testing showed 500ms cutting the user off mid-sentence repeatedly, badly enough that the barge-in recovery built for exactly this case wasn't making up for it.** A turn getting cut at 500ms isn't a brief mid-thought pause worth talking over -- it's genuinely still-talking. Still well under the original 1800/2600ms. Also confirmed, via a real live-mic joint debugging session (user talking into their own browser while I watched server-side timing logs in real time), that the three latency fixes above are holding together in practice: zero 429s, zero TTS hangs, a full 10.3s turn captured without premature cutoff at the new threshold. The user's reported "7 secs after it started transcribing" matched almost exactly against the server log's own numbers for that turn: 1.4s VAD wait + 2.78s final tail-patch transcription + 2.26s Claude first token (cached) + ~1.1s first-sentence TTS. That breakdown is the real finding -- all four stages are strictly serial, so total latency is a straight sum, and Claude/TTS are both already near their real network-round-trip floor. The one piece still meaningfully above floor is local Whisper's per-call overhead on this machine's CPU (1.7-3.4s per call all session, regardless of clip length) -- not fixable in code, a hardware/architecture ceiling for CPU-only local Whisper on this specific laptop.
- **`intake-app`: researched real pricing and scoped a Deepgram streaming-STT integration as the next real lever on voice latency, at the user's request** ("if this is going to be the major interface for this app, I really think it needs to be quicker"). Cost is not a blocker: researched current rates (Deepgram, AssemblyAI, OpenAI Realtime) and at ~250 minutes of audio (100 users × 10 responses × ~15s avg, stated assumption), even the priciest realistic option runs under $5 for the whole test batch; full pipeline cost (STT + Claude + TTS) for 1,000 turns is roughly $10-14 total, about a penny per exchange. The real win isn't just swapping the transcription engine -- Deepgram's streaming API has built-in `endpointing`/`speech_final` detection (transcribes continuously, flags end-of-turn server-side) and ~150ms interim results, which would directly eliminate BOTH of the two serial stages found to be the biggest remaining cost above: the VAD-guess wait AND the local-Whisper tail-patch call. Scoped architecture: a new FastAPI WebSocket relay (`/ws/voice`) piping the browser's existing `micProcessor` PCM stream to a server-side Deepgram connection and transcript events back, replacing `/api/voice/partial`/`maybeSendPartial`/`getFinalTranscript`/`mergeTranscripts` and (likely) the client-side VAD timer entirely, with local Whisper kept in place as a fallback rather than deleted outright. Estimated at 2-4 hours of real work, not a tuning tweak -- touches the core capture pipeline. **Not yet built** -- scoped only, in this chat; implementation is the explicit next step, intentionally starting as a new chat.
- **`intake-app`: two more real bugs found in the same live joint-debugging session (watching server timing logs together while testing voice, right after the partial-transcription fix below) -- the rate limiter silently dropping a sentence's audio mid-reply, and a hung OpenAI TTS call eating 30 full seconds**:
  1. **`/api/audio/<file>` (serving an mp3 this process already generated, sitting on disk, zero marginal cost) was getting rate-limited** -- confirmed live: `GET /api/audio/....mp3 HTTP/1.1" 429 Too Many Requests` in the server log mid-reply. Same root shape as the earlier `/api/voice/partial` rate-limit bug: the exemption was an exact-match set, but `/api/audio/<file>` is a different dynamic path per file, so it never matched and got counted against the same budget as real paid Claude/TTS calls. Sentence-streamed TTS (landed earlier this session) made this far more likely to actually trigger, since one reply now fetches several audio files instead of one. Fixed with a prefix-based exemption (`RATE_LIMIT_EXEMPT_PREFIXES`) alongside the existing exact-match one. Verified directly: flooded `/api/audio/` with 25 requests (more than `RATE_LIMIT_MAX=20`) and confirmed zero 429s.
  2. **A single `/api/speak` call measured 31.00s** in the same session's timing log -- almost exactly the TTS httpx client's 30s timeout, meaning the OpenAI call hung for the full ceiling before silently falling back to macOS `say`. No real sentence-length call ever measured above ~3s elsewhere in the same log, so anything still running past a few seconds is hung, not just slow. Lowered the timeout to 8s -- same eventual `say` fallback on a genuine hang, but an 8s stall instead of a 30s one. Verified directly: confirmed the live client object's configured timeout is actually 8s post-reload, not just the source change.
- **`intake-app`: finalizing a voice turn no longer blocks on an in-flight partial transcription -- the last real latency bottleneck found in a live joint debugging session (watching server-side timing logs together while testing)**. After prompt caching and sentence-streamed TTS above, a live re-test still showed ~10s from end of talking to reply -- ruled out machine CPU contention first (a live `ps aux` check found `StorageManagement`/`ApplicationsStorageExtension` eating ~68% of a core, almost certainly the earlier cache-cleanup this session triggering a storage rescan; closing it and retrying did improve Claude's own first-token time, 3.78s -> 2.13s, but the gap was still there). The real remaining cause was already half-documented in `maybeSendPartial`'s own comment: every live partial-transcription call resends and re-transcribes the WHOLE clip so far, so cost grows with turn length -- and `getFinalTranscript` was awaiting whichever partial happened to still be in flight when the user stopped talking, meaning the single MOST expensive Whisper call of the entire turn (the one covering the most audio) sat directly in the critical path right when speed mattered most. Fixed by no longer awaiting it at all: `partialSentSamples` is already bumped to the in-flight call's own start point the instant it begins (before it's awaited), so `lastPartialText`/`partialSentSamples` at the moment of finalize already point at the previous *completed* partial and the correct tail cut point, regardless of whether another one happens to be running right now -- finalize just uses those directly and does its usual single bounded tail-patch call, the in-flight call left to finish on its own and its result simply never used. Verified directly: simulated a previous completed partial plus a deliberately slow (5s) in-flight one and confirmed finalize resolved in 1.27s using the previous partial's text, not the simulated 5s wait.
- **`intake-app`: prompt caching on the system prompt, and sentence-streamed TTS -- the two real causes of "Thimble's reply is much slower than typing into Claude directly," reported live after the VAD/barge-in work above still didn't close the gap**:
  1. **No Claude call anywhere in `server.py` used prompt caching** -- `system=SYSTEM_PROMPT` was a plain string on all three call sites (`facilitator_reply`, `stream_facilitator_reply`, `stream_message_reply`), so every single turn reprocessed the whole ~8k-token system prompt from scratch before generating a single token, no matter how many turns reused the identical text. Fixed by defining `SYSTEM_PROMPT_BLOCKS` once (a content-block list with a `cache_control: {"type": "ephemeral"}` breakpoint) and passing that everywhere instead. Verified directly, not assumed: a raw API call showed `cache_read_input_tokens=9588, cache_creation_input_tokens=0` on a cached turn, and a two-turn streaming timing test showed first-byte latency drop 2.53s -> 1.69s turn-to-turn.
  2. **TTS waited for the ENTIRE reply to finish generating, then synthesized the ENTIRE thing as one `/api/speak` call, before any audio played at all** -- for a ~60-word reply this meant 6-9s of silence before Thimble's voice started, even though the text itself streamed in under 2s; the actual complaint was never the VAD gap, it was this. Rewrote to synthesize and play sentence by sentence instead: `static/index.html`'s `extractCompleteSentences()` pulls each newly-complete sentence out of the growing streamed text as it arrives, `queueSentence()` fires that sentence's `/api/speak` call immediately and concurrently with the rest of the reply still generating, and a small ordered queue (`speechItems`/`maybeAdvancePlayback`/`playSpeechItem`/`advanceSpeechQueue`) plays them back-to-back off the single existing `audioEl` as each one's synthesis resolves -- audio now starts as soon as the FIRST sentence is ready, not the last one. No backend change needed; `/api/speak` was already stateless and safe to call concurrently. Replaced `speakReply`/`playReplyAudio`/`skipRequested`/`speakAbortController` everywhere (`sendText`, the voice `onstop` handler, `start()`'s opening greeting, `skipIntroBtn`, `pauseSession`, `interruptNow`, `endReplyCycle`) with the new queue, all gated by the same `replyGen` counter the barge-in monitor already used, so an interrupt/skip/pause mid-queue cleanly cancels whatever's left queued instead of racing it. Captions got simpler as a side effect -- each playing clip IS exactly one sentence now, so the old proportional-duration-within-one-long-clip estimate (`splitCaptionChunks`/`computeCaptionStarts`) is gone, deleted as dead code. Verified directly: a unit test of `extractCompleteSentences` against six incremental text deltas split sentences at the right boundaries and flushed the trailing fragment correctly; a real Text-view send (no mic needed) showed all 4 sentences of a real reply queued and synthesized concurrently, played back in the correct order, and the cycle ended cleanly (`avatarState` back to idle, audio paused). A raw timing test on this server: first complete sentence available ~2.0s into a reply, full reply done streaming ~2.7s -- under the old flow nothing could start synthesizing before that 2.7s mark; now the first sentence's TTS starts at ~2.0s.
- **`intake-app`: lowered `VAD_SILENCE_MS` from 2.6s toward real human turn-taking pace (1.8s -> 2.6s -> now 1.0s), and added a barge-in monitor as the safety net that makes the lower number safe to ship** -- real conversational turn-taking gaps average ~200ms (Stivers et al. 2009), so 2.6s made every reply feel like it was waiting the user out; but the 2.6s figure itself existed because a shorter window previously cut someone off mid-sentence on an ordinary reflective pause. Rather than re-litigate that tradeoff, added the other half: a second, independent mic stream (`startInterruptMonitor`/`interruptNow` in `static/index.html`) that opens only during the `thinking`/`synthesizing`/`talking` window after a turn ends -- not during the turn recorder's own recording, and not in Text view, which never arms it -- and watches for sustained speech (300ms, to ignore a click or a cough). If the VAD guessed wrong and ended the turn early, talking again cancels whatever Thimble is doing (aborts the in-flight `/api/message/stream` fetch via a new `AbortController` param, aborts any in-flight `/api/speak` TTS fetch, stops playback) and goes straight back to listening, instead of making the user wait out a reply to a thought they hadn't finished. A `replyGen` counter guards the races this creates (interrupt vs. the reply finishing normally vs. Pause vs. Skip all potentially racing to end the same reply cycle) -- whichever happens first bumps it, so the others' next check sees it's stale and backs off instead of double-handling the same turn (e.g. two `startRecordingTurnSafe()` calls landing on top of each other). Self-tested what doesn't need a real mic: reloaded and drove the Text view end-to-end (typed a message, got a streamed reply) to confirm the new code paths don't throw and that the interrupt monitor correctly no-ops outside live voice mode (no stray mic permission prompt). The VAD timing change itself and the actual barge-in trigger both need real-mic verification -- added below.
- **`intake-app`: VAD was ending a turn mid-sentence on a natural reflective pause**, reported live as "the second response was cut off in the middle of me talking." First ruled out a red herring: the server log showed two `/api/message/stream` calls back-to-back with no transcription work between them, which looked like a possible double-submit bug -- confirmed instead it was a real voice reply immediately followed by a typed message, not a duplicate. The real finding: that voice turn's own audio only reached ~3.9s before finalizing, well short of other turns in the same session (7-11s) -- `VAD_SILENCE_MS` (1.8s) was ending the turn during an ordinary pause while still composing a thought, plausible in a conversation about a workplace conflict. Raised to 2.6s. Verified directly: simulated a 2.2s mid-speech pause (longer than the old threshold, shorter than the new one) and confirmed the turn no longer ends there, then simulated a genuine 2.8s pause and confirmed it still correctly ends the turn.
- **`intake-app`: Pause's big avatar label still said "Listening..." the whole time paused**, reported live as "when I hit pause it's still listening but not reacting" -- same failure shape as the earlier mic-blocked-banner bug: `avatarState` was never actually changed by `pauseSession()`, and `STATE_WORDS` maps both `'listening'` and `'idle'` to the identical "Listening..." label, so the real paused state was visually indistinguishable from normal listening (only the small, easy-to-miss `statusEl` line actually said "paused"). Fixed by setting `avatarState = 'paused'` with its own `STATE_WORDS`/`CHAT_STATUS` entries -- the big label now actually says "Paused", and `animateAvatar`'s listening-animation check (keyed on the exact `'listening'`/`'idle'` strings) no longer matches it either, so the avatar itself stops doing the listening lean/wobble animation while paused.
- **`intake-app`: Thimble's reply now streams in instead of arriving all at once**, closing the real gap behind "transcription was fine but the thinking took 6sec" -- `/api/message` was a plain blocking Claude call; the UI waited for the entire ~5-6s reply to finish generating before showing anything. New `/api/message/stream` (server) reuses the same SSE pattern already proven for the Tavus video path (`stream_facilitator_reply`'s marker-withholding logic, so `<mend_signal>` never leaks into visible text), just shaped as `{"text": ...}` chunks for this app's own frontend instead of Tavus's OpenAI-chunk format. New `streamFacilitatorReply()` (frontend) reads the SSE body and updates a reply bubble's text in place as it grows, resolving to the same `{reply, signal}` shape the old JSON response had so both call sites (`sendText()` and the voice-memo path in `onstop`) barely changed. Tested directly, not just assumed correct, in response to a fair complaint about not verifying voice-dependent work myself: the server-side stream verified with a raw curl request (incremental chunks, signal arriving clean in the final event, no leakage); the frontend verified in a real browser with no mic needed at all, since this piece is pure text -- drove the actual Text view end-to-end (typed a message, clicked Send, watched the reply bubble fill in live, confirmed via network inspection it hit `/api/message/stream` not the old endpoint), then separately exercised the voice-path's own slightly different bubble-update callback directly (14 real streamed deltas, status line cleared on the first one, final bubble text matched the resolved reply exactly, both the transcript-panel and chat-mirror bubbles in sync, signal received) -- covering the one piece of this call site a real microphone would otherwise have been needed for.
- **`intake-app`: three real fixes from one live debugging stretch -- a rate-limit bug that silently dropped Thimble's reply, stale browser caching that caused multiple false "it's not working" moments, and reload-starts-a-new-session per explicit request**:
  1. **Rate limiter was counting free local Whisper calls against the same budget as paid Claude calls**, reported live as "over a minute thinking and still has not responded." Server log showed the real cause: `POST /api/message` came back `429 Too Many Requests` -- Thimble's reply was never even attempted. `/api/voice/partial` (local Whisper only, never a paid API, deliberately chatty by design -- fires every ~1-2s of new audio during live listening) was counted by the same `RATE_LIMIT_MAX=20`/60s middleware that exists, per its own comment, specifically to protect paid-API endpoints. A single longer voice turn's own legitimate partial traffic could burn the whole window and get the real reply rejected right after. Fixed by exempting `/api/voice/partial` from the limiter. Verified directly: flooded it 25 times in a row (no 429s), then confirmed `/api/message` still succeeded immediately after, unaffected.
  2. **No `Cache-Control` header on `/` or `/static/*` meant a browser could serve a stale cached copy of `index.html` on an ordinary reload without ever re-checking with the server** -- the likely explanation for at least two separate "the fix isn't working" false alarms this session (a partial-cost cap that looked broken, then a reload-resets-session change that looked like it did nothing), both resolved once a cache-busted or hard-refreshed load actually picked up the current file. During active development the page changes every few minutes, so no caching at all is now the explicit default (`Cache-Control: no-store` on both routes) rather than relying on browsers' own heuristic freshness guesses.
  3. **Reload now always starts a fresh session**, per explicit request ("I want when I hit reload to have a new session -- I think things get weird"): reload used to deliberately RESUME an in-progress conversation (built that way earlier this session, for a genuine use case) -- now every reload runs the same `resetAndStart()` path the Reset button already used. Verified live: seeded a real conversation via `/api/message`, reloaded (after confirming the cache-busted page actually picked up the change, see above), confirmed `/api/history` came back with only a fresh greeting, the prior turn gone.
- **`intake-app`: confirmed a reported "17 sec" delay was this dev machine's own CPU contention, not a code issue** -- came right after the partial-cost-cap fix below, raising doubt about whether that fix actually worked. Tested directly against the real server with curl (lighter than a browser, to avoid adding my own contention): a realistic 9s turn, simulating the real partial sequence, measured ~1.8s for the final transcription step + ~2.7s for Thimble's reply, ~4.5s total -- close to the original baseline, not 17s. The one genuinely slow call in the user's own session (18.93s to transcribe a 2.37s clip) lined up with `ps aux` showing Chrome and WindowServer both heavily loaded at that exact moment (113%+ combined on this 2-core machine) -- external contention, same class of issue as the Google Drive incidents earlier, not a defect in the cap or tail-patch logic. Also resolved a real false alarm along the way: right after restarting the server, logs still showed partials growing past the 10s cap (10.41s, 12.03s) -- turned out to be the user's browser tab running JS from before that fix had landed, not the fix failing; a hard refresh plus a server restart cleared it, and the cap was confirmed working correctly afterward (never exceeded 10s audio per partial call).
- **`intake-app`: fixed live partial transcription cost growing with turn length, reported live as "the delay is now 9 sec vs 3" on a longer turn**: each `/api/voice/partial` call was resending the WHOLE clip-so-far, every time -- cheap early in a turn, but each call re-transcribes everything before it too, so cost grows with turn length and the LAST partial of a long turn (the one `getFinalTranscript` now correctly awaits, see two entries below) ends up re-transcribing the entire turn right when speed matters most -- not a regression in that logic, the partial pipeline itself was quietly O(n) per call all along, just newly load-bearing. First fix attempt (windowing each call to only new-audio-since-last-partial + a small merge-context overlap, same shape as the finalize tail-patch) was wrong and caught live before shipping: a window that small starves Whisper of sentence context, word choice drifts between calls, and `mergeTranscripts`' exact-word matching can't find the true seam -- produced a transcript with large repeated/garbled chunks on a real 18s test clip. Reverted that. Real fix: once a turn's total audio passes `PARTIAL_MAX_TOTAL_SEC` (10s), simply stop resending live partials -- the caption freezes, but the already-proven-correct tail-patch at finalize still transcribes whatever's left in one clean pass once the turn actually ends. Verified directly against the real server with the same 18s real speech clip: partial calls now stay bounded (~1.0-1.5s each, not growing), only 5 calls fired before hitting the cap (not one per 2s of the whole turn), and the final merged transcript matched a real one-shot ground-truth transcription of the whole clip exactly, word for word.
- **`intake-app`: fixed VAD never detecting silence under constant ambient noise (reported live: "it keeps listening after I've stopped talking, I don't know if it can hear my fan")** -- the real cause, and also the likely true explanation for the earlier 290s runaway clip (not confirmed as the cause then, but the same failure shape): calibration's fallback for "no genuinely-quiet sample ever appeared in the 400ms window" was to leave `vadThreshold` at the fixed 0.06 floor, on the assumption it might be the user talking rather than loud ambient noise. With a laptop fan sitting steadily above 0.06, that guess was wrong every time -- the threshold stayed below the fan's own level, so the fan read as nonstop speech and the turn could never detect real silence to end on. Now tracks the loudest level seen during calibration too, and when nothing quiet ever shows up, raises the threshold above that observed ambient level instead of leaving it stuck below it. Verified directly: simulated constant 0.08 "fan" noise through calibration (threshold correctly raised to 0.114), confirmed real "speech" at 0.25 still read as speaking, and confirmed the turn correctly ended once simulated speech stopped and only the fan noise remained -- the exact failure mode, fixed. Also added a hard `VAD_MAX_TURN_MS` (60s) backstop independent of VAD entirely, so no future cause of this same failure shape can ever again produce a multi-minute runaway recording that starves the whole server's CPU (verified directly: fed continuous loud "speech" well past 60s, turn force-ended on the cap). Known trade-off, explicit: someone who starts talking immediately in a genuinely quiet room could now get a threshold set near their own speaking volume, risking an early mid-word cutoff -- judged a cheaper failure than a recording that never stops.
- **`intake-app`: fast-path transcription experiment (below) tried live and reverted** -- reported back as "the speed was excellent but it missed the last sentence or two," confirming the real cost the experiment existed to measure was bigger than the speed was worth. Restored the tail-patch `getFinalTranscript`/`mergeTranscripts` implementation (the one two entries below this), kept the new Pause button. Net result of the experiment: tail-patch stays as the real implementation going forward, fast-path's code is still in git history if ever worth revisiting.
- **`intake-app`: added a real "Pause" control, and swapped the tail-patch transcription for a simpler fast-path to test its accuracy cost**: two real, live-reported gaps. (1) Mute was the only way to stop a live session mid-turn, but Mute deliberately keeps the mic running and just suppresses submission -- explicitly not what was wanted ("not mute... stop listening and stop anything, not just put the microphone on mute"). Likely the real explanation for the earlier 290-second runaway clip: if a turn was muted thinking it paused the session, the mic kept recording the whole time regardless. New Pause button is a real hard stop: releases the mic (`mediaRecorder.stop()`), discards whatever was mid-recording without submitting it (`discardNextStop`, checked at the very top of `onstop`), pauses Thimble's own reply audio in place, and suppresses VAD/partial sending while paused; Resume re-arms listening cleanly. Verified live in the browser: clicking Pause set `sessionPaused`/`liveMode` correctly and showed "paused"; clicking Resume cleared it and attempted to re-listen (blocked only by this sandbox having no real mic, same graceful failure path as everywhere else). (2) The tail-patch transcription (two entries above) still cost a network round trip (tail transcribe + merge) even though 95% of the turn was already sitting in `lastPartialText` -- reported live as still an 8s wait despite that. Replaced with a deliberately simpler fast-path: `getFinalTranscript()` now just reuses `lastPartialText` directly, stripped of its trailing Whisper "..." artifact, with zero extra network calls -- at the real cost of dropping whatever was said after the last partial fired (up to ~1s of audio, never transcribed at all). This is a live experiment, not a settled decision: trading some accuracy at the very end of each turn for an instant finalize step, to see in practice how much it actually costs before deciding whether to keep it over the correctness-first tail-patch version (recoverable from git history if the accuracy loss turns out to matter).
- **`intake-app`: fixed a real regression in the tail-patch transcription feature above, found live within minutes of shipping it**: reported as "transcribed correctly, then jumped into listening mode without me speaking" and separately "took 18 seconds, but 95% was already transcribed live." Root cause, confirmed via the real server log: under CPU contention (Google Drive's post-commit backup synced again, right after the tail-patch commit), live partials fell behind and were still in-flight when the user stopped talking. `getFinalTranscript()`'s fallback rule for "a partial is still in-flight" was to discard it and start a brand-new full-clip re-transcription -- the worst possible move under contention, since it throws away real progress and doubles the Whisper work on an already-overloaded CPU, which is what actually produced the 18s wait (the "jumped to listening without speaking" symptom was a separate, correct side effect: TTS got 429 rate-limited by the same contention window, so the existing "no audio to wait for" fallback correctly resumed listening with nothing to say). Fixed by tracking the in-flight partial's own request as a promise (`partialInFlightPromise`) that `getFinalTranscript()` now awaits and reuses instead of discarding -- same request, no redundant second pass. Verified directly against the real running server, not just reasoned about: simulated the exact race (called `getFinalTranscript()` while a real partial request to the real server was still pending) using a real synthesized speech clip served same-origin from `static/`, and got back the exact correct transcript via the awaited in-flight result, not the fallback path.

- **Root-caused why prompt-only edits kept requiring a manual server restart all session, not just once**: not a directory-scope issue as first diagnosed -- `uvicorn --reload` only watches `*.py` files by default, full stop, regardless of which directory it's scoped to; every `.md` system-prompt edit was silently invisible to the live server until an explicit restart, no matter where it was launched from. Added `--reload-include '*.md'` to `.claude/launch.json`'s `mend-intake` entry so this stops recurring.
- **That same fix immediately caused a real regression, reported live as "transcription taking over 30sec" for a 3-sentence clip**: `--reload-include '*.md'` matches *every* markdown file in the watched tree, not just the system prompt files -- including `STATUS.md`, which gets edited after nearly every fix in this log. A routine `STATUS.md` update mid-session triggered a full server restart (`WatchFiles detected changes in 'STATUS.md'. Reloading...` in the log) while a real `/api/voice` request may have been in flight, which would hang until a long network timeout rather than fail fast -- fully explaining a 30+ second stall on a 3-sentence clip, nothing to do with Whisper itself. Narrowed the pattern to `facilitator-*-system-prompt.md` specifically, so only the actual prompt files trigger a reload and routine STATUS/ROADMAP edits no longer yank the dev server out from under an active session.
- **Still reported slow once more after the restart-pattern fix above, with no obvious cause left in the logs** (clean request chain, no mid-flight restart this time) -- rather than keep diagnosing from subjective wait-time descriptions, added real timing instrumentation to `transcribe_file()`: logs model load time (and whether the model was already warm), actual transcription time, and the audio clip's own duration, every call. Not yet resolved -- waiting on the next real test to read the actual numbers off the server log instead of guessing. If `transcribe_s` turns out large relative to `audio_duration_s`, that's a genuine hardware limit on this old dual-core Intel Mac with no GPU, not a bug; if it's small, something else is still eating the time.
- **`intake-app`: resumed sessions (reload, or a second tab) armed `liveMode` but never actually grabbed the mic, reported live as "it just seemed like it was working... when I talked it could not hear me"**: the session-resume path (added earlier this session to fix "reload shows nothing") replayed history and called `armLiveMode()`, but `armLiveMode()` only arms the `liveMode` flag/UI -- the real `startRecordingTurn()` call is deliberately deferred to whichever of three TTS-related completion events fires first (audio ending, TTS unavailable, autoplay blocked), per the earlier mic-defer fix. None of those three ever fire on a resume, since there's no fresh reply to speak -- so the mic was simply never requested, while the UI confidently said "Listening..." the whole time. Fixed by explicitly calling `startRecordingTurnSafe()` in the resume branch itself, where there's nothing to wait for. Also fixed a second, compounding issue found at the same time: the replayed history was being written into the transcript panel while that panel was still hidden (collapsed by default in Voice view) -- invisible either way. Now auto-opens it when there's history to show. Verified live with two real tabs against the same server: tab 2's resume now shows the transcript panel open with all 3 prior turns visible, and a real mic-grab attempt (surfaced correctly as the "Microphone blocked" banner in this mic-less environment) instead of silently staying idle.
- **`intake-app`: finally fixed the "transcription takes so long" complaint that had resurfaced several times this session** -- the real cause, flagged early on but not actually finished until now: `/api/voice` did real transcription *and* the Claude reply in one request, with the frontend's status line and avatar label both stuck on "transcribing..."/`'thinking'` for the whole combined duration -- real transcription is a couple seconds on this hardware, but the Claude call after it can run much longer, silently mislabeled as part of "transcribing." Split into two real round trips, mirroring the TTS split already done elsewhere: `/api/voice` now only transcribes and returns, and the frontend POSTs the transcript to the existing `/api/message` endpoint (same path typed text already takes) to get the actual reply. Also added a distinct `transcribing` avatar state (own label, "Transcribing…") separate from `thinking`, same pattern as the earlier `synthesizing` split, so the big avatar pill is accurate too, not just the easy-to-miss status line. `watchfiles` also had to be installed into `intake-app/.venv` for `--reload-include` to do anything at all (uvicorn silently ignores it without that package) -- added to `requirements.txt`.
- **`intake-app`: captions centered and reply length brought back under control**, both reported live. Captions: `.caption-stack`'s asymmetric left/right bounds (left margin for the stage, right margin reserved for the "You" tile) meant the text itself was never actually centered within that box, just left-aligned inside it -- added `max-width` + `margin:0 auto` + `text-align:center` to match the original design handoff's own spec. Reply length: the opening greeting alone was measured at a real ~700 characters / 57 seconds of TTS earlier this session -- totally unworkable for a live back-and-forth. Root cause was the prompt asking for the opening's three informational pieces (welcome, confidentiality, the open question) to each be stated "in your own words, briefly" as separate elaborated bullets, which an LLM reliably expands into several sentences each. Added an explicit shared tone rule ("short turns -- a real person's side of a conversation is a sentence or two... a turn that would take a human more than about 15-20 seconds to say out loud is too long") and rewrote the opening-line instruction to give the literal target-length example instead of bullets to elaborate on. Verified directly: a fresh opening-line call now measures 409 characters, matching the compact template almost verbatim -- not yet re-verified against a full back-and-forth for later-turn length, since mid-conversation replies weren't the one directly measured.
- **`intake-app`: mic analysis silently never worked after the mic-defer fix, found live ("the listening did not react to me" -- dot never lit up even while talking)**: `startMicAnalysis()` creates its own `AudioContext` for the VAD/level meter, separate from the TTS playback one -- and unlike that one, never resumed it if the browser started it suspended. A fresh `AudioContext` starts suspended when there's been no user gesture yet, which is now the normal case here (live mode starts listening automatically once the greeting ends, no click required) -- a suspended context never actually processes audio, so the analyser read silence forever: VAD level stuck at 0, the mic indicator dot never lit, and a real turn could never end no matter how long or loudly someone spoke. `getUserMedia` had genuinely succeeded the whole time, making this look like a mic/calibration problem rather than the actual bug. Fixed with the same `if (state === 'suspended') resume()` pattern the other `AudioContext` already had.
- **`intake-app`: reload-after-first-message looked exactly like a broken app, reported live more than once ("no intro, not reacting")**: `conversation` lives server-side and survives a reload; the transcript panel and chat view are pure client-side DOM state and don't, and `/api/start` correctly no-ops once `conversation` is non-empty (by design -- a reload mid-session should resume, not restart). The combination meant every reload past the first exchange showed a blank, silent screen with no indication a session was already in progress. Added `GET /api/history` (strips the synthetic kickoff turn and each reply's `<mend_signal>` block, returns only what the user already saw) and wired the frontend's `start()` to replay it into both views whenever `/api/start` returns `reply: null`. Verified live: sent a real message, reloaded without resetting, and the full prior exchange (greeting, the sent message, the reply) reappeared correctly instead of silence.
- **`intake-app`: added a "Skip" button** (shown whenever Thimble is thinking/synthesizing/talking, mainly for testing -- a reply's TTS, including the opening greeting, can run past 30s), with a real bug found and fixed while building it: clicking Skip while still waiting on the reply *text* (before the TTS stage even starts) didn't actually stop anything, because the abort-flag was being reset at the top of `speakReply()` itself -- the exact function a pre-text-arrival skip needs to suppress once it's eventually called from the original, un-aborted `/api/start`/`/api/message` chain. Moved the flag's reset to each new-turn's own `avatarState = 'thinking'` point instead, and added an early-exit check at the very top of `speakReply()`. Verified live: clicking Skip the instant the button appears (text still in flight) now leaves `avatarState` stable at `idle` through the rest of that turn, with the text reply still arriving and displaying normally -- no reversion to `synthesizing`/`talking` after the fact.
- **`intake-app`: video-window background color fix**, reported live via an annotated screenshot -- the stage's own lighter oat tone (`#FBF8F3`) stopped at the stage's rounded box, with the area behind the control bar (mic/captions/menu buttons) showing the surrounding page's slightly darker oat (`--paper`) instead, a subtle but visible seam. Fixed by giving both `.call-main` and `.stage` the same new `--stage-bg` token, so the lighter shade now fills the whole call window edge to edge, button row included, instead of the first fix attempt which accidentally went the other direction (matched the stage down to the darker page color rather than extending the lighter one outward -- caught and corrected same-session).
- **`intake-app` + `manager-coaching-app` merged into one ambient-branching facilitator, a real product/architecture decision made collaboratively with the user, not a unilateral call**: the three mode-card buttons (Talk it through / Rehearse / For managers) are gone from the frontend entirely -- which of two tracks applies (an employee's own conflict vs. a manager preparing for a hard conversation with a direct report) is now detected by the facilitator from the conversation itself, asking directly only when genuinely ambiguous, per the user's explicit design call ("remove them all and let the ai handle this"). `facilitator-manager-coaching-system-prompt.md`'s entire content (four phases, its own mandatory floor, hard boundaries) is now folded into `facilitator-intake-system-prompt.md` as a second path alongside the original employee-conflict flow, sharing one tone section, one opening line, one signal schema (`track` field added); the old file is kept, marked superseded, for history. Real design tension resolved along the way, not glossed over: the manager-coaching stance ("not simply on the manager's side, push back if the plan is unfair") and the employee-conflict stance ("advocate for their interests") read as opposites at first, but the user pushed back correctly that real advocacy already means telling someone when their own planned approach will backfire or land unfairly -- both paths now push back on a bad *plan* equally, while staying neutral on who was right in whatever already happened. `intake-app/server.py`'s `/api/start` and `session_mode` global (the old talk/practice branching) are gone; a single track-neutral kickoff line is used instead. Two real bugs found and fixed while verifying this live: (1) the facilitator initially leaked its own internal "Track A"/"Track B" bookkeeping terms into replies ("This sounds like Track B...") -- traced to the labels appearing as salient capitalized section headers throughout the prompt, worse once an instruction literally quoted the banned phrase as a negative example (a known LLM priming pitfall); fixed by purging the literal strings everywhere except where they're genuinely describing the mechanism to the model, and rewriting the no-leak rule as a positive instruction instead of a "don't say X" quoting X. (2) `intake-app`'s dev server, running from inside its own subdirectory rather than the repo root, had `--reload` watching too narrow a directory to ever see edits to the system prompt file (which lives one level up) -- every prompt edit this session was silently invisible to the live server until an explicit restart from the repo root; `.claude/launch.json`'s own `mend-intake` entry already does this correctly, only the user's manually-started terminal copy was affected.
- **`intake-app`'s "Thinking..." label was reused for two different things, now split**: reported live ("transcription taking so long" turned out to be "the label says transcribing/thinking the whole time, including the part that isn't"). The TTS round trip (`speakReply()`, a separate fetch *after* the reply text has already arrived) was reusing `avatarState = 'thinking'`'s look and label, making "Thimble is composing a reply" and "the reply is done, audio is being synthesized" visually indistinguishable. Added a new `synthesizing` state (identical visual -- the three-dot mouth -- but its own label, "Getting ready to talk...") and wired it into every place that previously special-cased `'thinking'` for this moment (the stuck-state recovery paths in `playReplyAudio`'s `!url` and autoplay-blocked branches, the chat-view typing indicator). Verified live: polling `avatarState` through a real reply showed a clean `thinking` (while `/api/start` is in flight) → `synthesizing` (while `/api/speak` is in flight, ~3s on this machine, matching the user's own report) → `talking` (once audio actually starts) sequence, each with its own accurate label.
- **Fixed `intake-app` recording over its own intro, reported live right after the mic-blocked server-wedge fix below was deployed**: the earlier race-condition fix (await `start()` before entering live mode) stopped the UI from flashing "Listening..." before Thimble had said anything, but `enterVoiceLive()` still grabbed the mic the moment the *text* arrived -- not once Thimble had actually finished *speaking* it. Since TTS synthesis + playback is a second, slower round trip, this meant the mic would start (and, on a short clip, often stop again almost immediately) while the greeting's audio was still playing, reported live as "it turned on the mic and then turned it off, then started the intro... it says it's transcribing while it's talking, and the avatar mouth was in the thinking state not the talking state" -- a stray early recording's own `onstop` handler was setting `avatarState='thinking'` and firing a real `/api/voice` transcription request, both racing against and periodically clobbering the intro's own `avatarState='talking'`/caption state. Split `enterVoiceLive()`'s old behavior in two: a new `armLiveMode()` only sets the `liveMode` flag and live UI (timer, etc.) without touching the mic, and the actual `startRecordingTurn()` call is deferred to whichever already-existing completion path fires first -- the reply's TTS audio finishing (`'ended'`), TTS being unavailable (`playReplyAudio`'s `!url` branch, which previously didn't resume listening at all -- a second real gap, now fixed), or autoplay being blocked (the existing catch handler). `enterVoiceLive()` itself is unchanged and still used for the no-pending-reply cases (clicking the Voice tab, unmuting). Verified directly against a real ~57s intro clip: `avatarState` held at `'talking'` and `mediaRecorder` stayed `null` for the clip's entire duration, with the first mic attempt (and, in this mic-less environment, the "Microphone blocked" banner) only firing once `audioEl.ended` actually became true.
- **Real, more serious bug found right after the race-condition fix below, reported live when the user's own reload still showed no intro**: `intake-app`'s `facilitator_reply()` (the Claude call), Whisper transcription, and TTS synthesis were all synchronous, blocking calls made directly inside `async def` route handlers, with no thread-pool offload. uvicorn runs a single-threaded asyncio event loop by default -- a blocking call made straight from an `async def` handler freezes that *entire* event loop for its full duration, not just the one request waiting on it. Confirmed live and directly: one in-flight `/api/start` call (an earlier verification request of mine, still running) made a totally unrelated plain `GET /` take 9s instead of its normal <0.2s, and a second `/api/start` call queued up behind it for 60+ seconds with zero response. This is almost certainly why the user's own reload "still just showed Listening" after the sequencing fix below -- the server itself was wedged by prior traffic, independent of anything in the frontend. Fixed: added `transcribe_file()` (the previously-inline whisper call, now a standalone function) and wrapped every blocking call site (`facilitator_reply()` in `/api/start`, `/api/message`, `/api/voice`; `transcribe_file()` in `/api/voice` and `/api/voice/partial`; `synthesize_speech()` in `/api/speak`) in `fastapi.concurrency.run_in_threadpool`. Verified directly: with a real `/api/start` call in flight (~8-12s against the real Claude API), a concurrent `GET /` now returns in ~70ms instead of queuing behind it. The Tavus video-avatar path's own streaming equivalent (`stream_facilitator_reply`) was left as-is -- same class of concern in principle, but out of scope for this report (video mode isn't what the user hit, and Starlette's `StreamingResponse` already iterates a sync generator via its own threadpool wrapper, unlike the plain blocking calls here).
- **Fixed a race condition in `intake-app` that made the intro feel like it was just waiting on the user, reported live right after the mic-banner fix below**: on page load, fetching Thimble's opening line (`start()`) and starting to listen to the mic (`enterVoiceLive()`) used to fire at the same time, not sequenced. Grabbing the mic resolves far faster than a Claude round trip, so the UI flipped to "Listening..." within milliseconds -- well before Thimble had said anything -- making the whole wait for the actual intro look like the app doing nothing, just sitting there expecting the user to speak first. `resetAndStart()` elsewhere in the same file already sequenced this correctly (`await start()` then `enterVoiceLive()`); the initial page-load call just never got the same treatment. Fixed to match. Verified directly against a fresh (post-`/api/reset`) local session by polling `avatarState`/`liveMode` every 300ms through a real load: `avatarState` now holds at `'thinking'` for the entire wait (previously would've flipped to `'listening'` almost immediately) and `liveMode` only goes true, and the mic banner only fires, after the intro reply has actually landed and begun speaking.
- **Real root cause found for "avatar stuck on Listening" on the deployed `intake-app`, reported live by the user testing https://mend-mvp.onrender.com**: the autoplay fix below turned out not to be it -- the user confirmed the label never moved past "Listening..." at all, meaning `mediaRecorder.onstop` (and thus `avatarState = 'thinking'`) never fired. Root cause: `getUserMedia` was rejecting (mic permission denied/blocked on their browser), caught in `enterVoiceLive()`'s catch block, which only ever wrote a tiny 11px status line -- and critically, `avatarState` stayed at its initial `'idle'`, which `STATE_WORDS` maps to the exact same "Listening..." text as the real listening state. A blocked mic was therefore *indistinguishable* from Thimble just quietly waiting; the only trace was that easy-to-miss status line (made worse by the font bug below -- it was rendering in a fallback generic monospace, not even looking like intentional UI). Reproduced and confirmed directly: loaded the local app in a mic-blocked browser context and read `avatarState`/`liveMode`/`status` off the page -- `avatarState: 'idle'`, `liveMode: false`, `status: "couldn't access the microphone: Permission denied"`, same signature on both the local app and the live Render site. Fixed with a real, unmissable error state: a centered "Microphone blocked" banner (with a "Try again" button that re-invokes `enterVoiceLive()`) now shows on both the initial-connect failure path and the mid-conversation "lost microphone access" path, instead of silently falling back to idle's lookalike label. The underlying *why* permission was denied is still unconfirmed for the user's specific browser/OS -- this fix makes the failure visible and retriable, it doesn't explain why the permission prompt was declined or never shown in the first place.
- **`intake-app` was also missing the JetBrains Mono import** (same bug class as `demo-app`/`post-session-app` below): five places referenced `'IBM Plex Mono'` -- a font never loaded anywhere in this app either -- so labels, the live timer, and the mic/status line were all silently rendering in a fallback generic monospace. Renamed to `'JetBrains Mono'` throughout and added the actual Google Fonts import, matching the handoff and the other two apps' fix.
- **`demo-app` (sign-up/hub) and `post-session-app` restyled to match `design_handoff_mend_sessions/`**: these two were still on an earlier, partial brand pass (missing the JetBrains Mono import entirely — labels were silently falling back to the system monospace font — plus no Honey accent, square corners, bordered-box buttons instead of the handoff's pill shapes). Both now use the full brand kit token set (Pink mark, Evergreen ink/buttons, Honey for highlights like step numbers, Oat surfaces), the handoff's radii (18px cards, 999px pills/inputs) and bubble treatment (4px corner on the speaker's own side), and a proper header (mark + "mend" wordmark + the "Private. Nothing is recorded." pill) instead of a plain text eyebrow. `demo-app` had no `.claude/launch.json` entry; added `mend-demo-app` (port 8423, matches `run_all.sh`) so it can be previewed the same way as the other four. Verified live in-browser: sign-up screen, the 5-step hub, and a real post-session exchange (facilitator + employee bubbles) all render correctly.
- **A second, real but distinct bug in `intake-app`'s Voice view, found first and initially mistaken for the user's actual report (see the "stuck on Listening" entry above, which was the real cause)**: Voice mode auto-starts listening on page load with no user gesture (`if (ui === 'voice') enterVoiceLive();` at init), so Thimble's very first reply tries to autoplay TTS audio with no prior gesture to ride on, which browsers block — `audioEl.play().catch(() => {})` swallowed that silently, and since `avatarState` only leaves `'thinking'` via the audio element's `play` event (which never fires if play was blocked), the avatar would freeze on "Thinking" even though reply text had already arrived. Still a real gap, still fixed (in `playReplyAudio()`'s catch handler: falls back to `avatarState = 'idle'` and resumes the listening loop, same as the normal `'ended'` recovery) — just not what the user actually hit, which turned out to be upstream of this (mic permission denial, never even reaching a facilitator reply). Not independently re-verified against a real mic/speaker in this environment (sandboxed browser blocks `getUserMedia` outright) — worth a real-browser retest alongside the mic-banner fix above.
- **Render deploy is live and fully working at https://mend-mvp.onrender.com**, verified with a real browser against the real deployed site (signup with the real `ORG_ACCESS_CODE` → hub → `/intake/` → a real, on-tone facilitator reply from the real Anthropic key). Three real deploy bugs found and fixed to get there, none related to the GitHub suspension despite that being the first suspicion: (1) Cloudsmith's Caddy apt repo has an expired OpenPGP signing subkey, failing signature verification on Debian trixie (`python:3.11-slim`'s current base) — switched to fetching a plain Caddy release binary instead of apt or copying from the official `caddy` image; (2) the official `caddy` image's binary carries a `cap_net_bind_service` Linux file capability that Render's sandboxed runtime rejects at exec time ("Operation not permitted") when copied in via a multi-stage `COPY` — the plain release tarball has no such capability; (3) `deploy/start.sh` was redirecting every subprocess's (demo-app, intake-app, Caddy) output to log files inside the ephemeral container, which Render's dashboard never sees — hid the actual cause of the first two bugs. Fixed by streaming everything to the container's own stdout, tagged per-process. Also added the repo's own GitLab mirror (`git@gitlab.com:generallymatthew-group/mend.git`, remote name `gitlab`) since GitHub's suspension blocks Render's Blueprint from connecting to the original repo.
- **`demo-app` → `intake-app` handoff verified end-to-end through the actual `deploy/render.Caddyfile` routing**, not just read: ran both apps' real `.venv` servers plus a real local `caddy run --config deploy/render.Caddyfile` on `:8080`, drove a real browser through sign-up (real `ORG_ACCESS_CODE` check) → hub → "Open intake" → a real facilitator reply from the real Anthropic key, all through the single-port proxy exactly as Render will route it. Confirmed along the way: `demo-app`'s `/intake/` link 404s when hit directly on its own port (8423) — expected, documented behavior, and exactly why the Render deploy needs the Caddy front door rather than exposing each app's own port.
- **Render deploy scaffolding for `demo-app` + `intake-app`, not yet deployed**: new `render.yaml`, `deploy/Dockerfile`, `deploy/start.sh`, `deploy/render.Caddyfile` — one container running both apps unmodified behind Caddy, same path-prefix scheme as local `run_all.sh`/`Caddyfile` (`demo-app` at root, `intake-app` under `/intake/`), just listening on Render's `$PORT` instead of `:8080`. Build context is the repo root, not just the two app folders, since `intake-app/server.py` reads `../facilitator-intake-system-prompt.md` and `../product-spec.html` at runtime. Whisper's `tiny.en` model is pre-downloaded at Docker build time so a tester's first request isn't the first model load. **Real constraint found and designed around**: `intake-app`'s TTS falls back to macOS's `say` command when `OPENAI_API_KEY` isn't set — `say` doesn't exist on Linux, so on Render that key is effectively mandatory, not optional, or voice replies fail silently. Needs, before first deploy: connect this repo in the Render dashboard as a Blueprint, and set `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`OPENAI_API_KEY`/`ORG_ACCESS_CODE` as real secrets there (not committed — `render.yaml` marks them `sync: false`). Not yet live-deployed or tested against a real Render build.
- **Liability disclaimer footer added to `demo-app` and `intake-app`**, live-verified in both via local browser render: "Not legal or HR advice..." with an urgent/unsafe-situation redirect to real HR/counsel/emergency services. Deliberate scope: agreed as sufficient for the test-send phase (no real HR relying on output), not a substitute for the real counsel review `ROADMAP.md` Phase 3 still requires before an actual pilot.
- **`intake-app` UI redesigned end to end against a new design handoff, including a real DOM/CSS port of Thimble's avatar states**: the user dropped `design_handoff_mend_sessions/` (`.dc.html` mockups + README + brand kit) partway through the session with no initial message, then iterated on it live. Adopted "Thimble" as the facilitator's name in UI copy/labels only (placeholders, the state pill, chat header, banner text) — not in the system prompts, since that's a bigger decision about how the model refers to itself. Replaced the old canvas-drawn avatar with a DOM/CSS port of the handoff's actual per-state geometry: egg-shaped `listening` (leans and wobbles off the employee's own real mic level, with the handoff's double-nod-on-pause behavior), morphing `thinking` (CSS `blob` keyframes + 3 pulsing dots for a mouth), bobbing `talking` (two outward ripples, mouth height driven by real TTS amplitude rather than the handoff's canned loop — kept the real lip-sync since it's strictly more faithful, not a deviation), and nodding `empathizing` (honey circle blending in via `mix-blend-mode:multiply`, echoing the logo's two-circle mark). Built as reusable instances so the same engine drives both the big call-stage avatar and a 48px mini copy in the chat header, always in sync. Reworked the header (real logo + "Mend" wordmark, replacing a plain dev-label), mode cards (exact copy from the handoff, "For managers" now links out to `manager-coaching-app`), and — per a later design-handoff update the user pointed to directly — rebuilt Voice/Text as two genuinely separate views instead of a composer bolted onto the avatar stage: Voice has no text input at all, Text is its own chat panel with bubbles, a typing indicator, and Enter-to-send, and both share one message log. Per explicit user direction, later removed the "Go live" button entirely — Voice view now starts listening automatically on load/entry, Text stops it, and Mute (new control, with a matching icon on the employee's own tile) is the one way to pause listening without leaving Voice. Iterated the stage layout per direct user feedback: state pill top-left, Voice/Text toggle top-right, Mute/Captions/Transcript centered at the bottom, the "You" tile rebuilt as an actual video tile (avatar centered, label overlaying the tile's own bottom-left corner instead of stacked below it), stage background a lighter oat tint. Found and fixed four real bugs along the way, each live-verified in the browser: (1) the avatar got stuck mid-"Thinking" forever on a page reload once `/api/start` correctly no-ops server-side (`conversation` already non-empty) — the frontend never reset `avatarState` when there was no reply to speak; (2) a script-halting TDZ crash — `animateAvatar()`'s first call happens *synchronously* at its call site, and a refactor added an unconditional reference to `mediaRecorder` (declared later in the file) with no short-circuit guard in front of it, unlike the original code; moved the initial call to the end of the script; (3) after refactoring the avatar into reusable instances, the big avatar's ripple elements kept their old unprefixed HTML ids instead of the new instance-lookup pattern, causing a null-reference crash on every frame; (4) the chat placeholder text wrapped to two lines and overflowed the input pill (needed `white-space:nowrap`). **Not verified**: the actual live-listening/VAD loop, since this sandboxed browser blocks mic access — confirmed only that the failure path is graceful (clear error message, no crash), not that real speech still drives it correctly end-to-end. `joint-session-app` got a smaller pass (branded header, "Thimble" renamed in visible copy, honey-tinted private-checkin overlay) but not the same avatar-stage rebuild — that app has no avatar/state infrastructure today and would be new feature work, not restyling.
- **Post-session follow-up now opens in the same browser tab, not a new one**: `joint-session-app/static/index.html`'s end-of-session banner link to `/post/` had `target="_blank"`, opening post-session in a separate tab — flagged by the user as a real tracking problem (a facilitator/operator following up with someone needs one continuous window, not a session that forks into an orphaned tab). Removed `target="_blank"`/`rel="noopener"` so the link navigates the same window; the escalation/category/coaching-role/coaching-pattern context it already passes as query params (see that file's `handleEnded()`) is unaffected, since those travel in the URL regardless of tab vs. same-window navigation. `demo-app`'s hub links and `intake-app`'s own "Continue to joint session" link still use `target="_blank"` deliberately left alone — those are a menu launching a tool and a session-start link, not a follow-up-tracking handoff, so the same concern doesn't obviously apply; worth revisiting if the user wants the same treatment there. Not live-tested through a full two-employee session (would need two live WebSocket participants to reach the real end-of-session banner) — verified by reading the diff and confirming the query-param logic is untouched; low-risk, one-line markup change.
- **`manager-coaching-app/` live-tested end to end with a real API key and real model replies**, closing the gap the build left open: ran the full `run_all.sh` stack, hit `http://localhost:8080/coach/` in a real browser, and drove a real turn ("a direct report has been missing deadlines...") through the actual Claude call — got back an on-tone, in-character coaching reply (pushed for concrete specifics — "missed three of four sprint deadlines" style — before jumping to delivery advice), confirming the system prompt's phase logic actually works live, not just that the request path doesn't crash. Also found and fixed a real operational issue along the way: several stale `uvicorn`/`caddy`/`livekit-server` processes from earlier sessions (some running since the prior Tuesday) were still holding ports 8420-8424 and 8080, so a fresh `run_all.sh` run silently lost the port race — new instances failed to bind or hung, and the old, stale `Caddyfile` config (missing the new `/coach/*` route) kept serving on 8080, making `/coach/` 404 even though the new app was technically running. Killed the stale processes and restarted cleanly; worth remembering that `run_all.sh` doesn't detect or clean up leftover processes from a previous run on its own.
- **New prototype: `manager-coaching-app/`**, built from a real product insight (a hospital safety officer's observation that people promoted into management on the strength of their individual craft often get zero training in the actually-hard part of the job: hard conversations with direct reports). Gives a manager a private space to frame a performance/conduct issue, structure how to raise it (the behavior, why it matters, what needs to change), think through how the direct report is likely to react, and rehearse it via roleplay where the facilitator plays the direct report. New root-level `facilitator-manager-coaching-system-prompt.md` (same structural shape as `facilitator-intake-system-prompt.md`: disclosures, a mandatory floor adapted for the manager's own conduct — discrimination/retaliation intent, threats, illegal activity, immediate safety risk — a Talk mode for framing/structuring, a Practice mode for rehearsal). The app itself is copied and adapted from `intake-app/` rather than designed from scratch: same `server.py` mechanics (rate limiting, TTS staging with macOS `say`/OpenAI fallback, Whisper transcription, optional Tavus video avatar), same `static/index.html` frontend (avatar canvas, live/hands-free mode, captions, transcript panel), same brand tokens — only the system prompt, kickoff copy, `employee`→`manager`/`direct report` labels, and the "ready for joint session" banner (repurposed to a structuring-complete notice, since there's no downstream joint-session app on this path) actually differ. Wired into `run_all.sh` (port 8425) and `Caddyfile` (`/coach/*`), root `README.md` updated to list it as a fifth local prototype. **Verified live**: created its own venv, installed real dependencies (`fastapi`, `anthropic`, `faster-whisper`, etc. — all imported cleanly), ran `uvicorn server:app --port 8425` against the real server, confirmed `GET /` serves the page with the correct title, and exercised `POST /api/start`, `POST /api/message`, `POST /api/video/enabled`, and `POST /api/reset` against the running server — all responded correctly (no syntax/import errors, no crashes), including the system prompt file loading successfully (a malformed fenced prompt block would have thrown at startup, and didn't). **Not verified live**: an actual model reply — no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set in this environment, and none was copied from a sibling app's `.env` (per instruction not to reuse another app's secret). Without a key, `/api/start` and `/api/message` correctly return the designed "(server error: ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is not set...)" fallback rather than crashing, which confirms the request path works end-to-end up to the Anthropic client call, but the facilitator's actual tone/phase-logic quality (the thing `intake-app/README.md` calls out as this class of prototype's real purpose) is unverified — needs a real key in an environment that has one.
- **`product-spec.html`'s Mermaid diagrams brought into the brand rebrand**: the two flowcharts (`#employee-flow`'s intake routing, and the mid-session escalation tiers) were rendering with a generic gray palette left over from before the Evergreen/Oat rebrand — the injected Mermaid runtime's own `CFG.palettes` never got touched by that pass. Replaced with the doc's own `--surface`/`--ink`/`--line`/`--paper` values (light and dark both), so a diagram node now looks like every other card on the page instead of a leftover default. Verified visually via a local HTTP server (not `file://` — script loading behaves differently) in both light and dark mode (`prefers-color-scheme`), both diagrams render correctly.
- **`intake-app`: final voice-turn transcription now reuses the live partial instead of re-transcribing the whole clip, closing the real redundancy behind the user's "a little too long between when I finished speaking and Thimble responded" report**: `onstop` used to always send the entire recorded clip to `/api/voice` fresh, even though the partial-preview pipeline (`/api/voice/partial`) had already transcribed nearly all of that same audio seconds earlier -- pure wasted Whisper work on every turn long enough to have fired a partial. New `getFinalTranscript()` reuses the last partial transcript as the base and only transcribes the small audio tail recorded since then (falls back to the original full-clip path when no partial ever fired, e.g. short utterances under 0.6s -- no regression there). Found and fixed a real correctness bug in this approach before shipping it, not just assumed it'd work: a hard cut at the exact partial boundary corrupted the straddling word -- confirmed live via a real split-and-rejoin test against the running server, "caught" came back as "Cockme" with zero audio context across the cut. Fixed by including 1s of overlap audio before the cut (`TAIL_OVERLAP_SEC`) and a new `mergeTranscripts()` that matches the longest run of shared words between the base's end and the tail's start to de-duplicate the overlap, instead of blindly concatenating. Also caught and stripped a second artifact: Whisper marks a clip that trails off mid-sentence (which every partial clip is, by definition) with a literal "..." that a clean one-shot transcript never had -- now stripped from the base before merging. Verified end-to-end against the real running server (not simulated): split a real 8.68s synthesized clip at 6.0s, ran the actual two-call sequence the browser will make, and the merged result was byte-for-byte identical to a real one-shot transcription of the whole clip. `mergeTranscripts` also unit-checked live in the real browser page (basic overlap, no-overlap fallback, empty-tail cases, all correct). **Not independently verified**: the real end-to-end browser path (actual `MediaRecorder`/`getUserMedia` flow calling `getFinalTranscript` itself) -- this sandboxed browser blocks mic access, so only the function's real network calls and merge logic were exercised directly, not the full recording-to-submit flow with a live mic. Also worth noting: on this already-fast-again hardware (post Google-Drive-CPU fix) the measured win on an 8.68s test clip was modest (1.14s to 0.98s) -- the approach exists to avoid re-transcribing the *whole* clip, so the win grows with turn length and matters most on longer turns or under CPU contention, not on short ones where there's little to save.
- **Resolved the "transcription takes so long" report that came back again this session, closing the loop the earlier timing-instrumentation entry below left open**: ran a real timed `/api/voice` call against a real synthesized 5.36s clip through the live local server. First two calls measured `transcribe_s=6.52` and `transcribe_s=9.23` for that same 5.36s clip -- genuinely slower than real-time, far worse than the ~2.24s/9.5s baseline measured earlier this project. Checked `ps aux` for CPU contention rather than guessing further: Google Drive was at 95.9% CPU at that moment, almost certainly the post-commit git-bundle backup hook syncing, on a machine with only 2 physical cores -- Whisper's 4 threads were starving for real CPU time. Confirmed directly: user quit Google Drive, same clip re-timed at `transcribe_s=1.31`, back to faster-than-real-time. Not a code bug, no fix needed in `transcribe_file` -- worth remembering this machine's git-bundle-to-Drive backup can starve a live voice test for a window after a commit.
- **Prosody pipeline actually produces a transcript from real speech now — three real, stacked bugs found and fixed, live-tested end to end**: earlier work had validated `prosody.py`'s feature extraction against synthesized speech (`validate_prosody_real_speech.py`) and wired the *signal-forwarding* plumbing to `joint-session-app`, but the live `agent.py` → STT → prosody path itself had never once produced a transcript against a live `livekit-server --dev` room — confirmed by testing it directly with two fake-mic LiveKit participants playing real macOS-`say`-synthesized speech (genuine formants, not sine tones) through the actual pipeline, not a script that bypasses it. Found and fixed three bugs stacked on top of each other, each one masking the next until fixed: (1) `STT_INIT_LOCK` serialized not just the two employees' faster-whisper model loads (the real segfault risk it exists for) but the *entire* real-time-paced backlog replay too, so the second participant's pending-audio queue kept growing for the whole of the first's replay — 82s becoming a 230s backlog in one test — now split into `construct_stt()` (locked) and `drain_pending_stt()` (not locked); (2) `stt.py` never called `RealtimeSTT`'s `.listen()` (or `.text()`/`.wait_audio()`), so `start_recording_on_voice_activity` stayed `False` forever and the recorder's worker thread never started a single recording, regardless of any buffering/pacing fix — real audio was reaching the recorder the whole time, confirmed by reading `RealtimeSTT`'s own source, not guessing; even after adding `.listen()`, a second, separate flag (`stop_recording_on_voice_deactivity`) turned out to gate *stopping* a recording and is set only by the `.text()`/`.wait_audio()` path `.listen()` doesn't touch, so recording started but then ran forever — both flags now set explicitly, and re-armed after every finalized segment (nothing in `RealtimeSTT` re-arms them itself outside that blocking-loop pattern); (3) `agent.py`'s `_on_segment`/`_log_prosody` called `asyncio.create_task()` from inside `RealtimeSTT`'s own recording-worker thread (the `on_recording_stop` callback), not the event loop thread — `RuntimeError: no running event loop` on every finalized segment, and because `RealtimeSTT`'s worker loop has no outer `try`/`except` around invoking that callback, the exception silently killed the whole recording-worker thread, so a participant's first segment was also always their last. Fixed with `asyncio.run_coroutine_threadsafe(..., self._loop)`. End-to-end verified live after all three fixes: real synthesized speech through two fake-mic LiveKit participants produced real Whisper transcripts (`'Major to report.'`, `'I think the project is on track for now.'`, etc.) continuously, no crashes, across multiple segments per participant. Known gap, not yet closed: never observed a non-empty prosody flag fire in this live path (only empty `flags=[]` so far) — `base.en` Whisper measured well behind real-time on this machine, so building the ≥1.5-std speaker baseline needed for a flag to fire takes several more minutes of real playback than this session had patience for; the flag *logic* itself is already separately validated (see the synthesized-speech and unit-test entries below), so this is a live-path timing gap, not a known-broken code path.
- **Prosody wired into the facilitator LLM for real, not just the proof-of-concept**: `joint-session-audio/agent.py` now POSTs non-empty prosody flags to a new `joint-session-app/server.py` endpoint (`POST /api/room/{code}/prosody-signal`), stored per-speaker and consumed into the exact annotation shape the proof-of-concept validated the next time that speaker sends a message. Verified live against real running server code on both ends (not a script): drove two real WebSocket participants through the POC's textually-neutral scenario twice, once with the real signal sent via the real endpoint, once without. Without: facilitator raised the tension pattern in front of both employees. With: facilitator pulled the flagged employee aside for a private check-in — same finding as the POC, now confirmed on fresh API calls through production code. Real, honest scope boundary: this moves tone *signal* between two still-independently-run prototypes (an operator starts both under the same code by hand) — `joint-session-app` is still text-only, nothing here merges the two into one voice-driven session. That merge remains separate, larger future work.
- **Prosody proof-of-concept: does tone-annotating the facilitator prompt actually help?** Before building the real integration between `joint-session-audio` (has the prosody flags) and `joint-session-app` (has the facilitator LLM, no audio today) — a project on the same scale as the breakout room's facilitator-voice gap — tested whether the idea has real value first. `joint-session-app/poc_prosody_in_prompt.py` runs the same conversation twice against the real system prompt and real model, differing in exactly one line's tone annotation. Clearest result: a textually-neutral line ("Sure, I can take care of that too.") got an ordinary, untroubled response with no annotation, but triggered a private rising-tension check-in when annotated `pitch_flattened`/`pace_slowed` — tone alone gave the facilitator something text gave it nothing on, matching the system prompt's own "agreeing under strain" guidance.
