Real signal, not proof at scale (two scenarios, one run each, real LLM sampling variance observed) — enough to justify the real integration, not a substitute for validating it once built. Full writeup in `joint-session-app/README.md`'s new "Prosody proof-of-concept" section.
- **Prosody validated against real speech, one real bug found and fixed**: `joint-session-audio/tests/validate_prosody_real_speech.py` (new, manual, needs macOS `say` + ffmpeg) synthesizes real speech — genuine formants/prosody, not sine tones — and runs it through the exact live-pipeline code. Confirmed correct on real speech: stable pitch tracking across utterances from the same voice, and `energy_elevated`/`pace_elevated`/`pitch_elevated` all fire correctly on genuinely loud/fast/pitch-shifted segments. Caught a real bug doing this, not just a data point: `pitch_flattened` was comparing a segment's *average* pitch to baseline, conflating "lower pitch" with "flatter/less-varying pitch" — two different things. Fixed: `pitch_lowered` and `pitch_flattened` are now separate checks (the latter compares a segment's own within-utterance pitch variability to the speaker's usual variability), with two new unit tests built to isolate one from the other. Honest remaining limit: couldn't confirm whether `pitch_flattened` fires the way a human would expect on genuine deadpan/sarcastic delivery — macOS TTS has no real "flat affect" mode, so this still needs real recorded human speech, which this sandboxed environment can't provide.
- **Prosody/tone signal in `joint-session-audio`**, deliberately narrow scope: full audio-based sarcasm/intent detection was explicitly considered and rejected as the first move — a hard, unsolved research problem, and this product leans precision-first everywhere else it makes an automated call. Built the narrower version instead: `prosody.py` extracts pitch/energy/speaking-rate/pause-ratio per STT segment and compares each one against *that same speaker's own* running baseline (Welford's streaming mean/variance, not a fixed population threshold — absolute pitch mostly just proxies for who's talking). Logs legible flags (`pitch_elevated`, `energy_elevated`, `pace_elevated`, `pace_slowed`, `pitch_flattened`, `hesitant`) when a segment is ≥1.5 std from that speaker's own baseline, never a single opaque score, and nothing at all until there's enough of that speaker's history to compare against. Wired into `agent.py`'s existing STT pipeline as pure observability — deliberately not touching `delay_buffer.py`/the classifier's cut decision. 10 unit tests against synthetic tones (exact frequency/amplitude control), plus the real `ParticipantBridge._log_prosody` method verified end-to-end against a lightweight fake object. Known, stated gap: unvalidated against real recorded speech (no mic in this sandboxed dev environment), same constraint as the video opt-in gate.
- **Live-tested `intake-app`/`post-session-app`'s rate limiting + proxy-path fixes** (committed 2026-09-29, unverified until now): rate limiting confirmed on both apps against the real running servers — 20 requests succeed, the 21st within the 60s window returns `429 {"error": "too many requests..."}`, non-`/api/` paths (e.g. `GET /`) stay unaffected, and the window correctly clears afterward. Proxy-path fix confirmed through the actual `caddy run` proxy at `localhost:8080` (not just the apps' own direct ports): `/intake/` and `/post/` both load, fire `/api/start`/`/api/message`/`/api/speak` at the correct proxied paths (not the proxy's root), the returned audio URL resolves and plays through the proxy too, and the banner's `/joint/` link is relative (not the old hardcoded `http://localhost:8421/`). No bugs found in either fix.
- **Classifier per-class loss weighting, tried and honestly a mixed result, not a clean fix**: added `compute_pos_weight`/`WeightedTrainer` to `classifier/train.py` (per-class `pos_weight` for `BCEWithLogitsLoss`, computed from the train split only, `--no_pos_weight` to disable for comparison) to test the exact hypothesis `classifier/README.md`'s Results section raised — that `v4`'s cut_trigger regression came from training all 5 labels with equal loss weight regardless of how much each one's share of positives shifted between `v3` and `v4`. Retrained `v4` with it on: `immediate_safety_risk` recall did recover (0.33 → 1.0, the clearest casualty of the original regression), but cut_trigger F1 got *worse*, not better (0.81 → 0.76) — pos_weight fixed recall, which wasn't v4's actual problem; precision was, and pushing every under-performing class's recall up bought more false positives than the recall gain was worth, since cut_trigger is an OR across all four floor categories. Real, working mechanism, wrong lever for this specific problem — see the README's Results section for the full breakdown and what's worth trying next (a gentler weighting curve, or weighting only the class that was actually starved instead of all of them).
- **Live mode: two matching tiles (facilitator | you) in `intake-app`**: the facilitator's own spoken reply used to show up twice during a call — once as the caption/subtitle under its avatar, again as a full logged message in the side transcript panel. Live mode ("Go live") now swaps the single avatar + transcript log for two tiles, each with its own circle and a single current-sentence subtitle, mirroring the same one-place-per-speaker principle already applied to the employee's own live transcription. The employee's tile reuses that partial-transcription pipeline (mirrored into a new subtitle element); the facilitator's tile reuses the existing caption/chunking logic unchanged. Composer, mic-indicator dot, and transcript panel all hide while live, restored when live mode ends (including the "lost microphone access" exit paths, not just the manual button). Real bug found and fixed along the way: unsafe-centered flex content that overflows a box's *start* edge is invisible and unscrollable in every browser, which silently vanished the new facilitator label on a short/narrow viewport — fixed with `justify-content:safe center` on both tiles. Non-live Talk/Practice mode is unchanged. Verified both via direct layout-toggle calls and the real click → live-mode → real-speech-via-faked-mic path, at a realistic 1280x900 viewport.
- **Live partial transcription in `intake-app`, single-location display**: a first attempt at this showed live text in the mic-indicator label, then a separate final message once the turn ended — reported as confusing, the same content in two places. Rebuilt so a single transcript-panel bubble is created the moment recording starts (shown as "…"), updated in place as `POST /api/voice/partial` returns live partial text during speech, and corrected in place with the authoritative final transcript once `/api/voice` resolves — never duplicated. Still the torch-free approach (reuses the existing tiny.en/`beam_size=1` Whisper setup) over RealtimeSTT, whose torch/torchaudio dependency measured a real cold-start regression on this machine (~4 min) versus intake-app's current fast start. Verified live end-to-end in the browser via a faked `getUserMedia` (real speech audio piped through a decoded buffer, since the sandbox has no real mic).
- **Voice-memo latency in `intake-app`**, real fix not just relabeling: a voice memo turn measured ~30s end-to-end before this, and the UI blamed the whole wait on "transcribing..." even though transcription was a minority of it. Root-caused by direct profiling (not guessing): `/api/voice`/`/api/message`/`/api/start` were all synthesizing TTS audio inline before returning anything — TTS alone measured ~12-14s for a typical reply, the single biggest piece. `facilitator_reply()` now defaults to `synthesize=False`, returning text fast; a new `POST /api/speak` fetches audio in a second round trip the frontend doesn't block on, so the input re-enables as soon as text arrives instead of waiting on voice too. Also switched the OpenAI TTS model to `tts-1` (~2x faster than `gpt-4o-mini-tts`, less expressive voice, a real tradeoff). Separately, this dev machine turned out to be an old Intel MacBook Air (dual-core, no GPU/Neural Engine) — local Whisper transcription itself was measured at 5.16s for a ~9.5s clip, cut to 2.24s (tiny.en + explicit `cpu_threads`, both now the default) with no privacy tradeoff. A cloud STT option (OpenAI's `gpt-4o-mini-transcribe`, measured 1.81s) was deliberately not adopted — would send the employee's raw voice memo off the machine, a real step down for a product whose whole pitch is "nothing recorded," kept local-only on purpose even in this dev prototype. Combined result: `/api/voice` measured ~29.8s → ~6.6-7.1s in repeated live tests against the running server. Same fix now also applied to `post-session-app` (identical REST shape; was also missing the eager-startup-load fix entirely, and defaulted to `small.en` with no `cpu_threads` set, even slower than `intake-app`'s old default) and `joint-session-app` (WebSocket shape — `facilitator_reply()` never synthesizes inline now, a new `send_audio_followup()` fires via `asyncio.create_task` and pushes a separate `"audio"` event to the same recipient(s) the text `"message"` event went to, verified by reading the private-checkin routing directly and by a live two-participant WebSocket test: text arrived at +4.73s, audio followed separately at +8.11s, both participants getting identical content at each step). `joint-session-app` has no voice/Whisper input at all (text-only; real audio lives in `joint-session-audio/`), so only the TTS-staging half applied there.
- **Voice UI polish in `intake-app`** (mic feedback, VAD calibration, subtitle chunking, further transcription speedup): added a live "Listening" indicator (dot + label, lit by the same RMS level the VAD already computes) so it's visible whether the mic is actually picking up speech — previously "Go live" hands-free mode gave no signal at all. Fixed two real VAD bugs found by using it live: (1) noise-floor calibration tracked the *maximum* level in its first 400ms, so speaking immediately (the normal case in hands-free mode) poisoned the floor near the user's own speaking volume, cutting them off on ordinary between-word pauses — switched to tracking the minimum instead. (2) that wasn't enough alone: the calibration window also fully suppressed real speech detection for its whole 400ms, so the very start of anything said immediately was never marked as speech at all — redesigned so detection runs every frame from turn start regardless of calibration state, with calibration only refining the threshold in the background from genuinely-quiet samples. Known, accepted trade-off: a room with continuous ambient noise that never dips below the fixed floor can no longer calibrate upward around it, reintroducing a milder form of the original bug in that one case. Also fixed the "caption" subtitle overlay in the avatar/Talk view: chunking previously merged consecutive short sentences into one block up to a 90-char budget, so a reply's first few short sentences could all land on screen at t=0, before most of them were actually spoken — now one chunk is at most one sentence (a long sentence still splits further on clause breaks). Finally, cut further into transcription latency: `faster-whisper`'s own `beam_size=5` default measured ~30% slower on `tiny.en` than `beam_size=1` (greedy decoding) in repeated live tests, same transcript both ways — now the default in both `intake-app` and `post-session-app`.
- **"Caucus" → "side conversation" rename**, repo-wide (33 files): system prompts, `product-spec.html`, `joint-session-architecture.md`, both apps' `server.py`, the pitch lexicon doc, and the pitch video's own timeline (`Mend Pitch/mend-opening.jsx`'s `C.Caucus` → `C.SideConvo`, `Mend Session Opening.dc.html`'s scene name). Deliberately left the deprecated `Mend Pitch standalone/` bundle untouched (legacy, not built on).
- **Peer-suggested invites**, built and live-tested: `demo-app` gained `PAIR_INVITES` + four endpoints (create/get/accept/decline, mirroring the existing crypto-shredded `INVITES` pattern) and hub UI. Deliberately non-anonymous — the spec's original anonymous-suggester design turned out to be unbuildable (delivery is always manual, so the recipient always knows who sent it), so this reuses the same two-link, sender-identified mechanism a manager-initiated session would need anyway. `product-spec.html`'s "Peer-suggested invites" section and its Section 10 open-risk callout rewritten to match.
- **LiveKit private breakout room (side-conversation isolation)**, built and live-verified against a real `livekit-server --dev`: new `joint-session-audio/breakout_gate.py` (pure state gate, modeled on `video_parity.py`, 6 unit tests) wired into `agent.py` — pauses both employees' cross-publish, signals the non-target, opens a private subscription-restricted track for the target. LiveKit's `move_participant`/`forward_participant` confirmed Cloud-only, so this uses the same self-hosted-compatible primitives as the existing video-parity code. Manual trigger: `POST /api/room/{code}/checkin|resume` on `joint-session-audio/server.py`, via the room's own data channel (the same seam a future facilitator-LLM integration would use). **Scope boundary, deliberate**: transport only — no facilitator voice, no live connection to `joint-session-app`'s text-only facilitator LLM (a separate, larger integration).
- **Real pre-existing bug found and fixed** while live-testing the above: `ParticipantBridge.publish()` was `await`-ing `set_track_subscription_permissions`, which is actually synchronous in the installed SDK (`-> None`). The resulting `TypeError` was silently swallowed (fired inside a fire-and-forget `asyncio.create_task`), meaning the core audio bridge had likely never successfully completed against a live server before now. See `joint-session-audio/README.md`'s "Known gaps".
- Brand rebrand: new logo/color kit applied across all 5 apps, `product-spec.html`, `launchpad.html`, and `Mend Pitch/`'s full design system + both animated videos (chrome only, character illustrations left untouched on purpose). Real contrast bugs found and fixed (white text was illegible on Pink/Honey fills in several places).
- "Below-floor coaching" principle: codified in `product-spec.html` (harmful-but-not-illegal patterns like gaslighting get private coaching, never reach a manager/HR) and wired into `facilitator-joint-session-system-prompt.md` + `facilitator-post-session-system-prompt.md` + both apps' `server.py`. Live-verified against a running server, including a real bug fix (the facilitator wasn't flagging a pattern it had already handled live — fixed via an explicit prompt instruction).
- `joint-session-audio/`: real audio delay buffer (LiveKit + streaming STT + the real hard-cut classifier) and symmetric video opt-in, both live-tested. Found and fixed a real LiveKit Agents subprocess-timeout bug along the way.
- classifier `batch6`: 100 hand-labeled manager-statement examples, checked against the live v3 model (surfaced that v3 currently has 0% recall on live coercive-retaliation phrasing).
- `demo-app`'s real signup/invite backend + `run_all.sh`/`Caddyfile` unified local proxy, both verified.
- Legal counsel outreach template saved to `docs/pitch-strategy/10_LEGAL_COUNSEL_OUTREACH.md` — drafted, not sent.

- **Thimble prompt behavior pass, consolidating several AI-chat-session ideas directly into the live prompt files rather than a separate notes doc.** `facilitator-case-plan-system-prompt.md` (manager-app's live prompt, `THIMBLE_MINDSET.md` updated to match per that file's own "live prompt wins" rule): Beat 2's structural-alignment diagnosis expanded from a single competing-metrics hypothesis into six named drivers (metrics, bandwidth/overload, ownership gap, status/visibility, style clash, missing context) so Thimble stops flattening every case into a KPI collision; added an explicit internal-jargon-to-plain-language translation table (never say "bandwidth panic" or "swimlane" to the manager, even while using those words internally); added a rule against the "I want to be upfront with you, I'm on your side" disclosure-preamble tic (reads as pre-bad-news hedging, not reassurance; alignment should be shown through behavior, not announced); added deflective-reframing guidance for colloquial name-calling ("they're an idiot") that redirects to the specific behavior underneath without HR-style scolding; tightened beat 1 to one clarifying question per turn so a skeptical manager sees value faster. Same disclosure-tic and name-calling fixes mirrored into `facilitator-intake-system-prompt.md`'s shared tone section and `facilitator-persona.md`'s core traits, since both apply beyond manager-app. None of this is live-tested against a real model reply yet.
- **New `docs/pitch-strategy/17_PROMPT_TRANSPARENCY_AND_ATTESTATION.md`**: rationale and shape for publishing Thimble's real system prompt as a trust-building move (the existing `facilitator-*-system-prompt.md` files are already clean enough to publish as-is), plus a SHA-256 manifest + runtime attestation endpoint as the actual mechanism for proving a deployed instance matches what's published — not yet built, flagged as premature before a real server deployment exists to attest. Open question logged: which repo gets published, given `origin`'s status.

- **Thimble speed-to-value pass, consolidating a root-level strategy doc the user dropped in (`mend_product_strategy_conversational_flow.md`, now absorbed and deleted) directly into the live employee-conflict intake prompt.** `facilitator-intake-system-prompt.md`'s Phase 1 now has a "showing understanding early, not just asking" section: once there's enough to work with, Thimble reflects back what's actually at stake for the employee before asking for more, rather than extracting every detail first — the dropped doc's concern was that a user unsure of their ROI on time invested will bail if intake feels like a form. Carried over three of the dropped doc's archetype scripts (avoidant/evasive peer, a manager reversing themselves, cross-team defensiveness) but rewrote all of them into plain language, since the original phrasing ("double bind," "safe harbor," "status defense") was the same register of jargon the previous session's jargon ban was written to exclude — same tension as `manager-app`'s competing-metrics-only default from last time, not repeated here. Also made explicit: at most one or two questions per turn, never a numbered list. The communication-coaching deliverable (help them raise it themselves) is now structured in three parts (what's driving the other person, the actual wording, how to leave a clean trail) without introducing a new named concept Thimble says aloud. `facilitator-persona.md` carries the shared rationale. None of this is live-tested against a real model reply yet.

- **Live-tested the speed-to-value pass against a running `intake-app` server (not just reasoned about from the diff) and found 3 real bugs, all fixed and re-verified live.** (1) The vent-check ("is there more?") kept re-asking a reworded version of itself even after a clear "no" answer, because the prompt only described what to do if the employee kept venting, never what to do with a clean negative — fixed in `facilitator-intake-system-prompt.md`'s Phase 1 by making a "no" explicitly final, including when it answers a more specific version of the question. (2) The relationship question ("is this a peer, your manager...") got re-asked even after the employee had already stated it plainly two turns earlier — the old guard ("if it genuinely hasn't surfaced") was too soft; now explicit about checking the full conversation history, not just the latest message. (3) The disclosure-preamble tic ("I want to be upfront with you, I'm on your side") kept resurfacing in new wordings every time a specific phrase got banned ("for the record...", "to be straight with you...") because the prompt structurally required the model to assert the advocate/coaching stance as a standalone declaration, and the model needs *some* lead-in to introduce a flat loyalty claim. Fixed properly, not just re-banned: removed the standalone-declaration requirement from `facilitator-intake-system-prompt.md` entirely, and `intake-app/server.py` now splices in a fixed, pre-written stance sentence (`TRACK_STANCE_SENTENCES`/`maybe_stance_sentence`) the one time per session a track first becomes known, via both `/api/message` and the live `/api/message/stream` path, reset alongside the conversation on `/api/reset`. Mirrored the generalized "any lead-in is the tell, not just specific phrases" framing into `facilitator-case-plan-system-prompt.md` and `facilitator-persona.md` too. A real trade-off from hardcoding in the streaming endpoint: the sentence lands as a separate trailing paragraph rather than blended into the reply, since the track isn't known until the model finishes streaming and true prepending would mean buffering the whole reply first. Re-verified fixed with a fresh conversation after each change, same scenario (peer who agrees then goes quiet on a spec) run three times across the fix cycle. Not yet tested against the manager-coaching track, other archetypes, or a long multi-turn session.

- **Built `intake-app/tests/conversation_pattern_sweep.py`** (same spirit as `safety_floor_sweep.py`, but for the employee-conflict path's conversational shape rather than the safety floor): runs scripted multi-turn conversations across named archetypes against the live `/api/message` endpoint, saves every transcript to a timestamped `.jsonl` for manual read-through, and auto-flags known tics (disclosure preambles, "on your side," corporate jargon, pop-psych labels, numbered question lists, a same-question-twice heuristic). Paced with a `time.sleep()` per turn to stay under `server.py`'s own 20-req/60s cost-protection rate limiter, which a bare back-to-back sweep blows through in well under a minute.
- **Research-grounded fix: Phase 1 now distinguishes genuinely new venting from restated venting, and proactively offers the structural-read pivot after two rounds of the latter.** Prompted by a direct question about whether unstructured venting actually helps or just keeps someone's nervous system riled up -- the relevant research split cleanly: the folk "pressure valve" model (Bushman's venting-anger studies) doesn't hold up, but structured disclosure with eventual reframing (Pennebaker's expressive-writing research) does, and purposeless repeated dwelling with zero reframing (Rose's co-rumination research) correlates with worse outcomes over time. `facilitator-intake-system-prompt.md`'s Phase 1 now treats a same-complaint restatement (not a new incident/detail) as the signal to stop waiting for venting to "run its course" and proactively offer the structural pivot as a genuine, declinable offer -- "insight only, just needed to vent" stays a fully legitimate outcome even after the offer is declined. Live-tested across 5 repeats of a scripted repetitive-venting scenario: landed cleanly (the exact designed offer, verbatim in spirit) in 2/5 runs; the other 3 kept asking for more specifics on the same incident instead, which is a legitimate-ish read of "still gathering" but means the fix is inconsistent, not yet reliable -- left as-is rather than over-tuned on a 5-run sample.
- **Fixed a more consequential bug the sweep surfaced independently in 3 of the first 10 test conversations: Thimble ignoring an explicit "I'm ready to move on" and asking for more information instead of delivering.** Showed up in `avoidant_peer` ("I'd want some wording..." met with another structural question), `cross_team_pushback` ("I'd like to know how to ask..." met with a repeated relationship question), and `manager_coaching_track` ("I just want help opening the conversation" met with "I need a little more first"). Added an explicit rule to the shared tone section: once someone states what they want next, work with what's already known instead of gating the deliverable on more detail, and even with a genuine information gap, give a best-effort general-purpose answer rather than fully withholding help. First pass fixed 2 of 3 scenarios; the third (`cross_team_pushback`) still blocked because its script never actually supplies the one fact Thimble asked for twice (a real, not invented, gap) -- sharpened once more (bias toward delivering a usable answer even with a real gap, ask again only alongside delivering, never instead of) and reverified clean across 3 fresh runs of that scenario.
- **All of this round's fixes went through a real test-fix-retest loop against the live server, not just reasoned about from the diff** -- roughly 17 scripted API calls' worth of conversations across the session (cost: a few dollars of real Anthropic API usage, estimated beforehand and confirmed small). Every fix was re-run against the specific scenario that exposed it before moving on.

- **User-driven review round: sent the 10 most recent sweep transcripts as a saved `.md` file, user analyzed them directly and found 2 more real bugs plus correctly flagged one false positive.** (1) The employee-conflict path's structural-read mechanism only ever taught one example hypothesis ("what's the thing their manager's on their case about if it slips"), so it kept reaching for boss-pressure regardless of scenario -- the exact one-hammer problem already fixed in `facilitator-case-plan-system-prompt.md`'s Beat 2 earlier today, just never ported to this path. Ported the same six-driver structure (pressure from above, overload, unclear ownership, status/visibility, working-style mismatch, missing context) into `facilitator-intake-system-prompt.md`'s structural-read paragraph. (2) The relationship-tracking rule from the earlier fix missed a case where "my manager" was stated as an ordinary noun in the opening message's second word, rather than a dedicated clarifying clause ("he's a peer, not my manager") -- tightened to say the surface form never determines whether something counts as surfaced, only the content does. Also added a smaller fix for a related pattern found in review: an unanswered earlier question getting tacked onto the end of a reply as a disconnected afterthought after the real deliverable was already given -- now only re-asked if the answer would actually change the advice, woven in naturally, not appended out of habit. The user also correctly pushed back on a fourth flagged "flaw" (manager-coaching Turn 3 "jumping to a script without asking what changed") that was actually the earlier deliverable-blocking fix working as intended -- Thimble had asked, the scripted manager declined to answer and asked for the opening line instead, and Thimble correctly complied rather than re-blocking.
- **Reverified all three with fresh live runs.** Relationship-tracking: 3/3 clean on `repetitive_venting`, plus `avoidant_peer`'s "he's a peer" also correctly tracked. Driver diversity: confirmed real, not cosmetic -- `avoidant_peer` now correctly lands on overload ("what else is on his plate, something unrelated to you") instead of boss-pressure; `repetitive_venting` still landing on boss-pressure across its own reruns looks like a legitimate best fit for that specific scenario shape (a proposal dismissed in ten seconds), not a relapse. Tacked-on-echo: improved (framed as optional sharpening now, not a demand) but not fully eliminated, 1 of 2 `cross_team_pushback` reruns still appended a leftover question. Separately noticed, unrelated to this round: the `<mend_signal>` block went missing for an entire run's 3 turns a second time today (first seen with `manager_coaching_track`, now once more) -- low-frequency (twice in ~30 sessions), not chased further, worth keeping an eye on if it recurs.

## Open / next up

- **`manager-app`: the "managing up" translator and the decoupled peer-to-peer hand-off**, both designed in `manager-app/WORKFLOW.md` but not built. The translator (turning a raw vent like "Carol's team is ignoring us" into a scripted upward update) is partly covered by the system prompt's beat-3 instructions but has no dedicated UI affordance to request it on demand. The hand-off needs an actual endpoint that spins up a session in a peer-to-peer flow (likely reusing `joint-session-app`'s architecture) and a UI element for the manager to trigger it; right now the prompt only tells Thimble to *verbally offer* it.
- **`manager-app`: no automated tests yet** -- everything so far has been verified by hand (curl + Browser-pane live runs), same bar as this repo's other small prototypes, but there's no `tests/` directory here the way `intake-app` has. Worth at least a safety-floor sweep (reusing the pattern from `facilitator-intake-system-prompt.md`'s boundary-sweep test) given this prompt has its own copy of the mandatory-floor language.
- **Google Sheet setup for the survey still needs to be done by hand — the code is ready, waiting on this.** Until these env vars are set, every submission still works, it just falls back to the local `survey_responses.jsonl`/stdout log instead of landing in a Sheet (see the "survey deployed... real research instrument" entry above for why that fallback exists). Needs doing once, by the user, not something that can be scripted from here (it's your Google account):
  1. In [Google Cloud Console](https://console.cloud.google.com), create or reuse a project, then enable the **Google Sheets API** for it.
  2. Create a **Service Account** (IAM & Admin → Service Accounts), generate a JSON key, download it.
  3. Create a Google Sheet with this exact header row: `timestamp, overall_rating, felt_fair, felt_safe, trusted_privacy, sided, understood_situation, changed_approach, readiness, comparison, would_reuse, nps, emotional_shift, standout_moment, trust_improvement, issues, comments`.
  4. Share that Sheet with the service account's own email (the `client_email` field in the downloaded key, looks like `...@...iam.gserviceaccount.com`) as an **Editor**.
  5. Set two env vars — `GOOGLE_SHEET_ID` (the id segment of the Sheet's URL, between `/d/` and `/edit`) and `GOOGLE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON` (the entire downloaded key file's contents, as one line) — in Render's Environment tab for the live site, and in `intake-app/.env` for local testing. Never commit the key file or paste its contents anywhere other than these env vars.
  6. Once set, submit a real test response and check the Sheet for a new row — `get_survey_sheet()` logs why, via `print`, if auth or the Sheet lookup fails instead of silently falling back.
- **Send the live URL to real testers** — https://mend-mvp.onrender.com is up, Deepgram's live there too now, and a full mobile pass has been live-tested and fixed (see Recently completed); next step is actually sending it out, not further engineering.
- **Render's free/starter tier may sleep on idle** — worth confirming which plan is active and whether a tester's first hit after idle time eats a slow cold start; not yet checked.
- **This dev machine's CPU contention is a recurring confound when judging `intake-app` voice latency live** -- Google Drive's post-commit backup, this Claude Code session's own browser-pane testing, and ordinary background load (Chrome, WindowServer) have each separately caused a slow transcription call during testing this session, independent of any code issue. Worth remembering before diagnosing a future "it's slow" report as a code regression: check `ps aux` for contention first. Less relevant now that Deepgram (no local Whisper call) is the live path, but still applies to anyone testing without a Deepgram key.
- **`joint-session-app`: same avatar-stage treatment as `intake-app`** — a big centered animated Thimble avatar with live captions, matching the new design handoff, instead of the current flat 3-tile strip. Bigger lift than a restyle: needs the avatar/state infrastructure built from scratch for that app.
- **"Thimble" as the facilitator's actual self-reference** — currently only adopted in UI copy/labels; the system prompts still don't have the model call itself Thimble in its own replies. A real product/copy decision, not done implicitly.
- **`manager-coaching-app`: real conversation-quality test with a live API key** — the server, endpoints, and system-prompt loading are all verified live; the actual facilitator persona (does the coaching/structuring/roleplay flow feel right, does the mandatory-floor screening actually fire on the right inputs) has never been tried against a real model reply in this environment. Needs an `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, same as every other app here.
- **Facilitator voice for the breakout room** — the private check-in track is inert (no TTS publishes into it), and nothing decides *when* a check-in should happen live; that logic exists only in `joint-session-app`'s text-only facilitator LLM today, with zero live connection to `joint-session-audio`. Merging the two is a separate, larger integration.
- **Classifier: a gentler per-class weighting curve, or weighting only `immediate_safety_risk`** — the blanket neg/pos-ratio weighting tried above overcorrected toward recall; a softer curve (e.g. `sqrt(neg/pos)`) or weighting just the one class that was actually starved, leaving the already-strong classes at neutral weight, are the untried next steps. Fully self-testable, no human review needed.
- **Human review of classifier batches 4/5/6** — 378 examples queued in `classifier/data/review_page.html`, not yet judged by a person.
- **GitHub Pages hosting for `trymend.solutions`** — designed, blocked entirely on the account suspension above.
- **Real camera hardware test for video opt-in** — the parity *logic* is proven (`joint-session-audio/tests/test_video_parity.py`), but the actual LiveKit video-subscription-revocation path has never been exercised with a live camera (this dev environment has none).
- **Prosody: merge into one real voice-driven session** — the flags are now live-wired into the facilitator LLM (see above), but `joint-session-app` is still text-only and the two apps still need a human to start them under the same code by hand. A real merge (STT transcript replacing typed messages, facilitator TTS carrying replies back to both employees) is separate, larger future work, same scope as the breakout room's facilitator-voice gap. Two smaller gaps remain too: `pitch_flattened` still needs a genuine human deadpan/sarcastic reading to validate against (TTS can't fake real flat affect, and this dev environment has no microphone), and the two live-tested scenarios (POC + real integration) should get more runs before leaning on the exact behavior hard, given observed LLM sampling variance.
